# Decisions

Short ADR-style entries: decision, alternatives, why. Newest last.


> **D-rename (18 Oct 2026):** product renamed Origin Proof → Unde Labs (name collision with other submissions). User-visible text, code identifiers and env vars (with fallbacks) renamed. Kept: hash salts (stored IDs), GCP resource names (secret, bucket, services), research-folder paths, eval artefacts, module path. Cloud resource renames are follow-up.
> - Renamed: the frontend copy and wordmark ("Unde *Labs*"), the share image, the broker draft ("generated by Unde"; golden files differ only in the name), the case-agent prompt's self-reference (case-agent-v2, not re-evaluated: name only), the CROSS/HTS User-Agent (`cross.UserAgent`, "unde-labs (...)"), the ADK app names (`unde-case-agent`, `unde-verifier`; in-memory sessions), `UNDE_RESEARCH_RESULTS` (reads `ORIGINPROOF_RESEARCH_RESULTS` as a fallback), `$UNDE_API` in the Developers example, the OpenAPI title, the README and current docs.
> - Kept, with a "legacy name kept" comment where not obvious:
>   - the hash salts `origin-proof-visitor:`, `origin-proof-apikey:` and `origin-proof-user:` (every stored owner ID, API-key hash and user lookup depends on them)
>   - Secret Manager `origin-proof-admin-token`
>   - bucket `wassup-hackathon-2026-origin-proof-corpus`
>   - Cloud Run `origin-proof-api-staging`
>   - research-folder paths `hackathon-research/experiments/origin-proof/...`
>   - `eval/` artefacts (including a recorded case-agent answer that says "Origin Proof")
>   - the Go module path, routes and `/v1` paths
>   - `docs/issues/*`, `PR-*.md`, `plans/`, design keyframes and earlier decision entries
> - Follow-up, needs a cloud migration: rename the secret, the bucket and the staging service.
> - Naming audit (19 Nov 2026, #272): the explicit mapping (Unde Labs / Unde ← Origin Proof; repo and GCP project `wassup-hackathon-2026`) is now in the README and the GitHub About description leads with Unde Labs. Env-var fallback inventory: only `ORIGINPROOF_RESEARCH_RESULTS` remains — a deprecated alias of `UNDE_RESEARCH_RESULTS` in `backend/internal/retrieval/parity_test.go` — and it is kept until existing local setups no longer use it. No technical identifier was renamed.
>
> **Historical chat/auto-deploy artifacts (13–16 Oct 2026, #279):** issues #2, #14 and #17 and commits `eab0964`, `c55d06b`, `b6424c6`, `10cceac` and `f907641` describe the earlier auto-deploy chat prototype. Its Go/cloud tooling was removed in `d9d339a`, its demo app in `364b755`, its frontend `/chat` UI in `3418ed7`, and its chat deploy smoke test in `2ed7423`. An inventory on 19 Nov 2026 found no runtime `in-memory sessions`, `/api/chat/stream` or `get_time` tool left: current `session.InMemoryService()` calls are per-request ADK runners, and `chat` fields belong to the live case-agent feature (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/ask`, `ChatTurn`, Ask drawer). Retained audit text, `docs/issues/*`, PR notes, design keyframes and earlier decision entries are historical records, per the D-rename note above. `/health`, CI and deploy checks are shared infrastructure, not chat leftovers. The stale `/chat` wording in the frontend README, the backend helper comments/log and the UI skill brief was corrected; the unused `remark-gfm` dependency was removed. No deployment or workflow behaviour changed.

## D1 — Build inside the existing team repo's `backend/` and `frontend/` (15 Oct)
- **Decision:** Origin Proof's Go API lives in `backend/` (Cloud Run service `backend`), the Next.js app in `frontend/` (service `frontend`). The template deploy-agent code there was removed.
- **Alternatives:** new `api/` and `web/` folders as in the build prompt; a fresh `origin-proof` repo as in BUILD-PLAN.md.
- **Why:** CI, Workload Identity Federation deploys, service accounts and IAM (frontend may invoke backend; backend has Vertex AI User) already exist for these two services. New services need IAM changes the CI deployer can't make. The human owner confirmed the template code may be overwritten. The repo's first commit (13 Oct) is after the hackathon start (12 Oct).

## D2 — Health check at `/health`, not `/healthz`
- **Why:** Cloud Run reserves paths ending in `z` and returns 404 for them.

## D3 — Two LLM providers behind `llm.Model`; LiteLLM is development-only (superseded by D49)
- **Superseded (19 Nov 2026, #280):** D49 removed LiteLLM; only `vertex` and `fake` are accepted today. This entry is kept as history. `fake` remains an explicit opt-in for offline UI work and is refused on Cloud Run.
- **Decision (historical):** `LLM_PROVIDER=litellm|vertex|fake`. LiteLLM (OpenAI-compatible proxy) is used now because the team has no Vertex access yet. `config` refuses `litellm` and `fake` when `K_SERVICE` is set, and the deploy workflow sets `LLM_PROVIDER=vertex`.
- **Why (historical):** keeps the research proxy usable for development while guaranteeing the deployed service and official numbers come from Vertex. Every output records `llm_provider` and `model_id`.

## D4 — Real API names differ from the build prompt
- ADK for Go is imported as `google.golang.org/adk` (v1.8.0), not `github.com/google/adk-go`.
- Gen AI SDK: `google.golang.org/genai` v1.74.0 with `Backend: genai.BackendVertexAI`; JSON answers use `ResponseMIMEType: application/json` + `ResponseJsonSchema`; tools use `FunctionDeclaration.ParametersJsonSchema`.
- Gemini 3+ returns thought signatures on function calls that must be sent back unchanged. Vertex: `Part.ThoughtSignature` (carried in `llm.ToolCall.Signature`). LiteLLM encodes the signature into the tool-call `id`, so the ID is passed back unchanged.
- Vertex location defaults to `global` (Gemini 3.x Flash models are not served from `us-central1`, per the repo's earlier README).

## D5 — Temperature 0 and JSON schema on every call
- The research harness used temperature 0 with JSON *mode*. We use a response JSON *schema*, which constrains decoding slightly differently; the Gate B reproduction check (within ~2 points of 89.1%) covers this change.

## D6 — Country normalisation ports `norm()` with one fix
- `domain.NormalizeCountry` reproduces `norm()` from `predict.py` (including its quirks, e.g. "Bosnia And Herzegovina") so eval numbers are comparable. Exception: `norm("Türkiye")` returns "Trkiye" because the regex strips "ü"; we return "Turkey".

## D7 — Triage rule (human decision, 15 Oct)
- Fast path = engine confidence ≥ 0.95 **and** in scope. Scenario cases are **not** excluded. The matching research statistic is "confidence ≥ 0.95 covers 72.4% at 97.8%" (held-out, n=273 of 377); the routed-pipeline "65% at 98.0%" describes a different rule and must not be shown for this one.
- **Amended by D98 (19 Nov, #289):** "in scope" must be established. If a configured scope router fails, scope is unknown and the case goes to review whatever the confidence.

## D8 — Duty rates: sourced for the heat gun, illustrative elsewhere
- Heat gun: China ~41.7% vs Thailand ~16.7% ad valorem (HTS 8419.89.95, classification inferred and unverified for this model; Section 301 List 2 25% is the gap). Sources, dates and caveats in `docs/DUTY-SOURCES.md` (checked 15 Oct 2026). Show the source next to any duty number.
- Other fixtures (LiDAR) keep illustrative placeholder rates; annual volume and group costs are always user inputs labelled "illustrative — editable".

## D9 — CI smoke test without calling the private backend
- The backend accepts only `frontend@`; the CI deployer can't mint a token for it. The deploy smoke test checks the backend's latest revision is Ready and serving all traffic (startup config validation makes a bad config fail here) and that the frontend returns 200. Becomes an end-to-end predict call once the frontend proxies it.

## D10 — BM25 ported exactly, including IDF over the whole corpus
- `retrieval.BM25` mirrors predict.py: tokens `[a-z]{3,}` minus a stop list, first 3,000 characters of facts, k1 1.5, b 0.75, de-duplicated query terms, ties broken by higher corpus index. IDF and average length are computed over the whole corpus (including rulings later than a case); only the candidate set is date-filtered. That leaks term statistics, not text or holdings, and is kept so precedents match the research run.
- Parity on the 377 held-out cases (opt-in `TestResearchParity`): 369 identical precedent lists, 6 same set in another order, 2 with a different 5th precedent. Cause: the labels file gained manual labels after the research run, shifting corpus statistics.

## D11 — Facts splitter port is byte-identical
- `corpus.SplitFacts` reproduces split_facts.py on all 2,790 rulings (`go run ./cmd/corpus compare`), with character offsets handled as runes.

## D12 — Router regime label (new prompt section), dev-validated
- ROUTER.md has no regime check, so router-v1 added a regime label (textile 102.21, USMCA/Part 102, pharma API, substantial transformation, unclear). v1 over-triggered on any Canada/Mexico/USMCA mention (3 of the first 35 dev cases, all decided by CBP under substantial transformation); v2 narrows the USMCA definition. Only dev data was used.
- Measured by `eval scope` on dev: false out-of-scope rate on substantial-transformation rulings, and recall on labelled Part 102 / pharma-API rulings from Jul–Dec 2025. Labels have no separate textile regime (textile rulings fall under part102_tariff_shift).

## D13 — Router runs separately from the engine in eval
- With the router in the loop a dev run took ~45 min (Flash calls are slow through the proxy). The engine run and the router labelling now run separately; `eval report -scope` joins router labels to engine rows to compute the fast path. In the product both run per request.

## D14 — Cited rulings are only rulings shown to the model
- `cited_rulings` = precedents named in the model's reasoning/decisive step/key factor, in retrieval order; ruling numbers the model mentions that it was not shown are dropped. All shown precedents are returned separately as `precedents`.

## D15 — Planner moves operation groups; families are template files
- `planner/templates/*.json` define each family's operation groups (motorised tools & small appliances; LiDAR modules as a second family). Plants carry a capability matrix. The planner enumerates single and double group moves to capable plants in the target country; final assembly is not movable.

## D16 — Facts renderer matches CBP's restatement style (changed after the first heat-gun run)
- First version rendered processing detail for every group. On the 2022 heat gun the engine then predicted Thailand (0.78), though on CBP's own 2022 facts it predicts China (0.78) and CBP held China. The renderer now lists components by country, adds processing detail only for the groups a plan moves (as CBP did in N357771), and the final assembly.
- Consequence: the heat-gun acceptance run is a demo check on a renderer adjusted after seeing that fixture, not an unbiased evaluation. Unbiased planner evaluation needs fresh rulings (finals work).

## D17 — Plans start from CBP's holding on the current setup when the user has one
- Even with the corrected renderer, the engine called the 2022 heat gun Thai (0.78; CBP held China; on CBP's own facts the engine says China at 0.78 — a borderline case). With the wrong starting origin the verifier saw no change and rejected every move.
- A plan request may carry `current_origin` (country + ruling). It sets the starting origin for economics and is stated to the verifier ("CBP has already ruled on variant A: origin China"), the research "A's ruling known" setting (85.5% on 76 pairs). The engine's own baseline prediction is still shown next to CBP's holding. Verifier prompt version `verifier-v1+known-a` when used; dev-evaluated in both modes.

## D18 — Gate D runs on an in-memory store; corpus deployment is an open decision
- `store.Cases` has an in-memory implementation; Firestore drops in behind the same interface once the GCP project is available (SETUP-GCP.md). Cases are lost on restart and not shared between instances until then.
- The server needs the facts corpus (12 MB derived from public CBP rulings) at startup. The deploy builds from `backend/` only and respects `.gitignore`, so the corpus is not in the image. Options for the humans: (a) upload to the GCS bucket and load at startup (needs GCP access); (b) commit the facts files under `backend/data/` (derived public-domain text; the build prompt only rules out committing the 27 MB raw texts); (c) fetch from CROSS at startup (~50 min, not viable). Recommendation: (b) for the hackathon, (a) after.
- **Decided (human, 16 Oct): (b).** `backend/data/` holds `labels.jsonl` and the 2,790 facts files (13 MB); the Dockerfile copies them to `/data` and the server's defaults (`data/labels.jsonl`, `data/facts`) resolve from `backend/` locally and from `/` in the image. `make verify-data` checks the copy.
- Verifier evidence in production comes from live CROSS search with `toDate` (VERIFIER_SEARCH=cross); eval and the demo checks use the local corpus for reproducibility.

## D19 — Duty rates are inputs, not a package
- The build plan listed a `duty` package. Rates are product- and date-specific and the demo needs one sourced product; they are carried as request inputs (`economics.duty_rates` + a source note) and the demo fixture cites `docs/DUTY-SOURCES.md`. A dated HTS lookup can be added behind an interface later.

## D20 — Pasted facts are normalised to the benchmark format; the heat-gun prediction is borderline
- Corpus facts files, and therefore every benchmarked prompt, end with one newline. The API now appends it to pasted facts. Found because the heat-gun demo (CBP's own 2022 facts text) flipped with it: without the newline Thailand 5/5 runs (0.78); with it China 4/5 (0.75–0.85), Thailand 1/5. CBP held China.
- The change restores the evaluated input format; it was not chosen per case. The heat-gun current setup stays a borderline case for the engine: always below the 0.95 fast path (routed to expert review), and the demo should present it that way. Measured 16 Oct, gemini-4-argon via LiteLLM (proxy).

## D21 — Plan display bar stays at 0.95; near misses are shown greyed (human decision, 16 Oct)
- The heat-gun "motor-fan + PCBA" move is precedent-supported on every run (supporting N347421), but the verifier's confidence lands on 0.90 or 0.95, so it was shown in 1 of 3 runs. Dev data (54 pairs) showed every precedent-supported verdict correct at ≥ 0.90 and ≥ 0.95 alike; 0.95 only cuts recall (known-A: 13/46 vs 21/46). Small negative class (8).
- Kept 0.95 as the brief requires. Rejected plans now carry `rejection_kind`; `below_confidence_bar` rows (precedent-supported, confidence below 0.95) sort right after the current setup, and the UI shows them greyed with the label "Precedent-supported — below confidence bar" and their supporting ruling.

## D22 — Accuracy options tried on dev; none adopted (16 Oct)
- Hybrid/embedding retrieval and precedent-order voting were built (kept in code behind flags: `-retrieval`, `-votes`; engine defaults unchanged) and evaluated on dev only. None beat the baseline (eval/RESULTS.md). The configuration is unchanged, so the held-out split was not re-scored.
- Remaining error types need judgment the model lacks, not better retrieval. Family playbooks, the obvious next lever, overfit in research (dev 91.9% → test 88.3%) and are not retried before the deadline.

## D23 — Scoring adjustments: verified, mechanical, applied to every run
- `eval/data/scoring_adjustments.json`: 6 dev exclusions (4 multi-scenario holdings from `eval multiscenario`; H325607, a revocation covering two hose rulings with different origins; N354982, held "the country where the blanks are worked") and 1 alternative gold (N354448, CBP held "Indonesia/Malaysia"). Found by a mechanical scan of both splits (non-country labels, A/B holdings, revocations), each verified against the ruling text. Held-out: one flag (H360070), label correct; no change.
- Six labels the learnability judge called wrong (N353581, N354156, N356481, N350517, H331375, N348376-manual) were checked: CBP's holdings match the labels. The judge, being the same model, disagreed with CBP — not a label problem.
- `NormalizeCountry` keeps "A/B" answers as "A/B" (was glued into one word). Old result rows keep their stored prediction, so N354448 stays wrong in existing runs.
- Effect: dev 90.0% → 90.9% (421/463); held-out unchanged 89.9%.

## D24 — Clarify review: useful questions and conflicts, not an error detector
- `clarify` (clarify-v1, one extra call ~$0.009, only for in-scope cases below 0.95) reports whether an earlier ruling is on point, conflicting comparable rulings with the deciding difference, and up to 3 questions. Triage routes "no precedent on point + questions" to `needs_info`; `POST /v1/cases/{id}/answers` appends answers to the facts and re-analyses.
- Dev (127 below-threshold cases): the flags do **not** separate errors — engine error rate 26% with no precedent on point vs 22% with one; 24% where a conflict was found; needs_info 28% vs needs_review 21% (overlapping CIs). Reviewing the questions by hand, they target the facts CBP relied on (helmet: is the shell moulded to final shape; seamless pipe: are green tubes at final dimensions; filters: does the paper arrive in rolls or cut). Whether answers improve accuracy cannot be measured: the benchmark has no requester answers, and simulating them from ruling text would leak the holding.
- So it ships as broker-facing help, not as a claimed accuracy gain. No held-out run: clarify never changes the predicted origin, and its routing showed no error signal on dev.

## D25 — Cases persist as JSON files; Firestore before deploy (15 Oct)
- `GET /v1/cases` (Cases home page) needs cases to survive a restart. `store.File` writes one JSON file per case to `CASES_DIR` (default `../data/cases`, gitignored), atomically (temp file + rename); IDs are checked against `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$` so a crafted ID cannot escape the directory. `CASES_DIR=off` keeps the in-memory store (D33: an empty value means the default).
- Every write sets `updated_at`; export also records `exported_at`, which drives `step = export`. The list is ordered by `updated_at` descending.
- On Cloud Run the container disk is per-instance and lost on restart, so this is local persistence only. Before deploy, add a Firestore implementation of the same `store.Cases` interface (blocked on GCP setup).

## D26 — Follow-up answers do not settle split votes toward CBP; "unknown" answers stay out of the facts (15 Oct)
- On the 14 dev cases where the engine's 5 votes split between two origins, simulated-requester answers to the clarify questions moved the majority away from CBP more often than toward it (57% → 36% agreement; 1 fixed / 4 broken with "Not stated." lines, 0 / 3 with answered questions only). Small sample, but negative in both variants (eval/RESULTS.md).
- Mechanism: the engine treats "Not stated." and hedged answers as evidence against the processing country, and gives extra weight to facts restated in Q&A form. The simulated requester is also a weak stand-in for a real one: for NY rulings it had no facts beyond the input.
- Product changes: `POST /v1/cases/{id}/answers` keeps "unknown"/"not stated" answers on the case but does not add them to the facts, and refuses a set with no factual answer. Clarify stays broker-facing help (D24); a re-analysis after answers is not claimed to be more CBP-like. Not adopted: re-voting after answers as a confidence signal.
- Follow-up (answers-v2, adopted): `clarify.AppendAnswers` removes hedge sentences from answers and adds a header telling the engine the answers weigh the same as the facts and that unaddressed points are not evidence for any country. On the same 14 cases: 7/14 agree with CBP (0 fixed, 1 broken, vs 1/4 and 0/3 before). The remaining break is a judgment error on factual answers, not a format effect. The engine system prompt (engine-v1) is unchanged, so cases without answers are unaffected. Responses record `prompt_versions.answers`.
- Not tested: answers from real requesters with new facts, which the benchmark cannot supply.

## D27 — Written CBP rules for the confident errors: no gain, not adopted (15 Oct)
- The 12 high-confidence dev errors (96.4% accuracy at ≥ 0.95) split into about 5 real judgment errors (chemical change, preforms, essential character), 4 cases where CBP departed from a closely matching earlier ruling, and 3 scoring or label issues.
- rules-v1 (three short statements of CBP practice, appended to engine-v1) moved dev from 90.9% to 91.1%: 10 fixed, 9 broken, p = 1.0. The rules fixed 2 of the 5 targets and broke cases where CBP weighed other facts. Kept behind the eval flag `-rules`; the product prompt stays engine-v1. No held-out run.
- Conclusion with D22–D26: prompt-level changes do not move accuracy; the remaining confident errors need either examples of CBP's reasoning in training (fine-tuning on Vertex, finals) or human review, which the confidence bar already routes.

## D28 — CBP reasoning with each precedent: no gain, not adopted (15 Oct)
- Showing CBP's application paragraph under each precedent (reasoning-v1) moved dev from 90.9% to 91.4%: 7 fixed, 5 broken, p = 0.77, +13% cost. None of the 9 reviewed confident errors changed.
- With D22–D27, every in-context change tried (more votes, better retrieval, same-family precedents, written rules, precedent reasoning) lands within ±0.5 points on dev. The engine's remaining confident errors are stable judgments that context does not move. Next lever: fine-tuning on Vertex (blocked on GCP setup), with the train split only. Kept behind the eval flag `-reasoning`; the product is unchanged.

## D29 — Scenarios side by side and ruling watch (15 Oct)
- **Scenarios** (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/scenarios`): 1–3 alternative setups per case, each as facts or a structured product, predicted in parallel with the same engine, router and triage as predict. They skip the clarify review (one extra call each, and the question is the comparison) and never change the case's own prediction. Heat-gun demo via LiteLLM (`eval/results/scenarios-watch-heatgun-2022-litellm.json`, proxy): as today Thailand 0.75 (needs review); motor-fan assembly moved to Thailand 0.96 (auto); motor-fan + PCBA 0.98 (auto).
- **Watch** (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/watch`, `POST /v1/watch/run` for a scheduler): re-runs the engine with every precedent dated before today, where the original run only saw rulings dated before the case date. Records status (unchanged/changed), the new prediction and the precedents used that are newer than the case. It does not touch the case's prediction or updated_at, so the cases list order reflects user activity only. Heat gun: unchanged (Thailand 0.78), using N358092 (12 Feb 2026, held Thailand).
- New rulings only reach the pool when the corpus is rebuilt (`cmd/corpus fetch` + `build`); a scheduled rebuild plus `/v1/watch/run` is the deploy-time job (Cloud Scheduler, after the GCP setup). Not evaluated: watch is a product signal, not an accuracy claim.

## D30 — Plan search streams progress; baseline in parallel; concurrency 8 (15 Oct)
- `POST /v1/cases/{id}/plan` with `Accept: text/event-stream` streams the frontend's contract: `start` (every move, so rows draw as queued), `baseline`, `move_started`, `move_done` (the full Plan, economics included: each move waits for the baseline's origin before finishing), then `done` (same body as the JSON response) or `error` {code, message, retryable}, with `: keep-alive` comments every 10 s for Cloud Run. Plain JSON callers are unchanged. Live sample: `eval/results/plan-stream-heatgun-2022-litellm.sse` (proxy).
- The verifier is not deterministic through the proxy: two heat-gun runs today showed 1 plan above the 0.95 bar and then 0 (the 0.90-band plans greyed as below the bar), with the same engine predictions.
- The baseline prediction now runs alongside the moves (they need its facts, not its origin; economics are filled in after the search), and `PLANNER_CONCURRENCY` defaults to 8 (was 4) so the heat gun's 6 moves run in one wave. Heat gun via LiteLLM: 33.4 s → 24.6 s; all moves predicted by 11 s, the rest is the precedent checks (~10–15 s each, in parallel). Results and economics are unchanged (planner tests).

## D31 — Repeatable verdicts (cache), drafts for below-bar plans, CASES_DIR fix (15 Oct)
- Every model call is already at temperature 0, but the verifier's verdict on the same move still varied through the proxy (0.95 then 0.92), flipping the heat-gun Plan screen between "Best plan" and "No plan cleared the bar". `verifier.Cache` stores each verdict on disk, keyed by the task, verifier model and prompt version. A repeated plan search returns the same verdicts (heat gun: 6/6 reused, 29 s → 1 s). `VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR` defaults to `../data/verifier-cache` (gitignored); set `VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR=off` to disable, or delete the directory to re-verify. Plan stats report `verifier_cached`. This is caching, not a fix for the model's variability: the first verdict for a move is still one draw. Not done: majority-of-3 verification (3× verifier cost, ~$0.15 more per search), which would make the first draw steadier.
- `POST /v1/cases/{id}/export` accepts `move_key`: any shown plan, or a rejected plan whose precedent check found support below the bar (`below_confidence_bar`). The draft then states near the top: "Below the confidence bar: Origin Proof's precedent check rated this change 0.92, under its 0.95 bar…". Plans rejected for no precedent, another origin or a failed check return 400.
- Bug fixed: the `CASES_DIR` setting described in D25 was never read by the config loader, so the server kept cases in memory and lost them on restart. Now read (default `../data/cases`) and checked by a config test and a live restart.

## D32 — Auto-accept bar lowered from 0.95 to 0.92 (15 Oct)
- The user chose the lowest confidence at which cumulative accuracy stays at or above 95% on both splits. Proxy, engine-v1: confidence ≥ 0.92 is 95.7% on 81% of dev cases and 96.7% on 80% of held-out cases; with the router's scope check (fast path), 95.1% on 79.1% of dev and 96.6% (93.9–98.2) on 78.5% of held-out. At 0.95 it was 96.4% / 97.8% on ~72%.
- Trade-off: about 8% more cases skip expert review for about 1 point of accuracy; cases scored exactly 0.92 are ~88% right on their own, and the dev lower CI bound is 93.1%. Held-out confirmed the dev choice; it was not used to search for the bar. Re-check on Vertex before quoting official numbers.
- `FAST_PATH_CONFIDENCE` default (`config.DefaultFastPath`) is 0.92; the eval report and summary use it. The plan bar (verifier confidence for showing a plan, `PLAN_CONFIDENCE`) stays 0.95. Eval experiment flags (`clarify -below`, `torn -below`, `-vote-below`) keep 0.95 so recorded runs reproduce.

## D33 — Majority-of-3 precedent check: built, off by default (15 Oct)
- `verifier.NewMajority` runs the check n times in parallel and keeps the majority answer on support (ties against support), with the median confidence of the majority and `verdict.agreement` (e.g. "2/3"). The cache stores the majority verdict under a separate key.
- Two fresh heat-gun plan searches with 3 votes and no cache: 3 of 6 moves gave the same outcome and confidence, one move flipped between below_confidence_bar and no_supporting_precedent, and no plan was shown in either run. Each search took 54–73 s (18 agent runs through the proxy) against ~28 s with one vote.
- So `VERIFIER_VOTES` defaults to 1. The cache (D31) is what makes repeat searches identical; voting adds cost and latency without making the first verdict repeatable. Re-test on Vertex, where variance and rate limits may differ.
- Config: directory settings now take `off` to disable (`CASES_DIR=off`, `VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR=off`); an empty value means the default.

## D34 — BOM import and scenario pricing (15 Oct)
- `POST /v1/products/import` turns a CSV or Excel (.xlsx, first sheet; github.com/xuri/excelize/v2) bill of materials into the structured product: one row per component or operation, header aliases, countries normalised, groups matched to the family template, plants created from the plant column (or the country), capabilities from the groups each plant makes plus an optional `plant_capabilities` column. It returns warnings for what the plan search will lack rather than guessing capabilities. It does not create a case. `backend/testdata/heatgun_bom.csv` imports to the same 6 planner moves as the hand-written heat-gun fixture.
- Scenarios now carry a `comparison` against the current setup from the case's economics (given in the scenarios call, or kept from the last plan search): duty at the predicted origin, duty savings, added cost of the operation groups moved and net, labelled illustrative unless sourced. Heat-gun fixture economics: motor-fan assembly to Thailand saves $500k a year, adds $120k, net $380k. A product scenario that moves no operation group is the current setup: savings and net 0, duty at the current origin's rate, with `origin_differs_from_current` when the engine predicts another origin for it (the heat gun "as today" is predicted Thailand 0.75 while CBP held China). Facts-only scenarios have no moves to compare, so their savings follow the predicted origin.

## D35 — Plan bar lowered to 0.92 to match auto-accept (15 Oct)
- The user set the verifier confidence a precedent-supported plan needs to be shown to 0.92 (was 0.95), the same as the auto-accept bar (D32). `PLAN_CONFIDENCE` defaults to `config.DefaultPlanBar` = 0.92; `below_confidence_bar` now means precedent-supported under 0.92, and below-bar drafts name 0.92.
- Not measured separately: the verifier has no held-out test (README limitations), so this is a product choice, not a calibrated threshold. In the recorded stream (`eval/results/plan-stream-heatgun-2022-litellm.sse`, recorded under the 0.95 bar, so it still says below_confidence_bar) the motor-fan + PCBA move checked 0.92 and would now be shown; the other moves checked 0.65–0.90.

## D36 — Compare target countries; ask-the-case agent (15 Oct)
- **Compare targets** (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/compare-targets`): one plan search per target country (2–4), in parallel, ranked shown plan (best net) > below-bar > no supported plan > no capable plant. Moves only go to plants the product lists, so a country without a capable plant ranks last rather than being invented. Full results are stored for drafting (`export` with `target`); the case response carries only the ranking.
- **Case agent** (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/ask`, case-agent-v1): an ADK agent on the engine model with get_case, search_rulings / read_ruling (dated before the case, the case's own rulings excluded) and what_if (engine re-run on revised facts, ≤ 2 per question), ≤ 6 tool calls. Ruling numbers in an answer that the agent never saw are reported as dropped, not citations. The conversation is kept on the case. Heat gun via LiteLLM (`eval/results/ask-heatgun-2022-litellm.json`, proxy): "why Thailand when CBP held China?" read 5 rulings, cited 3 it had seen (35 s); "what if the motor-fan assembly were in Thailand?" ran one what-if, Thailand 0.98 (20 s). Answers ran slightly over the 150-word instruction.
- Compare demo (`backend/testdata/heatgun_multi.json`: heat gun plus illustrative Vietnam and Malaysia supplier plants, sourced rates in docs/DUTY-SOURCES.md; final assembly stays in Thailand because the template does not move it). One run via LiteLLM with the cache off (`eval/results/compare-targets-heatgun-multi-litellm.json`, proxy, 37 s): no country cleared the 0.92 bar. Malaysia (motor-fan + heating element, check 0.65, net $455k) and Thailand (PCBA, 0.65, $440k) were below the bar; Vietnam had no supported plan. Thailand's motor-fan + PCBA move, which checked 0.92 in an earlier run, varied again. Below-bar countries are ranked by precedent-check confidence before money.
- Not evaluated: the agent is an explanation and what-if aid; its what-ifs use the evaluated engine, its prose is not scored.

## D37 — Cases in Firestore on Cloud Run; GCP project set up (15 Oct)
- GCP (#37): Firestore, Cloud Storage and Vertex AI APIs enabled; Firestore database `(default)` created (Native, `us-central1`); corpus bucket `gs://wassup-hackathon-2026-origin-proof-corpus` created (uniform access, public access prevention) and holds the bundled corpus under `bundled/` (`make upload-corpus`). The bucket read grant is done (D39). `roles/datastore.user` still needs a project owner.
- `store.Firestore` implements `store.Cases`: one document per case in `cases`, holding the case as gzipped JSON (`case_json_gz`) plus `updated_at`. Gzip because a case with full plan and target searches can approach Firestore's 1 MiB document limit; the case shape stays defined in Go, with no Firestore field mapping to keep in sync. `List` reads every document and sorts in memory, the same as `store.File`; fine for a demo's tens of cases.
- `CASES_STORE=firestore|file|memory` defaults to `firestore` when `K_SERVICE` is set and `file` locally; `FIRESTORE_DATABASE` defaults to `(default)`. `deploy.yml` also sets `VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/verifier-cache`: the image's `/data` is owned by root and the distroless user is nonroot, so the default `../data/verifier-cache` would fail at startup. The verdict cache stays per instance.
- The corpus is still baked into the image (D18), so the service does not read the bucket at startup; the bucket is the shared copy for the team and for tuning data (#38). Reading facts from the bucket at startup would add start-up latency for the same bytes.
- Tested: gzip round trip; an emulator test (`FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`) covers put, get, list order and a restart. Run 15 Oct 2026 against the Firestore emulator (Java 21, `localhost:8085`): `TestFirestoreEmulator` passed.

## D38 — Tuning data for the origin engine, built and not launched (15 Oct)
- #38 plans to fine-tune the engine on CBP's own reasoning, because every in-context change (D22–D28) landed within ±0.5 points. This entry covers the data and tooling. No tuning job has been run and no model has been tuned.
- **Training split** (`eval tunesplit` → `eval/splits/splits_tune.json`, sha256-pinned): labelled single-origin substantial-transformation rulings with facts, dated before 2025-07-01 (the earliest dev/final/pilot ruling), minus every dev, final and pilot ruling. That gives 699 rulings, not the ~2,000 the issue estimated: 632 `train` and 67 `tune_check` (10% by FNV-1a of the ID), so epochs are chosen without touching dev.
- **Examples** (`eval tunedata`, Vertex Gemini supervised-tuning JSONL, written to the gitignored `data/tuning/`): the engine-v1 system prompt and the exact engine-v1 user message (facts plus the 5 dated BM25 precedents). The target is the engine's answer from `eval run -split train|tune_check -splits ../eval/splits/splits_tune.json`, with CBP's held origin and `corpus.Reasoning` as the reasoning. Where the engine's origin was wrong, its decisive step argued for the wrong country, so the target's decisive step becomes CBP's holding, flip is null and confidence is 0.9. That is a choice to revisit: a constant confidence on corrected examples could teach the model a fixed confidence. Examples without extractable reasoning are skipped. A precedent mismatch against the run's recorded precedents is counted.
- **Launch** (`scripts/tune-vertex.sh`, paid, not run): uploads the JSONL to the corpus bucket and creates a regional `tuningJobs` request with `BASE_MODEL` given explicitly. Which Gemini models Vertex can tune, and the price per training token, were not confirmed. A web search turned up rates for older and Flash-Lite models only, so check the Vertex tuning docs before choosing.
- Next, in order: the engine run on train + tune_check (~700 engine calls), then `eval tunedata`, the tuning job, a dev comparison with `compare` (McNemar, fast-path accuracy, calibration by band), and one held-out run only if dev improves. The Vertex path itself works (D39). The tuning job is still not launched.

## D40 — Demo seed and recording runbook (16 Oct)
- `make demo-seed` (`backend/cmd/demo`) prepares a running server for the video through the real API: a case per demo, its plan search, and for the heat gun the scenarios, ruling watch, one ask and the multi-country comparison. It repeats the heat-gun plan search and checks the verdicts match, so replays during recording are identical with the verdict cache on. LiteLLM run (`eval/results/demo-seed-litellm.json`, proxy): 3.5 min; repeat search identical, 6/6 from cache, 8 s.
- The seed records the first verdict and does not re-roll for a better one: re-seeding until a plan clears the bar would misrepresent the system. In this run no heat-gun or LiDAR plan cleared 0.92, so the runbook (`docs/DEMO.md`) covers the "No plan cleared the bar" path and uses scenarios (motor-fan to Thailand 0.95, net $380k, illustrative) as the stable "move X saves $Y" story.

## D41 — Case audit trail (16 Oct)
- Every case keeps `history`, oldest first (last 200): predicted, answered, plan_searched, scenarios, targets_compared, watched, asked and exported, each with a time, a one-line outcome (origin and confidence, plans shown, the move drafted and whether it was below the bar, cached checks) and, where a model was used, the provider, model and prompt version. A compliance lead can show how the recommendation was reached. Returned with the case; no separate endpoint.
- LiDAR sample BOM (`backend/testdata/lidar_bom.csv`) imports to the same planner moves as the LiDAR demo; BOM import now accepts `plant` rows for plants that make nothing yet.

## D42 — Draft a structured product from pasted facts (16 Oct)
- `POST /v1/products/extract` (extract-v3) lets the "Paste a description" path feed the plan search: the engine model lists parts with their stated countries, assembly steps and named plants from numbered paragraphs; code checks groups against the template, makes "<Country> plant" plants when none is named (flagged), cites paragraphs, warns about gaps and asks what else each plant could make. Nothing is saved.
- Heat-gun CBP facts via LiteLLM (`eval/results/extract-heatgun-2022-litellm.json`, proxy): 15 parts, 3 Thailand and 12 China, exactly as the text states; 5 assembly operations in Thailand, one per sentence of the assembly paragraph; warnings for "24 components; it names 15" and the 6 parts mentioned without a country. ~15.5 s with Argon or Flash. extract-v1 had assigned those 6 parts to China by inference and counted a sourcing sentence as an operation; v2 stopped that but split steps too finely.

## D43 — Money inputs at predict time and editable on the case (16 Oct)
- `POST /v1/cases/predict` accepts optional `economics`; `PUT /v1/cases/{id}/economics` replaces them later without model calls (recorded in the case history). Plan, scenarios and compare-targets use the case's economics when a call omits its own; the plan search did not fall back before (scenarios and compare-targets did), so economics given at predict would have been ignored by it. Rates must be fractions between 0 and 1 and amounts non-negative, which catches "41.7" typed for 41.7%. The heat-gun fixture economics round-trip unchanged.

## D44 — Untuned Gemini 3.5 Flash on dev; tuning not launched (15 Oct)
- `gemini-4-argon` tuning is not supported (model page, 15 Oct 2026). Tunable bases include Gemini 3.5 Flash. Smoke call of `gemini-3.5-flash` on Vertex `global` succeeded (12.7 s).
- Dev only, engine-v1, BM25, K = 5, no `-rules`, no `-reasoning`, no `-votes`. Adjustments (D23): 419/463 = 90.5% (87.5–92.8). Argon on the same rows is 91.1% (88.2–93.4). McNemar: Flash fixed 16, broke 19, p = 0.736. Unadjusted Flash is 419/469 = 89.3% (86.2–91.8). Held-out was not run.
- Fast path, same router-v2 scope file as the Argon dev run: Flash covers 424/463 = 91.6% at 92.0% (89.0–94.2). Argon covers 79.5% at 96.5%. Flash clears the ~3-point overall-accuracy rule, and it misses the 95% fast-path accuracy the 0.92 bar was set for. Latency p50 12.1 s / p95 51.2 s, against Argon 4.8 s / 8.1 s. Mean output is about 1,450 tokens because reasoning is included; Argon output is about 220.
- Cost: the result file records $0 because `evalkit` has no Flash 3.5 price. Vertex global standard, checked 15 Oct 2026, is $1.50 / $9.00 per 1M input / output tokens. That is about $0.017 per case and about $8 for this dev run, against Argon's $0.0079 promo.
- Decision: overall accuracy says a tuned Flash could plausibly match Argon, so this is not closed as a negative. The job was not launched. Supervised fine-tuning for Gemini 3.5 Flash is $0.01 per 1,000 training tokens ($10 per 1M). A rough bill, not a quote from a built dataset: 632 examples × ~3,000 tokens × 3 epochs is on the order of $60 of training, plus about $12 to generate the train and tune_check targets at the rate above. Anas: no Flash tuning before the freeze. D44 stays open. No tuning job was launched.
- Staging, from this baseline (`76b1720`), service `origin-proof-api-staging` revision `origin-proof-api-staging-00001-sgq` (URL `https://origin-proof-api-staging-guxax2teha-uc.a.run.app`). Engine Gemini 4 Argon, `LLM_PROVIDER=vertex`, `CASES_STORE=firestore`, `VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/verifier-cache`, timeout 300 s, service account `agent-backend`. Production `backend` was not changed (`backend-00014-dt5`). `GET /health` and the demo catalog returned 200. `POST /v1/cases/predict` ran the model (10.7 s) then the save failed, and `GET /v1/cases` failed: `PermissionDenied`, `agent-backend` had no `roles/datastore.user`. The seed client exited 1 with 403 on `GET /health` because it sent no identity token. Plan search was not run.

## D45 — Rate limits, pinned demo cases, cost on every step (16 Oct)
- Endpoints that call a model are rate-limited per client: light ones (predict, answers, ask, extract, scenarios, watch) 20 a minute, heavy ones (plan, compare-targets) 4 a minute, and a global cap of 120 light units a minute in which a heavy request counts 5 (`RATE_LIGHT_PER_MIN`, `RATE_HEAVY_PER_MIN`, `RATE_GLOBAL_PER_MIN`; 0 turns a limit off). Over the limit: 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`. Deployed, the backend is private and only the frontend's proxy calls it, so the proxy must forward the browser's address as `X-Client-IP`; without it every visitor shares one bucket and the global cap is what protects the bill.
- `ADMIN_TOKEN`, sent as `X-Admin-Token` (not `Authorization`, which carries Cloud Run's identity token), bypasses the limits and is required to pin a case, delete a pinned case and run the batch watch (`POST /v1/watch/run`, which re-runs every case). Empty: nobody is admin. `make run` and `make demo-seed` use a local default; deployed it must come from Secret Manager (at least 16 characters).
- `DELETE /v1/cases/{id}` and `PUT /v1/cases/{id}/pin`. Pinned cases list first and survive visitors on the shared Firestore store. The demo seed pins the cases it creates and sends `ID_TOKEN` as the identity token for a private Cloud Run service.
- Each history event carries `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cost_usd` and `latency_ms`: engine, router and clarify for a prediction; engine and verifier for a plan search (cached checks cost nothing); every scenario; the agent model only for an ask. Cost uses the eval's promo prices, so it matches `eval/RESULTS.md`. Heat gun via LiteLLM (proxy): predicted with clarify, 7,267 / 746 tokens, $0.0188, 13.6 s.

## D46 — Which conflicting ruling the case leans to (clarify-v2, 16 Oct)
- When clarify finds two comparable rulings that went different ways, it now also returns `conflict.leans`: "a" or "b", the ruling this case's facts are closer to on the deciding difference, or null when the facts do not say. Any other value is dropped to null. The Result screen's "Earlier rulings disagree" panel marks that ruling. Clarify still never changes the origin or the route.
- Re-run on the 29 dev cases that had a conflict under clarify-v1 (LiteLLM, proxy; `eval/results/clarify-dev-conflicts-clarify-v2-litellm.jsonl`): 26 still show a conflict; leans a 21, b 4, null 1. The leaned ruling's holding names the engine's predicted country in 21 of 25, a crude check since a lean can follow a principle rather than a country. Cabin air filter N351082: a = N350345 (Thailand, oil filter), b = N348086 (South Korea, water filter media), leans "b", matching the engine's reasoning that the filter media decides. $0.010 per review. Routing on this subset: 3 needs_info, 26 needs_review (clarify-v1: 4 and 25; run-to-run variation of the on-point judgement).

## D47 — Deep health check and portfolio triage (16 Oct)
- `GET /health?deep=1` writes, reads and deletes a probe case and makes one small call to the router model; 503 with the failing check's error otherwise. Staging's missing `roles/datastore.user` only surfaced after a 10.7 s predict; this reports it in about a second, for the deploy smoke test and before recording. Model errors are reduced to their kind or the provider's HTTP status and body, so a proxy URL never appears. Rate-limited like a light request.
- `POST /v1/cases/batch` triages up to 20 products by their facts (JSON, or a CSV/xlsx with name, facts and optional date), 4 at a time, each saved as an ordinary case. Rows carry origin, confidence, route, out-of-scope, the triage reason, cost and a per-row error, so one bad row does not fail the batch; the summary counts each route. It shows the fast path across a portfolio, which is how a compliance team works. Rate limit: one heavy request per client and 20 units of the global cap (the global cap must now be at least 20).
- Sample `backend/testdata/portfolio_sample.csv` via LiteLLM (proxy; `eval/results/batch-portfolio-sample-litellm.json`): heat gun China 0.85 needs info; both dryer latches auto (Thailand 0.96, China 0.99); sample neck gaiter flagged out of scope (textile rules); sample cordless drill China 0.72 needs review. 5 products in 15.9 s, $0.068. Deep health: store 1 ms, model 4.3 s, ok.

## D48 — Rename a case (16 Oct)
- `PATCH /v1/cases/{id}` with `display_name` (whitespace collapsed, 1–200 characters) returns the updated Cases-home row. A separate `display_name` keeps `product_name` as entered, since that name is part of the facts the engine read. Rows and the case response always carry `display_name` (the user's name, else the product name). Pinned demo cases need the admin token, as for delete. Recorded as `renamed` in the history; no model call; `updated_at` is unchanged.

## D49 — LiteLLM proxy removed; Vertex AI is the only model provider (16 Oct)
- D3 used a LiteLLM (OpenAI-compatible) proxy because the team had no Vertex access. Vertex is now set up (#37, D37), so the proxy provider is gone: `internal/llm/litellm.go` and its tests are deleted, along with `LITELLM_BASE_URL` / `LITELLM_API_KEY`, the LiteLLM embedder, and the provider cases in the server, eval and corpus commands.
- `LLM_PROVIDER` now defaults to `vertex` (Gemini on Vertex AI with Application Default Credentials; `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` required, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=global` because Gemini 4 Argon and 3.8 Flash are only on the global endpoint). `fake` stays for tests and offline UI work and is still refused on Cloud Run. `LLM_PROVIDER=litellm` is now a config error that names this decision.
- Recorded results are unchanged. Files named `*-litellm.*`, the eval summary's "proxy, development only" status and the report's proxy banner describe how those numbers were produced. The official engine numbers are the Vertex re-run (D39). No new model calls were made for this removal.

## D50 — Verdict cache shared in Firestore on Cloud Run (16 Oct)
- On Cloud Run the verdict cache was `/tmp/verifier-cache`: lost on every restart and separate per instance (up to 3). A replayed plan search could land on another instance and verify afresh, so the "replays are identical" promise (D31, `docs/DEMO.md`) did not hold in production.
- `VERIFIER_CACHE_STORE` is `firestore` (default when cases are in Firestore), `file` (`VERIFIER_CACHE_DIR`, default locally) or `off`. Firestore keeps one document per cache key in `verifier_cache`, created with `Create` so the first verdict for a key wins when two instances verify the same move at once. A failed cache read is an error rather than a silent re-verify. `deploy.yml` sets `VERIFIER_CACHE_STORE=firestore`.
- Unit-tested with a shared in-memory store (two caches, one verdict) and in the Firestore emulator test; the Firestore path still needs a staging run (`scripts/smoke.sh` twice: the second plan search reports all checks cached).

## D51 — Textile rules (19 CFR 102.21) as a second regime, off until evaluated (16 Oct)
- Cases the router labels `textile_102_21` were out of scope. With `TEXTILE_ENGINE=on` they get a prediction from the same engine with a textile prompt (`textile-v1`: 102.21(c)(1)–(5) in order, minor processes never confer origin, the deciding rule named) and precedents drawn only from textile rulings dated before the case. Triage reasons start with "textile rules (19 CFR 102.21)". Clarify is skipped (its prompt is about substantial transformation).
- Until the held-out run sets a bar, `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0` sends every textile case to expert review: the regime has no accuracy result yet. Off by default, so production is unchanged until the eval is in.
- Data: 371 labelled rulings (`part102_tariff_shift` whose text cites 102.21, with a primary origin and facts), built by `eval/splits/build_textile_split.py` into `eval/splits/splits_textile.json` (dev = Jul–Dec 2025, 99; final = 2026, 71; frozen 16 Oct) and `backend/data/textile_rulings.json` for the server.
- Eval command: `go run ./cmd/eval run -regime textile -split dev`, then held-out once with `-split final -confirm-final`. The fast-path bar is the lowest confidence where held-out accuracy is ≥ 95%, as for D32.

**Scored 16 Oct 2026** (Vertex, `gemini-4-argon`, `textile-v1`, K = 5, prompt frozen before held-out). Dev 79/99 = 79.8% (95% CI 70.8–86.5), about 11 points under the substantial-transformation dev result of 91.1% (88.2–93.4) and well above always-China at 30/99 = 30.3%. A where-assembled read of the facts is 21/99 = 21.2%. Cost $0.0092 promo per case; latency p50 5.5 s, p95 68.1 s. Confidence ≥ 0.95 is 64/67 = 95.5%. Held-out, scored once: 61/71 = 85.9% (76.0–92.2); $0.0093 per case; p50 5.7 s, p95 47.7 s. The lowest confidence at which held-out accuracy stays ≥ 95% is **0.95** (40/42 = 95.2%, CI 84.2–98.7; 42 of 71 cases). No held-out score falls in [0.93, 0.95), so 0.93 would select the same cases; 0.92 falls to 45/49 = 91.8%. Proposed `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0.95`. `TextileSystem`, K, and `splits_textile.json` were not changed because of the held-out result. Production stays off until Anas turns it on. Rows and the error lists are in `eval/RESULTS.md`.

Staging check the same day, revision `origin-proof-api-staging-00008-665`, `TEXTILE_ENGINE=on` and `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0.95`. Gaiter facts (knit polyester neck gaiters; fabric knit and dyed in China, cut and sewn in Vietnam; date 2026-09-01) came back Vietnam at 0.96 under (c)(2), prediction `prompt_version` `textile-v1`, triage reason starting "textile rules (19 CFR 102.21)". The check case was deleted afterward. Production was not deployed.

**Production, 16 Oct 2026.** Anas approved turning the textile engine on, auto-accept at 0.95. `deploy.yml` sets `TEXTILE_ENGINE=on` and `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0.95`, and keeps `VERIFIER_CACHE_STORE=firestore` and the `ADMIN_TOKEN` secret. The basis is the held-out result above: 85.9% overall, 40/42 = 95.2% at confidence ≥ 0.95, Wilson lower bound 84.2%. The weak spot is the two held-out misses at or above 0.95, both China predicted as Slovakia (N364456 at 0.98, N364457 at 0.95), both under (c)(2). `TextileSystem`, K, and the split were not changed.

## D52 — Sourced duty rates fill the plan's economics (16 Oct)
- Duty rates were typed into the economics by hand. `backend/internal/duty/rates.json` now holds the researched rates (HTS 8419.89.95 for China 41.7%, Thailand 16.7%, Vietnam 16.7%, Malaysia 14.2%, from `docs/DUTY-SOURCES.md`, checked 15 Oct 2026), each total broken into MFN and additional duties with its HTS heading, source URL and whether it is confirmed from a primary source (Vietnam's forced-labor 12.5% and the China stacking were not at first; both were confirmed from the notice text and HTS note 52(a) on 17 Oct).
- A product may carry `hts`. Plan, scenarios and compare-targets fill every origin the table has that the user did not give; the user's rates always win. `economics.duty_rates_from_table` lists the filled origins and `duty_rates_note` cites the table. Table rates are not stored on the case, so a later table update applies to the next search. `GET /v1/duty-rates?hts=` returns the breakdown for the UI.
- The heat-gun demo now gets its rates from the table (its fixture drops the typed rates and gains `hts`); import value and group costs stay illustrative. Only researched lines are in the table; anything else stays the user's input, and the table is not a classification tool.
- **17 Oct:** second line, HTS 9015.80.20 for the LiDAR demo (Hesai OT128): China 37.5%, Thailand 12.5%, Vietnam 12.5%, Malaysia 10% (MFN Free; China 301 List 1; forced-labor 301), all components confirmed from primary sources. The classification itself is inferred from LiDAR classification rulings, not ruled for this model; 9015.10.40 would change the totals (`docs/DUTY-SOURCES.md`). The LiDAR fixture drops its placeholder rates (Thailand 10%, Vietnam 5%, China 35%) and gains `hts`; each line now records its own check date.

## D53 — textile-v2: CBP's reasoning with each precedent (16 Oct)
- Dev error analysis of textile-v1 (21 misses, LiteLLM proxy, 78.8%): 7 are not textile goods at all (steering wheel, brake calipers, potato flakes, LED module, foil, carbonless paper, skate inserts; CBP applied 102.11/102.20 and mentions 102.21 only in passing), so the split is not pure; in production the router sends those to `usmca_part102`. Most of the rest are filled or made-up home textiles (comforters, quilts, mattress pads, pillows) where the model credited the shell-fabric country under (c)(2)/(c)(4) and CBP decided under (c)(5) or a heading rule. Restricting the split to rulings that cite 102.21(c) is not a fix: it removes many correct cases too (held-out 33/40 = 82.5%).
- Variants on the same 99 dev cases (proxy; McNemar against v1): K = 8, 79.8% (no gain); precedent reasoning (each precedent's CBP law-and-analysis, as in reasoning-v1), 84.8% (fixed 9, broke 3, p = 0.15); **textile-v2 = reasoning plus one paragraph on filled made-ups, 85.9% (77.7–91.4), fixed 8, broke 1, p = 0.039**. The paragraph comes from the textile rulings dated before the dev window (filled made-ups: 14 decided under (c)(2), 11 under (c)(5), 2 under (c)(4)), not from dev labels. Fast path unchanged: ≥ 0.95 covers 66/99 at 95.5%. Cost $0.0109 per case (+20%, longer prompts); p50 6.9 s.
- Caveats: the held-out split was spent on textile-v1 (D51), and v2 was chosen on dev, so there is no independent test of v2 yet. Next independent check: textile rulings issued after 24 Sep 2026 (the split's last date). Until then production stays on v1 (D51, `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0.95`).
- Server: `TEXTILE_PROMPT=v2` loads `backend/data/textile_reasoning.json` (CBP reasoning for 366 of the 371 textile rulings, written by `go run ./cmd/corpus reasoning`). Eval: `eval run -regime textile -textile-prompt v2 -reasoning`.

**Vertex dev, 16 Oct 2026.** Same 99 dev cases on Vertex (`gemini-4-argon`, `global`). Not held-out. 86/99 = 86.9% (95% CI 78.8–92.2). Against the Vertex textile-v1 dev file (79/99 = 79.8%, 70.8–86.5): fixed 8, broke 1, exact McNemar p = 0.039. Confidence ≥ 0.95 is 66/68 = 97.1% (89.9–99.2). Cost $0.0111 promo per case; latency p50 7.2 s, p95 76.6 s. Five calls returned 429 or 504 and were retried; the result file has one row per ruling and no remaining call error. `rulings.cbp.gov` answered 403 from this environment and the corpus bucket has no raw JSON, so the 366 excerpts in `textile_reasoning.json` were what `-reasoning` attached (the same text `TEXTILE_PROMPT=v2` loads). `TEXTILE_PROMPT` stays v1, so merging this does not change production. Anas decides whether to switch.

**Production, 16 Oct 2026.** Anas approved switching production to textile-v2. `deploy.yml` sets `TEXTILE_PROMPT=v2` and keeps `TEXTILE_ENGINE=on`, `TEXTILE_FAST_PATH=0.95`, `VERIFIER_CACHE_STORE=firestore`, and the `ADMIN_TOKEN` secret. Basis: the Vertex dev result above, 86.9% against textile-v1 at 79.8% (fixed 8, broke 1, p = 0.039), with confidence ≥ 0.95 at 97.1% (66/68). Not yet tested on unseen rulings. The first independent check is textile rulings issued after 24 Sep 2026. Latency settings were left unchanged: the slow plan searches were long single `gemini-4-argon` calls, not a quota rejection.

## D54 — Engine predictions cached; 30 s per model call (16 Oct)
- On 16 Oct, 7 of 37 production `gemini-4-argon` calls failed after all retries (429 Resource exhausted, 504 Deadline expired, or cut off at Cloud Run's 300 s), all between 08:35 and 09:10 UTC; Flash calls kept succeeding. No project quota was exceeded (token quotas 5M/min for Argon; no requests-per-minute quota exists for it), so it is shared Vertex capacity, not something to raise.
- Engine predictions are now cached in the same store as verdicts (D50): Firestore collection `engine_cache` on Cloud Run, `engine-cache` beside the verdict directory locally. The key is model, prompt version, K, voting, facts, case date and exclusions, so a prompt change or a later date predicts afresh. A replayed prediction has `trace.cached: true` and no tokens or cost. With verdicts and predictions cached, replaying a seeded demo case, including its plan search, makes no Argon calls. Cases whose ID is a ruling number (eval rows) are never cached.
- `LLM_TIMEOUT` defaults to 30 s per attempt instead of 90 s. Normal calls take 2–10 s; with 3 retries and backoff the worst case is about 2 minutes, inside Cloud Run's 300 s, instead of a request cut off with no answer.
- A Gemini 3.5 Flash fallback for Argon failures was considered and not built (Anas's call; D44 found Flash overconfident at the 0.92 bar).

## D55 — Plan-stream race fixed; the seed survives provider outages (17 Oct)
- The plan stream's keep-alive goroutine could write to the response after the handler returned (`go test -race` caught it once in CI; reproduced 1 in 200 runs with a 50 µs keep-alive). The handler now waits for that goroutine to exit before returning. On a server this could have written a keep-alive into a reused connection.
- The production seed stopped on the LiDAR plan search when `gemini-4-argon` returned 504 after all retries. `cmd/demo` now retries a request that fails with 502/503/504 or a transport error after 1, 2, 4 and 8 minutes. Retrying cannot change a verdict already given: finished predictions and checks are cached (D50, D54).

## D56 — Demo cases stay as seeded (17 Oct)
- The production dry-run found 15 pinned demo cases (3 seed runs × 5 demos), several named after the first words of their facts, and opening the Draft page on a demo wrote an `exported` event to it. Viewing the demo should not change it.
- `POST /v1/cases/{id}/export` on a pinned case without the admin token now returns the draft without saving anything (as `preview: true` does); with the token it records as before.
- `make demo-seed` names each case after its demo title (`display_name`) and skips a demo that already has a pinned case with that name (`-force` seeds anyway). `scripts/demo-dedupe.sh <API>` cleans an existing server: it keeps one pinned case per demo (furthest step, then newest), renames it, and deletes the rest; dry run unless `APPLY=1`. Tested locally on 15 duplicates down to 5, keeping the two dryer latches apart (their facts share the first 408 characters).
- The case response adds `economics_priced`: the stored economics with the sourced duty rates a plan search would use (D52), so the Scenarios money strip can show them; `economics` stays exactly what the user entered.

## D57 — Security review fixes before the freeze (17 Oct)
A read-only review of `main` (rate limits, admin token, case edits, uploads, caches, the frontend proxy) found two high and two medium issues, fixed here; the rest are listed for later.
- **Demo cases are read-only for visitors (high).** Only delete and rename checked `pinned`. A visitor could answer clarify questions on a demo ("all components are made in China") and change its prediction for every viewer, set absurd economics, wipe or write its Ask chat, or replace its saved plan. Now, without the admin token, edits that change what a demo shows (economics, answers, chat reset) return 403, and plan, scenarios, compare-targets, watch and ask still answer but do not save to the demo. Export already did not (D56).
- **Excel uploads are bounded (high).** `ReadRows` used excelize's defaults (about 16 GB unzip limit) and `GetRows`, which pads rows to their last column: a 614 KB file allocated 1.8 GB in a test, enough to kill a Cloud Run instance and everything on it. It now unzips at most 32 MB (16 MB of sheet XML), streams rows, stops at 1,000 rows and 64 columns. `POST /v1/products/import` is now rate-limited (light).
- **A batch takes a client's whole heavy burst (medium):** one batch a minute per client, so two clients can no longer use up the global cap. **The limiter no longer forgets new clients when full, and groups IPv6 by /64 (medium):** a new entry was created with no last-seen time and dropped at once when the map was over 10,000, which turned per-client limits off for new visitors.
- Fine as is: header spoofing through the proxy (it builds headers itself and takes the rightmost X-Forwarded-For), the admin token (constant-time, never sent from the browser), SSRF (ruling IDs validated), prompt injection (tools read only this case and public rulings), cache keys (cover the full model input), error bodies, `deploy.yml`.
- Later: visitors can list, rename and delete each other's unpinned cases (no ownership); the proxy buffers request bodies up to Cloud Run's 32 MiB; `GET /v1/rulings/{id}` is not rate-limited (CROSS could block our user agent); `GET /v1/cases` reads every document; first-write-wins caches keep a verdict made during a CROSS outage; limits are per instance.

## D58 — Hardening after the review: lookups, verdicts without evidence, list cap, case ownership (17 Oct)
Four "later" items from the D57 review.
- **Ruling lookups are rate-limited.** `GET /v1/rulings/{id}` fetches from CBP's CROSS; enumerating IDs could get our user agent blocked, which would break the precedent checks and Ask. Fetches now take from a per-client lookup bucket (`RATE_LOOKUP_PER_MIN`, default 30) that does not touch the model budget. A ruling already in memory is free.
- **A precedent check that hit a tool error is not cached.** If CROSS failed during a check, the agent answered without that evidence and the verdict was cached forever (D50 is first-write-wins). The verifier now counts failed search/read calls (`ToolErrors`, summed across majority votes), and the cache skips those verdicts, so the next run checks again.
- **`GET /v1/cases` is capped:** every pinned case plus the 200 newest others (`CASES_LIST_LIMIT`). On Firestore that is two single-field queries (`pinned == true`, `updated_at` descending with a limit) instead of reading and unzipping every document. Cases now store `pinned` as a Firestore field; `PUT /v1/cases/{id}/pin` with `pinned: true` on an already pinned case re-saves it to write the field for cases saved earlier.
- **Case ownership, off until the frontend sends a visitor ID.** With `CASE_OWNERSHIP=on`, each case created stores a hash of the `X-Visitor-ID` header (never returned), and a visitor sees the pinned demos and their own cases only; another visitor's case is a 404 on every endpoint, so its existence does not leak. The admin token sees everything. The frontend proxy has to set the header from an httpOnly cookie (`docs/issues/frontend-visitor-id.md`); until then the server behaves as before. Cases made before this have no owner and are visible only to the admin once ownership is on.

## D59 — Daily model-spend cap; one warm instance (17 Oct)
- The rate limits live in each instance, so with up to 3 instances the real global cap is about 3× the configured one (D57). A spend cap bounds the Vertex bill whatever the traffic: every model call's estimated cost (promo prices, as in the history's `cost_usd`) is added to a per-UTC-day total in Firestore (`model_spend/<date>`, atomic increment, shared by all instances). Once the total reaches `DAILY_SPEND_CAP_USD` (production: $25; 0 disables it), model-calling endpoints return 503 `budget_reached` with `Retry-After` set to the next UTC day. Reads, the pinned demos and cached replays keep working; the admin token (the seed) is counted but not refused. The frontend shows a calm note with a link back to the demos.
- For scale: a full heat-gun plan search costs about $0.11 and a predict with clarify about $0.02, so $25 is well over 100 uncached plan searches a day.
- `deploy.yml` sets `--min-instances 1`, so the first visit after a quiet spell does not wait out a cold start (18.7 s measured on 16 Oct). It costs one always-on instance; set it back to 0 after judging.
- The fake model now reports a little token usage, so the cap can be checked locally.

## D60 — Textile and routing numbers on the Accuracy page; proxy body cap (17 Oct)
- The Accuracy page showed only the substantial-transformation results. `eval summary` now also writes, from the result files: textile-v1 held-out (`textile_final_*`: 61/71 = 85.9%, 76.0–92.2; at confidence ≥ 0.95, 40/42 = 95.2% on 59% of cases, the textile auto-accept bar), textile-v2 on dev (`textile_v2_dev_*`, 86.9%, the prompt in production), and three router numbers from the Vertex dev-window scope check (PR #142, `scope-window-dev-router-v2-vertex.jsonl`): pharmaceutical and USMCA rulings kept out of the substantial-transformation engine 38/43 = 88.4% (75.5–94.9); textile rulings sent to the textile engine 29/33 = 87.9% (72.7–95.2); substantial-transformation rulings wrongly sent out of scope 2/40 = 5.0% (1.4–16.5). Router and textile-v2 numbers are labelled development set. The page adds a "Beyond substantial transformation" row of three cards. Existing summary items are unchanged.
- The frontend proxy read any request body into memory, up to Cloud Run's 32 MiB (security review L1). It now reads at most 6 MB (the backend's upload cap is 5 MB), counting chunked bodies too, and answers 413 above that.

## D61 — USMCA / Part 102 rules as a third regime: data first, off until evaluated (17 Oct)
- Cases the router labels `usmca_part102` (USMCA preference, or marking of a good of Canada or Mexico) were out of scope, and the corpus had only 33 labelled non-textile Part 102 rulings (dev 5, held-out 7), too few to measure anything. CBP decides these under 19 CFR 102.11 with the 102.20 tariff shifts, not substantial transformation.
- Data: `go run ./cmd/corpus usmca-fetch` pages CROSS search (`102.20` and `102.11`, newest first, back to 2015) into `data/corpus/usmca_index.jsonl` (509 rulings) and downloads the raw JSON (322 new, about one request a second; rulings whose CROSS tariffs are all textile chapters are skipped). `corpus usmca-label` keeps the rulings whose text applies 102.11 or a tariff shift and no paragraph of 102.21 (`corpus.USMCARegime`), writes their facts with the existing splitter, reuses the 182 rows already in `labels.jsonl`, and labels the other 291 with a Go port of the research regex labeller (`corpus.LabelHolding`: method `regex`, the holding sentences as evidence; it gives the same primary origin as `labels.jsonl` on all 2,696 regex rows). Result: `eval/data/labels_usmca.jsonl`, 473 rulings, **356 with a primary origin** (2015–2026). `labels.jsonl` is unchanged.
- Split: `eval/splits/build_usmca_split.py` writes `eval/splits/splits_usmca.json` (frozen 17 Oct): dev = 2024–2025, 72 cases; final = 2026, 32 cases, held out. 2025 alone had about 40, so dev takes two years. 38 rulings whose holding sentences answer only a substantial-transformation, Section 301 or procurement question stay precedents but are not scored (decided from the labels before any run). Precedents for each case are the labelled Part 102 rulings dated before it.
- Prompt `usmca-v1` (`engine.USMCASystem`): 102.11(a)(1)–(3), (b) essential character among the materials that cannot make the shift (102.18(b)), (c) sets, (d) minor processing, simple assembly, last production; 102.17 non-qualifying operations; name the deciding rule. Same answer schema.
- Server: `USMCA_ENGINE=on` (default off) loads `backend/data/labels_usmca.jsonl` (`USMCA_LABELS`) with facts from `CORPUS_FACTS_DIR` as its own BM25 pool and predicts `usmca_part102` cases with `usmca-v1`; triage reasons start "USMCA / Part 102 rules (19 CFR 102.11)". `USMCA_FAST_PATH=0` (default) sends every USMCA case to review. Clarify is skipped, as for textile. If the engine fails the case stays out of scope. Most USMCA precedents (2015–2022) are not in `labels.jsonl`, so with the flag on the API looks precedents up in the main pool, then the USMCA pool (`httpapi.PrecedentStores`): `precedents_detail`, the ruling view's held origin, watch's newer rulings and the Ask context keep date, subject and origin. Predictions are cached like the others (the key includes the prompt version). Off by default, so merging changes nothing in production.
- Eval: `go run ./cmd/eval run -regime usmca -split dev` (defaults to `labels_usmca.jsonl` and `splits_usmca.json`; pass `-facts data/facts` locally, where the new facts live). **Dev, LiteLLM (proxy, development only), 17 Oct:** 54/72 = 75.0% (95% CI 63.9–83.6); confidence ≥ 0.95 is 36/37 = 97.3% (86.2–99.5) on 51% of cases; $0.0099 promo per case; p50 6.8 s. The substantial-transformation prompt on the same cases and pool: 52/72 = 72.2% (61.0–81.2); fixed 6, broke 4, McNemar p = 0.75, so the prompt's gain is not shown. Always-Mexico 38.9%. Error analysis in `eval/RESULTS.md`: 3 of 18 misses are label problems (two reused manual rows hold the Section 301 origin, not the marking origin; one regex row took a facts sentence); 12 credit the country of a main component where CBP found the shift met in Mexico or Canada.
- Not done: no Vertex run and no held-out run (32 cases; run once with the frozen prompt before setting `USMCA_FAST_PATH`); the three bad labels are left as labelled (fixing only dev misses would bias dev); no spot-check of the 291 new regex labels beyond reading a sample of 14; the router's `usmca_part102` recall on these rulings is unmeasured; precedents show no CBP reasoning (the textile-v2 lever); 102.20 rule text is not looked up per heading; the frontend and the Accuracy page do not show this regime.

**USMCA held-out, 17 Oct 2026** (LiteLLM proxy, the same `gemini-4-argon` as Vertex; Anas: proxy accuracy matches Vertex). Scored once with `usmca-v1`, K and the split unchanged: 25/32 = 78.1% (95% CI 61.2–89.0). Confidence ≥ 0.95: 15/15 = 100% (79.6–100) on 47% of cases; ≥ 0.90: 21/23 = 91.3%. All seven misses are at 0.92 or below; five credit China, Taiwan, the United States or Mexico where CBP found the shift in Canada or Mexico. Proposed `USMCA_FAST_PATH=0.95` (the lowest bar with ≥ 95% held-out accuracy); with n = 15 its lower bound is 79.6%, so review-all (0) is the cautious choice. Rows: `eval/results/usmca-final-gemini-4-argon-prec-usmca-v1-litellm.jsonl`.

**Production, 17 Oct.** Approved by Anas. `USMCA_ENGINE=on` and `USMCA_FAST_PATH=0.95`. The bar uses the same rule as the main engine's 0.92 (D32): the lowest confidence where held-out accuracy stays at or above 95%. Held-out at 0.95: 15/15 = 100% (79.6–100), auto-accepting 47% of cases. At 0.92 it is 18/19 = 94.7%, and nothing scores between 0.92 and 0.95, so 0.95 is the lowest bar that holds.

## D62 — Pharmaceutical (active ingredient) regime, off until evaluated (17 Oct)
- Cases the router labels `pharma_api` were out of scope (187 labelled rulings have that regime and a primary origin). CBP's practice for drugs: origin is generally where the active pharmaceutical ingredient (API) is made; processing bulk API into measured doses (mixing with excipients, tableting, encapsulating, filling, packaging) does not substantially transform it. The exception CBP states in its own rulings (HQ 563207): two or more different APIs combined into a fixed-combination drug with added medicinal benefit take the origin of the country where they are combined. Of the 96 pharmaceutical rulings dated before the dev window, 83 give the API country and 6 the combining country.
- With `PHARMA_ENGINE=on` those cases get a prediction from the same engine with a pharma prompt (`pharma-v1`: the API rule, the dosage-form operations that never confer origin, the fixed-combination exception, chemical reactions, and formulated pesticides and cosmetics) and precedents drawn only from pharmaceutical rulings dated before the case (`backend/data/pharma_rulings.json`). Triage reasons start with "pharmaceutical rules (active ingredient)". Clarify is skipped, as for textile. If the pharma engine fails, the case stays out of scope as before. Predictions are cached like the other engines (D54); the key includes the prompt version.
- `PHARMA_FAST_PATH=0` (default) sends every pharmaceutical case to expert review. Off by default (`PHARMA_ENGINE=off`), so merging changes nothing in production. `PHARMA_RULINGS` overrides the list path. Both flags are validated in config.
- Data: `eval/splits/build_pharma_split.py` builds `eval/splits/splits_pharma.json` (pool 187; dev = Jul–Dec 2025, 35; final = 2026, 56; frozen 17 Oct) and the server list. Eval: `go run ./cmd/eval run -regime pharma -split dev`; held-out once with `-split final -confirm-final`.
- Measured (dev only, **LiteLLM proxy, development only**, `gemini-4-argon`, K = 5): 33/35 = 94.3% (95% CI 81.4–98.4). Confidence ≥ 0.95: 32/32 = 100% (89.3–100) on 91% of cases. The lowest confidence with ≥ 95% dev accuracy is 0.90 (33/34 = 97.1%); no score falls in (0.90, 0.95). $0.0070 promo per case; p50 6.0 s. Both misses are Dermasil creams held "Malaysia or Vietnam" (predicted Malaysia at 0.90 and 0.85); the router labels both `substantial_transformation`, so they would not reach this engine. The substantial-transformation engine on the same 35 is also 33/35 but with two misses at 0.98 (one is the US-menthol cough drops, which pharma-v1 gets right), so the gain is calibration, not accuracy. Always-India is 13/35. Details in `eval/RESULTS.md`.
- Not done: the held-out 2026 split (56) was not run, and there is no Vertex run; a fast-path bar should come from the held-out result on Vertex, not from 35 proxy dev cases. No precedent-reasoning variant (as textile-v2), no Accuracy-page item, no router change (the router's `pharma_api` label is the only gate), and no frontend change beyond the triage reason text.

**Pharmaceutical held-out, 17 Oct 2026** (LiteLLM proxy, same model). Scored once with `pharma-v1`: 50/56 = 89.3% (95% CI 78.5–95.0). Confidence ≥ 0.95: 49/49 = 100% (92.7–100) on 88% of cases; ≥ 0.90: 50/50. All six misses are at 0.85 or below, so they go to review under any bar ≥ 0.90; four of them are European formulations where the model named the dosage-form country or a supplier. Proposed `PHARMA_FAST_PATH=0.95` (the dev-and-held-out lowest bar with ≥ 95%; 0.90 also holds on held-out). Rows: `eval/results/pharma-final-gemini-4-argon-prec-pharma-v1-litellm.jsonl`.

**Production, 17 Oct.** Approved by Anas. `PHARMA_ENGINE=on` and `PHARMA_FAST_PATH=0.85`. The bar uses the same rule as the main engine's 0.92 (D32): the lowest confidence where held-out accuracy stays at or above 95%. Held-out at 0.85: 50/51 = 98.0% (89.7–99.7), auto-accepting 91% of cases. The next value down (0.65) gives 94.3%. Dev at 0.85 is 33/35 = 94.3%; both dev misses are creams that the router labels substantial_transformation, so they don't reach this engine. Dev and held-out pooled at 0.85: 83/86 = 96.5%.

## D63 — Auto-accept bar by product family: built, measured, left off (17 Oct)
- The auto-accept bar is one global 0.92 (D32), but confidence may mean different things by product: on dev, electronics at ≥ 0.92 is 99.1% right while router subfamily `other` (sets, commodities, scenarios and assemblies the router could not place) is 92.6%. The question was whether a bar per subfamily auto-accepts more cases, or fewer wrong ones, at the same 95% target.
- `go run ./cmd/eval calibrate` (no model calls) joins the Vertex engine rows with router-v2's subfamily (`scope-{dev,final}-router-v2-vertex.jsonl`), applies the scoring adjustments, and fits **on dev only**: per subfamily, the lowest confidence at which in-scope accuracy stays ≥ 95% at every bar that keeps ≥ 20 cases (`-target`, `-min-n`); otherwise the global 0.92. Fitted bars: electronics 0.78, metal_tools_parts 0.78, motorised 0.75, filters 0.95, other 0.96; apparel, battery/solar, furniture and medical have under 20 in-scope dev cases and keep 0.92. The frozen bars were then applied once to held-out. It writes `eval/results/calibration-by-family.{json,md}` and `backend/internal/triage/family_bars.json`, which the server embeds; a test checks the two agree.
- Server: `FAST_PATH_BY_FAMILY=on` (default `off`; `on|off`, validated) makes triage use the router subfamily's bar, falling back to `FAST_PATH_CONFIDENCE` when the subfamily has no fitted bar or the router failed. The triage reason names the bar, e.g. "confidence 0.85 ≥ 0.78 (electronics family bar) and in scope" or "… (global bar; no medical family bar)". Off, the reason and the decision are exactly as before. Textile triage (D51) is unchanged.
- **Measured (Vertex result files, official runs; held-out applied once): the family bars are worse, so the flag stays off.** Held-out, n = 377: global 0.92 auto-accepts 300 (79.6%, 75.2–83.3) at 96.0% (93.1–97.7), 12 wrong; family bars auto-accept 287 (76.1%, 71.6–80.2) at 94.1% (90.7–96.3), 17 wrong. Exact McNemar on the cases the policies treat differently: coverage 25 only-family vs 38 only-global, p = 0.130; wrong answers auto-accepted 8 vs 3, p = 0.227. Not significant, but both in the wrong direction, and under the 95% target. On dev, where the bars were fitted, they only reshuffle cases (369 vs 368 auto, 17 vs 13 wrong). The low bars rest on a few low-confidence dev cases each; on held-out the metal_tools_parts bar added 15 cases, 6 of them wrong. Tables in `eval/RESULTS.md`.
- Not done: no smoothing or shrinkage toward the global bar, no bars by route or by regime, no per-family bar for the textile engine, nothing on the Accuracy page, and no new held-out data to test a different rule (the held-out split is now spent on this one). The router's subfamily is a Flash label, not a gold family, so a mislabelled case gets the wrong family's bar. Merging changes nothing in production.
- **Amended by D98 (19 Nov, #289):** the fall-back to the global bar when the router failed applies to choosing a bar for a case whose scope is known. A failed configured router no longer reaches that fall-back for auto-accept; see D98.

## D64 — HTS subheading suggestions from rulings that state one, off by default (17 Oct)
- Problem: the duty table (D52) fills rates only for a product with an HTS subheading, and most users do not know theirs. Many corpus rulings are "tariff classification and country of origin" rulings whose text names the subheading CBP applied, so the corpus can point to likely candidates.
