- `go run ./cmd/corpus hts-index` (from `backend/`, needs the raw corpus) writes `backend/data/hts_index.json`: every labelled ruling whose text states a product subheading, with ID, date, subject and the subheadings normalised to 8 digits. The subheading comes from the `TARIFF NO.:` header (605 rulings), else the last HOLDING section (16), else "the applicable subheading … is NNNN.NN.NN" (194). Chapters 98 and 99 (9802 assembly provisions, Section 301 headings) are skipped. Spot checks of 14 entries against the ruling text found none wrong.
- `POST /v1/products/hts-suggest {facts}` or `{product}`: BM25 (internal/retrieval, over subject + facts) takes the 10 indexed rulings most like the product, groups them by subheading, and returns the 3 with the highest summed score. Each candidate lists its supporting rulings (ID, date, subject, link), whether `GET /v1/duty-rates` has sourced rates for it, and a one- or two-sentence reason from one router-model call (`hts-suggest-v1`). The JSON schema allows only the candidates' subheadings and rulings; code also drops any citation not retrieved for that candidate and any subheading it did not offer. If the call fails, the candidates come back without reasons (`reasons_unavailable: true`). Every response carries "suggestion only — confirm the classification with a licensed broker". Rate-limited as light, and counts toward the daily spend cap.
- Turn on with `HTS_SUGGEST=on` (`HTS_INDEX`, default `data/hts_index.json`, ships in the image with the facts). Off (default) the route is not registered, so it returns 404 and production does not change.
- Measured: the index covers 815 of 2,790 rulings (31 HQ, 784 NY; 2023-01 to 2026-09) and 449 distinct subheadings in 68 chapters. Coverage is uneven: chapter 71 (jewellery, mostly 9802 rulings) has 169 subheading mentions, chapter 84 has 114. Neither subheading in the duty table (8419.89.95, 9015.80.20) appears in the corpus, so `duty_rates_available` is false for nearly every suggestion today. Tests use a fake model. There is no accuracy eval: no live model run and no held-out check of whether the right subheading comes first.
- Not done: no frontend (the product form could call this and pre-fill `product.hts`); no accuracy eval (an obvious one: hide each indexed ruling and see if its own subheading is in the top 3); no date filter (a suggestion may cite a ruling CBP later revoked or modified); no check that a subheading is still valid in the current HTS revision; the 10-digit statistical suffix is dropped; rulings that state a subheading only in other wording are not indexed (192 more contain an HTS-like number somewhere, some only 98/99 headings).

## D65 — Live MFN rates for HTS lines outside the sourced table (17 Oct)
- The duty table (D52) covers two researched lines (8419.89.95, 9015.80.20). Any other product with an `hts` got no rates, so its plan showed no duty effect until the user typed every rate in.
- With `DUTY_LIVE_MFN=on`, a subheading the table does not have gets its MFN (column 1 general) rate from the USITC HTS REST search (`https://hts.usitc.gov/reststop/search?keyword=<NNNN.NN.NN>`, inspected 17 Oct: a JSON array of rows with `htsno`, `description`, `general`). The search is fuzzy (0402.21.25 also returns 2106.90.03), so only rows with the same 8 digits count. The rate is on the 8-digit row, or on its only 10-digit row (8471.30.01.00); statistical lines with different rates, or no rate printed, give no rate. Only plain ad valorem rates are parsed ("4.2%" = 0.042, "Free" = 0). Specific and compound rates ("4.4¢/kg", "20.7¢/kg + 7.5%") come back as printed with `supported: false` and are never converted.
- The table always wins. A live rate never includes Section 301, forced-labor or 232 duties, or special (FTA/GSP) rates; it is marked `mfn_only` with its source URL and `fetched_at`, and every note says "additional duties (Section 301, forced-labor, 232) not included — check before relying on the total". No 301 rate is guessed. Column 2 origins (Cuba, North Korea, Russia, Belarus) are never filled. Nor is the United States (or Puerto Rico, inside the US customs territory): a US plant, US current origin or reshoring target to the US is not imported, so giving it the MFN import rate would put a false duty into the plan's duty effect, net and savings. It stays unpriced unless the user gives a rate, and on its own never triggers an HTS fetch.
- `GET /v1/duty-rates?hts=` answers such a line as `{hts, description, live_mfn}`. A fetch (not a cached answer) counts against the per-client lookup limit (D58), so enumerating subheadings cannot get our user agent blocked. Plan, scenarios and compare-targets fill the MFN rate for the case's countries the user gave no rate for (plants, components, current and predicted origin, target or scenario origins) and list them in `economics.duty_rates_live_mfn`. They ask the HTS only when an origin is missing a rate. Like table rates, live rates are not stored on the case. `economics_priced` on GET case uses only rates already cached, so viewing a case never waits on the HTS.
- One client, 5 s timeout per fetch (`DUTY_LIVE_MFN_TIMEOUT`, max 30 s), our CROSS user agent, at most 4 MB read. Answers and missing lines are cached in memory for `DUTY_LIVE_MFN_TTL` (default 24 h). A failed fetch is cached for 1 minute, so an outage does not cost every plan search a timeout. A request the caller cancelled is not cached. Failure means no rate (404 on the endpoint, rates left as given in a plan), never an error. At most 2,000 subheadings are cached.
- Measured (live, 17 Oct): the HTS gives 8419.89.95 as 4.2%, the same MFN component as `rates.json`. 6505.00.90 comes back as unsupported compound "20.7¢/kg + 7.5%". Run locally on port 8205 (fake model, `DUTY_LIVE_MFN=on`), a heat-gun case relabelled 8516.79.00 got China at 2.7% from the HTS, and the user's Thailand 20% was kept. The note cited the source. Tests use an httptest server with copies of real answers; `DUTY_LIVE_SMOKE=1 go test ./internal/duty -run TestLiveSmoke` queries the real site. No accuracy number applies: this is a lookup, not a prediction.
- Not done: no Firestore cache (each instance fetches once a day per subheading); no special-program rates (FTA/GSP) even when the origin qualifies; no Section 301 / 232 / forced-labor lookup; no `exportList` endpoint (the search covers single lines); no frontend change (the existing `duty_rates_note` display shows the MFN-only warning, but the UI does not yet mark live rates separately); the 8-digit "Free" or ad valorem rate is assumed to apply to every statistical suffix below it, which is how the HTS prints it.

## D66 — Ruling-request letter laid out on 19 CFR 177.2, off by default (17 Oct)
- The Draft page's export is a short generic letter (facts, issue, position, rulings). A broker filing it still has to work out what 19 C.F.R. § 177.2 requires and what CBP's eRulings page lists as mandatory (parties, port, transaction, the not-pending statement, prior advice), so most of the filing work is left undone.
- `POST /v1/cases/{id}/export` takes `"format": "cfr177"` (the default stays `markdown`, unchanged byte for byte). `internal/export.CFR177` writes a letter to the National Commodity Specialist Division (address from 177.2(a)) in eleven sections: interested parties (one manufacturer line per plant, with MID), port of entry, the transaction (prospective; the ruled current setup and the proposed change), the merchandise (name, description, HTS for reference only, proposed marking), materials and processing (each component's country and each operation's plant from the structured product, with a plan's move applied, then the facts as rendered), the question (origin for marking and Section 301, under substantial transformation, or 19 C.F.R. § 102.21 for cases the router labelled textile), position with the earlier rulings cited as in the Markdown draft (177.2(b)(6)), prior or current consideration (the 177.2(b)(5) statement, prior CBP advice, series of transactions), documents and samples (177.2(b)(3)–(4)), confidential treatment (no request, or a bracketed 177.2(b)(7) request with the 177.8(a)(3) withdrawal window), and the declaration and signature (177.1(c), 177.2(c)). Whatever the case does not know is a `[FILL IN: …]`. Preview and the pinned-case rule are the same as for Markdown (D56, D57); a recorded export's history line names the format.
- Turn on with `EXPORT_CFR177=on`. Off (default), `format: cfr177` is a 400 and the export is as before, so merging changes nothing in production.
- Tested: golden files for both formats on four inputs (`internal/export/testdata`; the Markdown goldens were written before the citation list moved into a shared helper, so the refactor is checked byte for byte), a section-by-section content test, HTTP table tests (flag off, plan by index, below-bar plan by move key, textile case, unknown format, preview and the pinned case write nothing), and the config test. No model calls, no eval: this is a template.
- Not done: the Draft page has no format choice (it cannot tell whether the server has the flag on, and its requester form fills the Markdown placeholders, not `[FILL IN: …]`); no Word/PDF output; no eRulings form-field mapping; the letter has not been reviewed by a broker or counsel; the 177.2 text was read from eCFR (2025-01-01 version) and CBP's eRulings requirements page on 17 Oct.

## D67 — Supplier change alerts from a newer BOM, off until turned on (17 Oct)
- A case's prediction is made from the BOM as it was. When a supplier changes (the motor now comes from Thailand, a part is dropped, final assembly moves), the user had to start a new case and compare origins by eye, and nothing said which change mattered.
- `POST /v1/cases/{id}/bom-version` takes a newer BOM for an existing case (multipart `file` or JSON `csv`, read with the same `ReadRows` limits as `/v1/products/import`, D57) and parses it with the case's family template. The product name, description and HTS stay the case's, and plants with the same name keep the case's plant IDs, so economics and the current origin still point at the same plants. `bom.Diff` lists what can change the origin: components added or removed, a component made in another country or at another plant, operations added, removed or moved (matched by name, case and spacing ignored; groups and capabilities are not compared). If anything changed, the new product is rendered with the template and predicted like a scenario (router, engine, triage, one engine call); with no change there is no model call. The response is `{changes, old_origin, new_origin, new_confidence, origin_changed, triage, prediction, repredicted, applied, facts_basis, case_origin}`.
- The alert compares two BOMs, not two wordings. A case made from facts plus a product (the pinned heat-gun demo, a drafted description, or a case with clarify answers) was predicted from text other than the rendered BOM, and the wording alone can move the origin (D16: the heat gun is Thailand on rendered facts, China on CBP's). Then the old product is also rendered and predicted (a second engine call, run alongside, as the plan search's baseline), `old_origin` and `origin_changed` come from rendered old against rendered new, and `facts_basis` is `rendered`; `case_origin` is still the case's own prediction. When the case's facts are already the rendered product, `facts_basis` is `case` and the case's prediction is the "before".
- Nothing is saved unless `apply` is true. Then the case takes the new product, facts and prediction (with the clarify review, as predict), records a `bom_updated` history event with its usage, and drops what was worked out from the old product or facts: plan, target comparison, watch, draft, clarify answers and the current origin (CBP's ruling, e.g. China N328954, was on the old BOM; kept, a plan search would tell the verifier CBP had ruled on this product (D17), scenarios would start from it and the draft would cite it). The event names the dropped ruling and, when the case's own wording is replaced by rendered facts, says so. The history keeps the earlier events; scenarios stay. `apply` on a pinned demo needs the admin token (403, D57); another visitor's case is a 404 with ownership on (D58). Rate-limited light.
- Turn on with `BOM_VERSIONS=on` (default `off`: the route is not registered, so merging changes nothing in production). The frontend has no screen for it yet.
- Measured: unit tests only, with the fake model. The heat-gun sample BOM (`backend/testdata/heatgun_bom.csv`) against the stored heat-gun product reports no change; with the motor moved to the Rayong plant it reports one `country_changed` (China to Thailand, China suppliers to Rayong plant) and makes one engine call on the rendered new product with the case's date and exclusions. Also tested: the demo case made from CBP's facts with a fake engine that reads rendered facts as Thailand, where dropping the knob is `origin_changed: false` (rendered old and new both Thailand; two engine calls) although the case says China; apply drops CBP's ruling and a later plan search gets no current origin; another plant in the same country, a part dropped and added, final assembly moved to Vietnam, demo and ownership rules, the rate limit. A local smoke run on the fake provider gave the same diff and a `bom_updated` event. No live model run: the change is in the facts the engine already reads, and no accuracy claim is made.
- Not done: no frontend (upload button, diff table, alert banner); no stored BOM versions or diff history beyond the history event; no automatic alert when a supplier file changes (the user uploads); components are matched by name only, so a renamed part shows as removed and added; group and capability changes are not reported; the economics are not re-priced for the new product (a plan search or scenarios call does that); applying a BOM to a case made from CBP's wording replaces that wording with the rendered summary (the old text is not kept; start a new case to keep both); the current origin is dropped even when the change cannot matter to CBP's holding (deciding that needs a person).

## D68 — Sign-in and team workspaces, backend half (17 Oct)
- **Problem:** visitors are anonymous (D58: a hashed `X-Visitor-ID` per browser), so a trade-compliance team cannot share cases, and a user loses their cases on another device. History does not say who took a step.
- **What it does:** with `TEAMS=on`, the frontend proxy forwards the user's Google ID token as `X-User-Token`. The backend verifies it with `idtoken.Validate` (signature against Google's keys, expiry, audience `TEAMS_AUDIENCE`, issuer `accounts.google.com`, `email_verified`) behind a `teams.Verifier` interface, so tests use a fake. A bad or expired token is 401 `unauthenticated` everywhere (not a silent fall-back to anonymous, which would file the user's cases outside their workspace). Workspaces (`id`, `name`, `members`, `invited`, `created_by`) live in Firestore next to the cases (`workspaces`, plus `workspace_members/<email>` so a user is in at most one workspace and the lookup is one read), in memory locally. Endpoints: `POST /v1/workspaces` (the caller becomes admin), `GET /v1/workspaces/mine` (workspace plus pending invitations), `POST /v1/workspaces/{id}/members` (admin only; 404 for a workspace the caller is not in), `POST /v1/workspaces/{id}/join`. Adding a member is an invitation (an `invited` list on the workspace document; no `workspace_members` document): the invitee is not a member, and their cases stay personal, until they sign in and call `join` with their own token, which in one transaction checks the invitation and that they are in no workspace. So an admin cannot pull someone into a workspace, read the cases they create, or pre-claim a company's addresses to stop its real team forming (review finding: before this, adding an email made it a member at once, and the victim could neither leave nor create their own). Inviting does not check whether the email is already in another workspace, so the answer does not reveal it; joining a second workspace is 409. Members plus invitations are capped at 50. A signed-in user's case stores a hash of their email as `owner` and their workspace ID as `workspace` (a Firestore field, queried so workspace cases are never pushed out of the 200-case list cap). A signed-in user sees the pinned demos, their own cases, their workspace's cases and the anonymous cases from the same browser; everyone else gets 404, anonymous visitors included, whatever `CASE_OWNERSHIP` says. History events record `actor` (the email). Anonymous requests keep D58 exactly.
- **Turn it on:** create an OAuth client ID (Web) in the GCP project, set `TEAMS=on TEAMS_AUDIENCE=<client id>.apps.googleusercontent.com` (config refuses `TEAMS=on` without one: an empty audience would accept any app's Google token), then ship the frontend half (`docs/issues/frontend-teams.md`). With `TEAMS=off` (default) the routes are not registered, `X-User-Token` is ignored and nothing changes.
- **Measured:** nothing model-related (no model calls). Tests: verifier claims table (issuer, audience, unverified email, malformed token), workspace store rules, and invite and join rules (invitation is not membership, uninvited join 404, joining a second workspace 409, invitations count toward the cap), an invited-but-not-joined victim whose cases stay hidden from the inviter and who can still start their own workspace, cases from before joining staying personal, and a visibility matrix of 9 viewers × 8 cases (list and GET) with `CASE_OWNERSHIP` off and on, outsiders 404 on every case endpoint, actors in history, teams off unchanged. Smoke-tested locally on :8208 with the fake model: startup, 401 for a token with the wrong audience, anonymous list 200.
- **Not done:** the frontend (sign-in button, forwarding the token); declining or revoking an invitation (a stale one is harmless: it grants nothing until accepted), removing members, leaving or deleting a workspace, renaming it, transferring admin, invitations by link; moving existing anonymous cases into a workspace; a user in several workspaces; per-user rate limits (still per IP). The Firestore workspace store is covered by the emulator test only, which was not run here (no gcloud on this laptop). An email removed from a Google Workspace domain keeps access until removed from the workspace (not possible yet).

## D69 — API keys with daily quotas for brokers (17 Oct)
- Brokers want to call the API from their own systems, but the API only knew browser visitors: limits keyed on `X-Client-IP`, cases owned by the frontend's `X-Visitor-ID` (D58). A broker's server has one address shared by many users, no visitor cookie, and no way to be stopped short of blocking the address.
- **Keys.** `POST /v1/admin/api-keys {name, daily_quota}` (admin token) returns a key once: `op_` plus 32 random bytes in base64url. Only its SHA-256 is stored, with name, `created_at`, `daily_quota` and `disabled`; the key ID is the first 16 hex characters of the hash, so a check is one read by ID and a constant-time comparison of the full hash. `GET` lists keys without secrets; `DELETE /v1/admin/api-keys/{id}` disables a key for good (it stays in the list). Stored in Firestore (`api_keys`; per-day counts in `api_key_usage/<id>_<date>`, transactional increment shared by all instances) when cases are there, else in memory.
- **Requests with `X-API-Key`.** Every `/v1` request with the header is checked first: unknown, malformed or disabled is 401 `unauthorized` (never a silent fall back to anonymous). Each request counts against the key's quota for the UTC day; over it, 429 `quota_exceeded` with `Retry-After` at the next UTC midnight. A key gets its own light/heavy/lookup buckets instead of the `X-Client-IP` ones (same sizes as a visitor's), and still takes from the global bucket and the daily spend cap (D59): the cap refuses keys like everyone else. Cases created with a key store a hash of the key ID as owner (the D58 field, prefixed `key_`); a key sees the pinned demos and its own cases only, and its cases are a 404 for visitors and other keys even with `CASE_OWNERSHIP` off. The admin token still sees everything. Requests without the header behave exactly as before.
- **Turn on:** `API_KEYS=on` (default off; needs `ADMIN_TOKEN`, and `CASES_STORE=firestore` on Cloud Run so keys survive restarts and are shared). Off, the header is ignored and the admin endpoints are 404, so merging changes nothing in production. Documented in `openapi.yaml` (securitySchemes `ApiKey` and `AdminToken`).
- **Measured:** tests only (`internal/apikey`, `internal/httpapi/apikeys_test.go`: issue/list/disable, 401, quota and reset at UTC midnight, per-key buckets that follow the key across addresses, spend cap, ownership both ways with ownership off; config validation). A local run on port 8209 with the fake model issued a key and got 200, 200, 429 `quota_exceeded` with `Retry-After`, and 401 for a bad key. The Firestore store has an emulator test that skips without `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`; it was not run here (no emulator on this laptop).
- **Not done:** the backend is private on Cloud Run (only the frontend may invoke it), so brokers cannot reach it yet: the frontend proxy does not forward `X-API-Key`, and a public route (or opening the backend with its own ingress rules) is a separate decision. Quotas count every request including reads and 4xx answers, and a request is counted before its rate limit is checked; there are no per-key rate sizes, no key expiry or rotation, no usage endpoint, and no UI for keys. If the usage counter is unavailable the request is served (and logged) rather than refused. A disabled key's cases stay owned by it, visible to the admin only.

## D70 — Rate limits shared across instances, off by default (17 Oct)
- **Changed by D137 (21 Nov):** on Cloud Run an unset `RATE_LIMIT_STORE` now means `firestore`.
- The rate limits (D45, D57, D58) are token buckets in each instance's memory, so with up to 3 Cloud Run instances a client gets about 3× its limit and the global cap is about 3× too (D57, D59). The spend cap (D59) bounds the bill but not how fast one client can use it.
- `RATE_LIMIT_STORE=firestore` counts the same classes and limits (light 20, heavy 4, global 120 in light units with heavy = 5 and a batch = 20 and the whole heavy burst, lookups 30 outside the global cap) in fixed one-minute windows shared by every instance. Each counter is a document in `rate_limits` (`<sha256 of client key, 24 hex>-<class>-<window start>`, and `global-<window start>`) with `n` and `expires_at` (the window's end). One request is one Firestore Commit that increments its client counter and the global counter together and returns both new totals (the low-level API, since only a raw Commit returns increment results). Over a limit, the request takes its increments back with a second write, so a refusal uses nothing, and gets 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After` until the window ends. A counter seen full is refused without a store write for up to 10 s. Same admin bypass, same `X-Client-IP` keying with IPv6 grouped by /64; addresses are stored only as hashes. The Commit has a 1 s timeout and no automatic retries (a retried increment could count a request twice).
- Fail open: if the store errors, the request falls back to this instance's buckets (the current limiter, still limited) and a warning is logged at most once a minute per instance.
- How to turn it on: `RATE_LIMIT_STORE=firestore` (needs `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`; uses `FIRESTORE_DATABASE`). `local` (default) keeps today's per-instance buckets, so merging changes nothing in production. `memory` runs the shared limiter in one process, for trying it locally. Before turning it on, add a Firestore TTL policy on `rate_limits.expires_at` so ended windows are deleted (`gcloud firestore fields ttls update expires_at --collection-group=rate_limits --enable-ttl`); without it the documents stay (a few hundred bytes each).
- Measured: unit tests only. Two limiters on one in-memory store enforce one combined limit for each class (light, heavy, lookup, global in heavy units, batch); a refused heavy request leaves the global count unchanged; window rollover; a full client costs no store write; two HTTP servers on one store share a client's limit with 429 and Retry-After; with the store failing, requests fall back to the local limit (201, 201, 429) and the outage is logged once. The Firestore path is tested against a fake gRPC Firestore server (one Commit per request, mask `expires_at` only, increment on `n`, no retry on error); an emulator test (`FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`) is written but was not run here (no emulator on this laptop). Smoke-tested locally on port 8210 with `RATE_LIMIT_STORE=memory`: third request in the minute returned 429 with `Retry-After`. Latency on real Firestore was not measured.
- Not done: no staging run on Firestore, no TTL policy created, no `deploy.yml` change. A fixed window allows up to twice a limit across a minute boundary, which the token buckets did not; a sliding window would cost a second read. Every limited request writes the single `global-<minute>` document, which Firestore sustains at about one write a second per document before contention; at the configured 120 light units a minute that is within reach, but a sharded global counter would be needed for much more traffic. The spend meter (D59) still reads its total on every request; that read was not merged into this write.

## D71 — Predict streams its steps (17 Oct)
- `POST /v1/cases/predict` takes 5–15 s against Vertex, and the product step showed only a spinner. With the flag on, a request with `Accept: text/event-stream` gets server-sent events: `stage` {name: router|precedents|engine|clarify, status: started|done, ms} as each step starts and finishes (`ms` is time since the request began for `started` and the step's duration for `done`), then `done` with exactly the 201 JSON body (the case is saved), or `error` {code, message, retryable} with no case saved. Keep-alive comments every 10 s and the same error shape as the plan stream. Input errors, rate limits and the spend cap stay plain JSON before the stream opens. There is no token-level streaming: the engine returns constrained JSON, so stage events are all there is to show.
- How: `analyse()` and `classify()` take an optional stage reporter (nil on every JSON path, including answers, scenarios and batch). The router is reported from its own goroutine; the engine reports `precedents` and `engine` through a hook in the context (`engine.WithProgress`), since it sits behind the `Predictor` interface and the prediction cache. A cached prediction reports `engine` started and done at once, with no `precedents`. The reporter only observes: a test checks the engine's result is identical with and without it. The plan stream and the predict stream now share one SSE writer (`stream.go`) that keeps D55's fix: the handler stops the keep-alive goroutine and waits for it before returning, and writes after that are dropped.
- Frontend: the product step asks for the stream and lists the steps as they run (running steps pulse, finished ones show their time; `aria-live`). A backend with the flag off answers JSON and the page behaves as before, with only the spinner.
- On: `STREAM_PREDICT=on` (default `off`; validated in config). `deploy.yml` is unchanged, so merging changes nothing in production. The frontend proxy already passes `Accept` and streams `text/event-stream` responses (it was built for the plan stream).
- Measured: tests only (fakes). SSE event order under `-race` for the fast path and for a case below the bar that goes to clarify, with the router running beside the engine; keep-alive comments arrive; the error path (`model_unavailable`, retryable, engine reports no `done`, no case saved); input errors stay JSON; flag off answers 201 JSON even with the header. Checked by hand against a local server (port 8211, fake provider). No latency numbers against Vertex.
- Not done: no streaming on `POST /v1/cases/{id}/answers`, scenarios or batch (they still pass nil); the textile engine's second `precedents`/`engine` run is not given its own stage name; nothing cancels the analysis when the browser leaves mid-stream beyond the request context; the UI was not screenshotted with `npm run ui-check` against a streaming backend.

## D72 — Regression slice in CI; prompt snapshot on every PR (17 Oct)
- A prompt or engine change could silently drop accuracy: nothing ties a prompt's text to the version recorded in every result row, and the only accuracy check was a manual 469-case dev run.
- **Offline, every PR** (no flag, no model): `internal/promptsnap` hashes every prompt the server sends to a model with its version constant into `backend/internal/promptsnap/snapshot.json`. Covered: engine `System` (with its schema), the rendered engine user message with and without the `CBP REASONING:` lines (the second is what textile-v2 sends in production, recorded under `+reasoning-v1`), `Rules`, `TextileSystem` and `TextileSystem2`, router system (with schema) and user message, clarify system (with schema), review message and answers header, verifier system and task message, case agent system. The extract call (extract-v3) and the two ADK agents are hashed as the first request they really send, captured with a fake model: system instruction (for the agents including the identity line ADK appends), user message, every tool's name, description and argument schema, and the answer schema; so a tool-description edit or an ADK upgrade that changes the request needs a bump too. Tests check each exported renderer (`engine.RenderUserWithReasoning`, `router.RenderUser`, `clarify.RenderReview`) is byte-for-byte what the call sends. Left out on purpose: the eval-only prompts in `cmd/eval` (`learnSystem`, the torn-dev requester, which records `requester-v1` itself), the health check and the `-smoke` call, none of which produces a recorded prediction. `go test ./...` in `ci.yml` fails when a prompt's text changes and its version does not, with a message to bump the version, run the live slice and then `make prompt-snapshot` (`go test ./internal/promptsnap -update`, which refuses while a text changed under the old version). A bumped version with a stale snapshot also fails, so the snapshot is always current.
- **Live slice, not on PRs** (they have no model credentials, and the Workload Identity provider issues tokens only to `main`): `eval slice` runs engine-v1 (BM25 over the bundled corpus, K from config) and the router on the 30 dev rulings in `eval/splits/slice30.json`, and compares with `eval/results/slice30-baseline.json`. It fails when the run has more than `EVAL_SLICE_MAX_DROP` (default 2) fewer correct cases than the baseline, or its accuracy at confidence ≥ 0.95 is under `EVAL_SLICE_BAND_MIN` (default 0.95, also failing when no case reaches 0.95). Failed or missing cases count as wrong. The report lists fixed and broken rulings. The router's scope label is reported, not scored.
- The slice: from the Vertex dev run (`dev-gemini-4-argon-prec-engine-v1-vertex.jsonl`), rows with no error and no scoring adjustment (462), shared across evalkit's confidence bands in proportion (≥ 0.95: 22, 0.90–0.95: 5, 0.80–0.90: 2, < 0.80: 1), within a band the first by FNV-1a of the ruling ID (not by correctness). Dev only; a test checks every ID is in `dev`, none in `final`, and has bundled facts. Baseline = the same run's rows: 27/30 correct, ≥ 0.95 21/22 = 95.5% (Vertex, official dev run; a 30-case slice is a tripwire, not an accuracy number).
- Turn on: `EVAL_SLICE=on` (default off) is required for the live run, which makes about 60 model calls; `eval slice -in FILE` compares an existing result file with no model and no flag. `make eval-slice` locally. `.github/workflows/eval-slice.yml` runs it on `workflow_dispatch` and on pushes to `main` that touch `backend/internal/{engine,router,clarify,verifier}`, authenticating as `deploy.yml` does (same WIF provider), and writes the report to the job summary and the rows as an artifact.
- Measured, no model calls, comparing recorded dev runs with the baseline: the Vertex source run passes (27/30, 21/22); the Argon LiteLLM dev run passes (27/30, 21/22; proxy, development only); Argon with 5 votes passes (26/30, 23/24; proxy); Gemini 3.5 Flash (D44) fails, 27/30 but 27/30 = 90.0% at ≥ 0.95, the overconfidence D44 found. Comparison logic is tested on fixture files (inside the tolerance, one case over it, overconfident, failed and missing cases).
- Not done: the workflow was not run (no dispatch, no push to `main`). `scripts/setup-github-deploy.sh` does not grant `github-deployer` `roles/aiplatform.user` (live IAM was not checked); without it the workflow fails at the first model call. Granting it, or a dedicated eval service account, is a GCP change left to Anas. Because of the WIF condition, a manual dispatch works only from `main`. The slice does not exercise clarify, extract, the verifier, the case agent or the textile engine (their prompts are covered only by the snapshot); a textile slice would need its own baseline. Two-case tolerance on 30 cases catches a large drop, not a 2-point one; the full dev run stays the measure.

## D73 — Tuned Gemini 3.5 Flash as a fallback when Argon fails (16 Oct)
Anas approved the paid tuning job and a fallback engine. It is not in the 17 Oct freeze: nothing here is on `main` or in the production environment until he sees the numbers.

- **Price check, before spending** (docs read 16 Oct 2026). The tunable model ID is `gemini-3.5-flash` (Gemini 3.5 Flash model page: model ID `gemini-3.5-flash`, tuning = supervised fine-tuning, GA). `gemini-4-argon` cannot be tuned (D44). Tuning runs in `us-central1` or `europe-west4`; a tuned model is served only on the `us` or `eu` multi-region, not `global`. Supervised fine-tuning for Gemini 3.5 Flash is $0.01 per 1,000 training tokens ($10 per 1M). Training tokens are the tokens in the training dataset multiplied by the number of epochs (Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform pricing, Model Tuning). From the Argon dev rows (mean 2,836 input + 224 output tokens): 632 train examples × about 3,060 tokens × 3 epochs is about 5.80 million training tokens, about $58. The 67 tune_check examples are the validation file and are not in that product. Building the targets is about $6–$12 at the Argon promotional rate. Together about $70, under the $100 stop. The job is launched only after `eval tunedata` counts the real file.
- **Corrected-example confidence** revises the constant 0.9 noted in D38. That constant could teach one confidence on every correction, which is the calibration problem D44 found in untuned Flash. The eval row records one confidence, and it belongs to the origin the engine named. That is the engine's confidence for CBP's origin only when the engine named CBP's origin, and that path already keeps it. On a corrected example the engine named a different origin, so a confidence for CBP's origin is not available and the target confidence is 0.75.
- **Fallback.** `FALLBACK_ENGINE_MODEL` defaults to `gemini-3.8-flash` on `FALLBACK_ENGINE_LOCATION=global` (the tuned 3.5 Flash endpoint is not the fallback: its dev result is below). `FALLBACK_ENGINE_MODEL=off` disables it. The origin, textile, USMCA and pharmaceutical engines use the wrapper; the router, verifier, clarify and agent keep `LLM_MAX_RETRIES`. The primary is tried `ENGINE_ATTEMPTS` times (default 2 once a fallback exists) and, if the final error is a 429, a 503, a 504 or a deadline, the fallback model is called once with the same request. A 400, a 404 or a bad response is not retried onto the fallback. `trace.model_id` is the fallback id. A fallback prediction never auto-accepts unless `FALLBACK_FAST_PATH` is set from a held-out result. The default is 0, and the reason is `answered by the fallback model (<id>) because the primary engine was unavailable`. Clarify is not run on that answer. The engine cache key includes the model that answered, so a fallback answer is stored under the fallback model and does not block a later Argon success for the same facts. The spend cap wraps this client too.
- **Tuning data, 16 Oct.** Argon targets (`-workers 4`, Vertex `global`): train 632/632, tune_check 67/67. `eval tunedata` wrote 624 train examples (82 corrected to CBP's origin, 8 skipped with no extractable CBP reasoning) and 67 tune_check examples (11 corrected). Vertex `countTokens` on `gemini-3.5-flash` counted 1,724,598 tokens in the training file. At $0.01 per 1,000 tokens and 3 epochs that is 5,173,794 training tokens, **$51.74**, under the $100 stop. The successful target calls recorded $5.45 promo ($4.93 train, $0.52 tune_check); calls that failed before a later success are not in that figure. Job `projects/88903435351/locations/us-central1/tuningJobs/3897104692752154624`, display name `origin-engine-cbp-reasoning-20261016-1811`. It succeeded (`JOB_STATE_SUCCEEDED`, no error) at 2026-10-17T00:29:25Z after starting 2026-10-16T18:12:17Z. The job's own `totalBillableTokenCount` is 1,710,345; times 3 epochs that is 5,131,035 training tokens, **$51.31** at $0.01 per 1,000. The tuned model is `projects/88903435351/locations/us/models/8576401802885332992@1`. The serving endpoint is `projects/88903435351/locations/us/endpoints/2563603719136477184`. Console: https://console.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/tuning/locations/us-central1/tuningJobs/3897104692752154624?project=wassup-hackathon-2026. Last logged step (87, end of epoch 3): training loss 0.7506042718887329, validation loss 0.6046217679977417. Production is unchanged.
- **Dev, 17 Oct.** Same engine-v1, BM25, K = 5, scored at that endpoint with `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=us`. Adjusted accuracy 407/463 = 87.9% (84.6–90.6), against Argon 422/463 = 91.1% (88.2–93.4) and untuned Flash 419/463 = 90.5% (87.5–92.8). McNemar, same adjustments: tuned vs Argon fixed 15, broke 30, p = 0.036; tuned vs untuned Flash fixed 12, broke 24, p = 0.065. Fast path at 0.92, using the Argon router-v2 scope file: 435/463 = 94.0% coverage, accuracy 389/435 = 89.4% (86.2–92.0). The ≥ 0.95 band is 394/440 = 89.5% (86.3–92.1). Cumulative accuracy stays at or above 95% only for confidence ≥ 0.99 (148/150 = 98.7%). Latency p50 6.0 s / p95 17.1 s. Cost about $0.0248 per successful case at 1.5× Gemini 3.5 Flash non-global ($2.475 / $14.85 per 1M); the 457 successful final calls are about $11.34. Six rulings still returned no JSON object and are counted wrong (H344538, N351672, N352670, N355167, N355168, N356144). Rows: `eval/results/dev-gemini-3.5-flash-tuned-prec-engine-v1-vertex.jsonl`.
- **Held-out was not run.** The gate was: within 2 points of Argon, and calibration improved. Adjusted dev is 3.2 points under Argon, and the 0.92 fast path (89.4%) is worse than untuned Flash (92.0%). The final split stays untouched.
- **Base gemini-3.8-flash, dev, 17 Oct.** The tuned model is dropped. Same engine-v1, BM25, K = 5, Vertex `global`, no router call (the scope file is Argon's router-v2). Unadjusted 418/469 = 89.1% (86.0–91.6). Three rulings (N350517, N350963, N354291) first returned HTTP 504 and succeeded on a resume with `LLM_TIMEOUT=120s`. Adjusted, scoring_adjustments.json: 417/463 = 90.1% (87.0–92.5), against Argon 422/463 = 91.1% (88.2–93.4). McNemar: Flash fixed 13, broke 18, p = 0.473. Fast path at 0.92 on that scope file: 406/463 = 87.7% coverage, 378/406 = 93.1% (90.2–95.2). Argon on the same band is 368/463 = 79.5% coverage, 355/368 = 96.5% (94.1–97.9). The ≥ 0.95 band is 384/413 = 93.0% (90.1–95.1). Latency p50 5.5 s / p95 21.6 s. Held-out was not run. Rows: `eval/results/dev-gemini-3.8-flash-prec-engine-v1-vertex.jsonl`.
- **Tuned endpoints deleted, 17 Oct.** Undeployed and deleted so they stop billing. Serving endpoint `projects/88903435351/locations/us/endpoints/2563603719136477184` (deployed model `4998643742660362240`). Checkpoint endpoints `projects/88903435351/locations/us/endpoints/4869446728350171136` (deployed model `553590910445682688`) and `projects/88903435351/locations/us/endpoints/7585117303654580224` (deployed model `4300585800417935360`). The tuned model id `projects/88903435351/locations/us/models/8576401802885332992@1` is not served. `gcloud ai endpoints list --region=us` returned no endpoints after the deletes.
- **Staging deadline check, 17 Oct, then restored.** Revision `origin-proof-api-staging-00016-dhc` set `ENGINE_MODEL=gemini-4-argon`, `FALLBACK_ENGINE_MODEL=gemini-3.8-flash`, `FALLBACK_ENGINE_LOCATION=global`, `FALLBACK_FAST_PATH=0`, `ENGINE_ATTEMPTS=1`, `LLM_TIMEOUT=8s`. The primary returned `vertex: HTTP 504: Deadline expired before operation could complete` after 1 attempt. The stored prediction had `trace.model_id` `gemini-3.8-flash`, route `needs_review`, and reason `answered by the fallback model (gemini-3.8-flash) because the primary engine was unavailable`. The case was deleted. An earlier revision with the same 15s timeout on both clients (`origin-proof-api-staging-00014-wgf`) did call the fallback, and that call also returned 504, which is why the fallback client has its own 60s budget. Staging was then returned to image `sha256:907b8dff0e336d1e932efd1fb488df42f956965201a1518ae015821676323fb2` (revision `origin-proof-api-staging-00017-hqr`) and the previous env: no `ENGINE_MODEL`, no `FALLBACK_*`, no `LLM_TIMEOUT`, no `ENGINE_ATTEMPTS`.
- **Staging check, then restored.** A revision of this branch (`origin-proof-api-staging-00009-6mz`) set `ENGINE_MODEL=gemini-does-not-exist`, `FALLBACK_ENGINE_MODEL` to the endpoint above, `FALLBACK_ENGINE_LOCATION=us`, and `ENGINE_ATTEMPTS=2`. The heat-gun predict returned HTTP 502 in 2.3 s, `{"code":"model_unavailable","message":"the model provider is unavailable; try again"}`. The primary error, logged at 70 ms, was: vertex: HTTP 404: Publisher model `projects/wassup-hackathon-2026/locations/global/publishers/google/models/gemini-does-not-exist` was not found or your project does not have access to it. Ensure you are using a valid model name and that the model is available in the specified region. For more information, see: https… (the client truncates the body at 300 characters). A 404 is not a 429, a 503, a 504, or a deadline, so the wrapper did not call the fallback. No prediction was stored, and there is no triage route. Staging was then returned to the previous image (`sha256:907b8dff0e336d1e932efd1fb488df42f956965201a1518ae015821676323fb2`, revision `origin-proof-api-staging-00010-85p`) and the previous env: no `ENGINE_MODEL`, no `FALLBACK_*`, no `ENGINE_ATTEMPTS`. Production was not changed. `FALLBACK_FAST_PATH` stays 0; this dev run does not set a bar.

## D74 — verifier-v2: counter-ruling search and evidence-tied confidence (17 Oct)
- Issue #150: the landing showed a precedent-supported heat-gun plan while the live demo showed none clearing the 0.92 plan bar. Anas's rule: keep the bar, improve the check. verifier-v1's confidence carried little information: on the dev flip pairs every precedent-supported verdict was right (62/62), yet most were scored 0.75–0.90, so the 0.92 bar hid correct plans.
- **verifier-v2** (`verifier.PromptVersion2`, `VERIFIER_PROMPT=v2`, now the default) keeps v1's method and burden of proof and adds two steps: a required counter-search for a ruling where the same kind of move did *not* change origin, and a confidence scale tied to what was read (0.95–0.98 = same product type, same deciding component or operation, facts match, no counter-ruling on point; down to < 0.60 = nothing relevant). Written from the dev pairs only. The heat-gun flip pair (BA-N328954-N357771, the demo product) is excluded from every flips run, dev and held-out, so v2 was not fitted to the demo.
- **Results** (LiteLLM proxy, same `gemini-4-argon` as Vertex; Anas: proxy results count). Real origin changes the check supports at ≥ 0.92, all of them correct:

| | dev v1 | dev v2 | held-out v1 | held-out v2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unknown-a | 11 of 46 | **16 of 46** | 4 of 13 | **6 of 13** |
| known-a | 14 of 46 | **16 of 46** | 3 of 13 | **5 of 13** |

  **Caveat on "held-out":** v1's burden-of-proof sentence (`FlipFinal`, kept unchanged in v2) was written by the research team after they saw failures on these 2026 pairs (see the comment on `FlipFinal` and the README limitations), so they are not a fully clean test for either version. v2's own additions were written from the pre-2026 pairs only, and v1 and v2 share the contaminated sentence, so the v1→v2 comparison on the 2026 pairs is fair; the absolute numbers are not independent. A clean test needs flip pairs from CBP rulings published after 25 Sep 2026.
  2026 pairs v2, scored once: unknown-a precision 12/12, recall 12/13 = 92% (v1 9/13), specificity 8/8; known-a precision 11/11, recall 11/13 = 85% (v1 10/13), specificity 8/8. No wrong verdict at ≥ 0.92 in any run. Dev known-a has one wrong precedent-supported verdict, at 0.90 (NEG-H323218, a flip CBP itself reversed on reconsideration), below the bar. Cost $0.035–0.045 per check (v1 $0.031–0.042), one more search.
- **Heat gun, one run, then frozen** (LiteLLM, fresh cache, `VERIFIER_SEARCH=cross`; `eval/results/plan-heatgun-2022-verifier-v2-litellm.json`): prediction China 0.85 as before; one plan clears 0.92: **motor-fan assembly + switch, cord and minor parts to Thailand, check 0.92, supported by N347421, no counter-ruling, net $345k/yr**. Motor-fan + PCBA checked 0.90 (N322364), motor-fan alone 0.85 (N325810); the three PCBA/switch-only moves found no supporting ruling. CBP's real 2026 change (N357771) moved the motor-fan assembly, the PCBA and most of the switch-group parts, so the shown plan is a subset of what CBP held decisive.
- The plan bar stays 0.92 (`PLAN_CONFIDENCE`). The cache key now includes the verifier prompt version, so v1 verdicts in Firestore are not reused for v2: production's first plan search per move re-checks once, then caches.

## D75 — verifier-v3 (fixed first search): built, measured, left off (17 Oct)
- Production's single verifier-v2 run on the heat gun (`backend-00038-zw2`) showed no plan over 0.92; the motor-fan + switch/cord move that had checked 0.92 locally came back 0.75 on a different supporting ruling (N345859). The closest move on production is the motor-fan assembly alone, 0.90, N325810. Each run searches its own way and anchors on different rulings, so the same move's verdict swings.
- **verifier-v3** (`VERIFIER_PROMPT=v3`, not the default) runs one deterministic first search for the change (the words variant B adds, then what the product is) before the agent starts, and tells it to start from those results.
- **Measured on the dev flip pairs (known-a, LiteLLM proxy, two runs each, heat-gun pair excluded):** v2's real changes supported at ≥ 0.92 were 16 and 21 of 46 across two runs; v3's 20 and 21. Precision 97% for both (one wrong verdict, below the bar). Run-to-run agreement on the "shown at 0.92" decision: v2 49/54, v3 51/54; mean confidence change between runs 0.031 vs 0.030. Within noise: v3 does not make the check more repeatable.
- **Heat gun, two fresh v3 runs:** no plan cleared 0.92 in either; the moves' checks moved between 0.55 and 0.85 with different supporting rulings each time.
- **Decision:** keep v2 in production (D74) and do not re-roll the demo. The landing states production's result: closest move, motor-fan assembly to Thailand, check 0.90, just under the 0.92 bar, N325810 for, no counter-ruling, which goes to a broker for review. The cache (D50) keeps that verdict for every visitor. Run-to-run variance in the agentic check is a known limitation (README); a fix needs more than prompt changes (for example a majority of several runs, D33, at several times the cost), which is a finals item.

## D77 — A second plan demo with a close precedent: LED tub light (17 Oct)
- The heat gun's closest move checks 0.90 in every run, with or without the seed (D76) or a 3-vote majority. No earlier ruling is close enough for the 0.95 band. The bar stays 0.92 (D74). Instead, the demo now also has a product where a precedent-supported plan clears the bar.
- **How it was picked:** dev flip pairs whose real change cleared 0.92 in all 9 verifier runs (v1/v2/v3, seeded, 3-vote). Of those, the LED tub light is a component move the planner can express, the engine predicts both rulings correctly (dev: N350085 China 0.98, N351143 Thailand 0.99), and the same manufacturer asked about both setups a month apart.
- **The demo** (`tublight-2025`, family template `led_lights`):
  - Current setup: CBP's July 2025 facts, all Chinese parts assembled in Thailand. CBP held China (N350085).
  - Target: Thailand. Planning date 9 Jul 2025, the request date of N351143. Both tub-light rulings are excluded.
  - Three plan runs (LiteLLM proxy, seeded, live CROSS, fresh cache): the engine predicted China 0.98 every time. The same two plans were shown each time:
    - **LED PCBA (SMT) to Thailand:** check 0.94/0.96/0.96, N345899 for, no counter-ruling. This is exactly CBP's real change (N351143).
    - **LED PCBA plus base, harness and seals:** 0.96, N344188.
  - Moving the lens with the PCBA checked 0.90 (below the bar). The moves without the PCBA were predicted China.
  - Rows: `eval/results/plan-tublight-2025-verifier-v2-seed-run{1,2,3}-litellm.json`.
- **Caveats, as for the LiDAR demo:**
  - This pair is in the dev flip pairs that verifier-v2 was written from, so the demo cannot test the verifier.
  - Duty rates are illustrative: no ruling classifies the tub light, so there is no sourced HTS line.
- The heat gun stays the first demo and the landing's story (closest move 0.90, under the bar). The tub light is second, ahead of the LiDAR.

## D78 — Pause between model calls (17 Oct)
- Production's first tub-light plan (`case_100715a11651`, `backend-00043-bvb`) predicted China 0.98, then the three moves the engine called Thailand never got a precedent check: each verifier call died with Vertex HTTP 504 "Deadline expired" after four attempts. The planner runs the baseline and every move together (`PLANNER_CONCURRENCY` 8), so one search puts the engine and several verifier loops on Vertex at once.
- **Decision:** `LLM_CALL_GAP` (default 0; production 8s) makes every model client — engine, flash fallback, verifier — share one gate. The next call starts only after the previous one has returned and the gap has elapsed. Cloud Run's request timeout is 900s so a paced plan search can finish. The bar, the prompt and the heat-gun verdict are unchanged.

## D79 — Pace only after an overload; cap calls in flight (17 Oct)
- D78's gate made every model call in the process run one at a time, 8 s apart. A fresh plan search (a user's cordless-drill BOM, nothing cached) had finished 1 of 6 moves after about 8 minutes, and every other user's search waited in the same queue. Demos were not affected because their verdicts are cached.
- **Change:**
  - The shared gate caps calls in flight (`LLM_MAX_IN_FLIGHT`, production 4) and otherwise runs at full speed.
  - When a call fails because the provider is overloaded (429, 5xx or a deadline; not a 400 or the caller's own cancel), the gate spaces call starts by `LLM_CALL_GAP` (8 s) for `LLM_COOL_DOWN` (60 s), then returns to full speed.
  - Defaults: no cap, gap 0 (off), cool-down 60 s.
- Rough sizing: a 6-move search is about 30 calls. At 4 in flight that is about 1–2 minutes when Vertex is healthy (was 8+ minutes). Under overload it slows to D78's pace, which got the tub-light checks through.

## D76 — A fixed seed on every model call (17 Oct)
- **Why the check picked different rulings run to run.** Temperature 0 is not enough on Gemini. The verifier's first request, captured and sent unchanged 5 times, came back with 5 different sets of searches. Each different search turned up different rulings, so each run anchored on a different supporting ruling (D75). With a fixed seed, the same request returned the same searches 5 of 5 times, and 19 of 20 in a second test.
- **Change.** `llm.Seed` (20261017) is sent as `generationConfig.seed` on every Vertex call: engine, verifier, router, clarify, agent and fallback.
- **Measured** (dev flip pairs, known-a, verifier-v2, LiteLLM proxy with the same seed, two runs; heat-gun pair excluded):

  | | unseeded (D74/D75 runs) | seeded |
  |---|---|---|
  | identical verdict, rulings and confidence | 13 of 54 | **21 of 54** |
  | different supporting ruling or verdict | 10 of 54 | 9 of 54 |
  | real changes shown at ≥ 0.92 | 16 and 21 of 46 | 17 and 18 of 46 |
  | wrong at ≥ 0.92 | 0 | 0 |

  Results: `eval/results/flips-dev-verifier-v2-seed-run{1,2}-known-a-litellm.*`.
- **What the seed does not fix.** Google documents the seed as best effort. A trace of single pairs showed seeded runs that matched turn for turn, then differed at one turn (for example one run with 100 output tokens and the others with 101), and from there the agent searched and read differently. This persists with thinking off. The serving side is not bit-for-bit repeatable, and a multi-turn agent turns a one-token difference into a different ruling. The production guarantee that the same input gets the same answer remains the verdict cache (D31, D50). The seed makes a fresh run more likely to agree with an earlier one.
- Cached verdicts are kept (the cache key does not include the seed), so the demo's stored verdict is not re-rolled.
- **Engine, dev, seeded** (LiteLLM proxy, same engine-v1, BM25, K = 5): 424/469 = 90.4% (87.4–92.8), against unseeded 422/469 on the same proxy. McNemar: fixed 4, broke 2, exact p = 0.69. The ≥ 0.95 band is 325/337 = 96.4% (unseeded 324/338). 9 of 469 answers differ from the unseeded run, about the same as LiteLLM vs Vertex unseeded (7 of 469). Rows: `eval/results/dev-gemini-4-argon-prec-engine-v1-seed-litellm.jsonl`.
- **Heat-gun baseline** (the plan search's prediction from the structured product, not the predict step's CBP text): Thailand 0.72–0.78 both seeded and unseeded, as in every earlier plan run (D74, D75, the Vertex demo seed). This is not a change from the seed. The plan starts from CBP's holding (China, N328954) through `current_origin`.
- **Tub-light verdicts re-checked once with the seed (17 Oct, Anas's call).** Production's tub-light plan on `backend-00044-q8k`, unseeded, checked LED PCBA → Thailand at 0.90 on H304910. Two unseeded proxy runs gave the same 0.90 on H304910. Three seeded proxy runs gave 0.94–0.96 on N345899 (D77). This is an exception to "don't re-roll demo verdicts" (D56), recorded here. Only the tub light's three cached verdicts are deleted and checked once with the seed; the result stands whatever it is. The heat gun's verdicts are not touched.
- **Held-out (2026 flip pairs), seeded, scored once (18 Oct; LiteLLM proxy, verifier-v2; heat-gun pair excluded; the FlipFinal contamination caveat in D74 applies):**

  | | unknown-a seeded | unknown-a unseeded (D74) | known-a seeded | known-a unseeded (D74) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | shown at ≥ 0.92 | 6, all real | 6, all real | 6, all real | 5, all real |
  | wrong at ≥ 0.92 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | precision (any confidence) | 10/12 | 12/12 | 10/10 | 11/11 |
  | recall | 10/13 | 12/13 | 10/13 | 11/13 |
  | specificity | 6/8 | 8/8 | 8/8 | 8/8 |

  - Unknown-a's two wrong precedent-supported verdicts are NEG-N364156 (N354393) and NEG-N363700 (N359371), both at 0.85, below the bar. Both were unsupported at 0.82–0.85 in the unseeded run.
  - Known-a had 1 proxy timeout (BA-N349038-N360390), counted as an error.
  - What the user sees, plans at ≥ 0.92, is unchanged or better, and nothing wrong clears the bar. Below the bar, 21 pairs is too few to tell the seed's effect from run-to-run noise.
  - Rows: `eval/results/flips-final-verifier-v2-seed-{unknown-a,known-a}-litellm.jsonl`.

## D80 — The precedent check's search trail on every verdict (18 Oct)
- The agentic precedent check is the planner's distinctive part, but the API returned only its conclusion: supporting ruling, counter-ruling, confidence. The verifier already recorded its tool calls (`verifier.Result.Steps`) and then dropped them.
- `domain.Verdict.Trail` lists each search (query, plus the ruling numbers it returned) and each ruling read, in the order the agent made them. It is stored with the verdict, so a cached verdict replays its trail. Verdicts cached before this change have no trail; the field is omitted.
- No model, prompt or cache-key change: verdicts and costs are unchanged. The frontend can show "searched …, read N345899, counter-searched …, found nothing against".

## D83 — A panel of three views for checks near the bar, off by default (18 Oct)
- With the seed (D76), repeating the same prompt mostly repeats the same answer, so extra identical runs add little (D76: 3 same-prompt votes, 52/54 runs agreeing, 3× the cost). Anas's idea: give the extra runs different views of how CBP could rule, so they cover more ground.
- **Panel** (`VERIFIER_PANEL=on`, needs `VERIFIER_PROMPT=v2`): when the first check's confidence is in [`VERIFIER_PANEL_LOW`, `VERIFIER_PANEL_HIGH`) (default [0.89, 0.95)), two more checks run in parallel. Both keep v2's method and confidence scale and change only where they look first:
  - **skeptic** (`verifier-v2-skeptic`): argues as the CBP import specialist and searches first for rulings that called the same move minor assembly.
  - **product** (`verifier-v2-product`): searches first by what the finished product is, then by the moved part.
- **Combining:** the panel keeps the majority on precedent support (a tie goes against support), the median confidence of that majority, and the citations of that median run. A counter-ruling found by any view is kept. `agreement` reports e.g. "2/3".
- **Cache key unchanged:** the panel keys under its first checker's version, so turning it on never re-checks stored verdicts (demo verdicts stay, D56). It applies to new checks only.
- **Measured** (dev flip pairs, known-a, seeded, LiteLLM proxy, two runs each, heat-gun pair excluded):

  | | real changes shown at ≥ 0.92 | wrong at ≥ 0.92 | same shown/not-shown run to run | cost per check |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | single check (D76) | 17 / 18 | 0 | 51 / 54 | $0.034 |
  | 3 votes, same prompt | 16 / 18 | 0 | 52 / 54 | $0.105 |
  | **panel of 3 views** | **19 / 18** | **0** | **53 / 54** | **$0.061** |

  Precedent-supported verdicts were all real changes at any confidence (33/33 and 32/32). The first attempt at this measurement hit LiteLLM proxy 403s from too many parallel agents (42 and 37 of 54 rows errored). It was discarded and re-run at 2 workers per run. These results are from that re-run. Rows: `eval/results/flips-dev-verifier-v2-panel-run{1,2}-known-a-litellm.jsonl`.
- Held-out not run. Production stays off until Anas turns it on.

## D81 — Near-miss advice: what would clear the bar (18 Oct)
- A move whose precedent check lands just under the 0.92 plan bar is a dead end on the Plan page: the user sees "0.90, below the bar" and nothing more. The heat gun's closest moves sit there in every run (D74–D77).
- **Change:** for the closest moves rejected as `below_confidence_bar` with check ≥ 0.85, one extra model call compares the move's facts with its supporting ruling, read through the verifier's own searcher (only rulings dated before the case, the case's own rulings excluded).
  - It returns `near_miss {difference, would_match, ruling, trace}`: how the move differs from that ruling on the point CBP relied on, and what change to the setup would make the two match.
  - At most two per search, highest check first.
  - Prompt `near-miss-v1`, written before any measurement and fingerprinted in promptsnap (D72).
  - Cached beside the verdicts (Firestore on Cloud Run, keys prefixed `nearmiss-`).
  - Off by default: `NEAR_MISS_ADVICE=on`.
- **Advice only.** It runs after every verdict is final. It never changes a verdict, the bar or `shown`, and a failed advice call leaves the move without advice. Tests check that verdicts and shown plans are identical with advice on, off and failing.
- **Measured once** (LiteLLM proxy, seeded, verifier-v2, live CROSS, fresh cache; scratch build, 18 Oct):
  - Heat gun: one move in the band, motor-fan assembly + PCBA at 0.90 on N342916. Advice: "Both setups perform final assembly and produce the PCBA and main housing in Thailand, and your setup goes further by making the motor and heating element in Thailand rather than China as in N342916. However, in N342916 ALL plastic parts were injection-molded in Thailand (along with some screws and the switch plate), whereas in your setup plastic parts like the knob, back cover, and support ring, as well as the switch and fasteners, are sourced from China." Would match: "Produce the knob, back cover, support ring, switch, and fasteners in Thailand instead of sourcing them from China." That is close to what CBP's real 2026 change did (N357771 moved the motor, fan, PCBA, knob, power cord, back cover and outer shells).
  - Tub light: LED PCBA + lens at 0.90 on H331515. Advice: in H331515 the harness and end-caps came from the assembly country, while here the harness, lamp base and sealing come from China. Would match: make the harness and lamp base in Thailand. Moving them with the PCBA is the second shown plan (0.96), so the advice points at a plan the search already found.
  - Cost: one call each, about 1,700–1,900 input and 150 output tokens, about $0.005 per search (promo) on top of $0.13–0.15 for the search. Latency 5–7 s, after the verdicts.
  - In this run, the heat gun's motor-fan-alone move (production's closest, 0.90 on N325810) came back unsupported at 0.65, so it got no advice. The production demo case keeps its cached verdicts. Advice for it appears only after `NEAR_MISS_ADVICE=on` and a new search on that case, using its stored verdicts.
  - Rows: `eval/results/plan-{heatgun-2022,tublight-2025}-near-miss-v1-litellm.json`.

## D82 — Clean held-out flip test: no rulings to build it from yet (18 Oct)
- **Goal.** The 2026 flip pairs are partly contaminated: v1's FlipFinal sentence, which v2 keeps, was written after seeing failures on them (D74). A clean test needs CBP rulings published after the corpus cut-off (25 Sep 2026), built into flip pairs with the research pipeline and scored once with the frozen verifier.
- **Pipeline reused, unchanged except the date.** `hackathon-research/experiments/origin-proof/scripts/collect_index.py`, with `CUTOFF = "2026-09-26"`, searched the same terms ("substantial transformation", "country of origin", "origin") in DATE_DESC order, 1 request a second, and found 1 candidate. A second probe (21 searches: "printed circuit board", "Vietnam", "Thailand", "Mexico", "substantial transformation", "country of origin", "classification", "tariff", "ruling", "marking", "China", "manufactured", "assembled", across the ALL/NY/HQ collections) found 2 rulings dated after 25 Sep 2026 in all of CROSS:
  - H335012 (2 Oct 2026): a classification ruling for a colpocamera stand. It only restates the requester's claimed origin (Japan) and holds no origin, so it has no label and no scenarios.
  - H364652 (15 Oct 2026): a coastwise-transport (Carriers) ruling.

  The newest NY ruling CROSS returns is dated 14 Aug 2026. CROSS publishes with a lag, so the newest rulings in the corpus are the newest available.
- **Result: 0 clean flip pairs (0 before/after, 0 multi-scenario, 0 negatives).** Nothing was scored, and no pairs were padded with older rulings. Frozen config for when pairs exist: `main` at `8d23488`, verifier-v2 (`verifier.PromptVersion2`), `llm.Seed` 20261017 (D76), unknown-a and known-a, scored once. Re-run `collect_index.py` with the same cutoff; once origin rulings appear, follow `data/flips/README.md` (candidates → hand read → `flips_items.py` → `flips_build.py`) into `eval/data/flips_clean.jsonl`.
- The README limitation stands: the planner's only held-out numbers are the partly contaminated 2026 pairs (D74, D76).

## D84 — Three-group moves in the plan search (18 Oct)
- The planner listed single and double moves only (`planner.Enumerate`). CBP's real heat-gun change (N357771, Jan 2026) moved the motor, fan, PCBA and the switch/cord parts together, which is three of the template's groups, so the setup CBP approved was never searched.
- **`EnumerateUpTo`:** every single and pair as before, in the same order, then three-group moves while the total stays at most `PLAN_MAX_MOVES` (default 12). When not every triple fits, the triples whose groups cost most where they are made now come first (the request's `group_costs`), ties in template order. Singles and pairs keep their keys and order, so their cached verdicts are reused. `domain.MaxGroupsPerMove` is now 4 (a triple plus at most one group from D85). Describe reads "Make A, B and C in Thailand".
- Heat gun: 6 moves become 7; the new one is motor-fan + PCBA + switch/cord. Tub light: 6 become 7 (LED PCBA + lens + base/harness/seals). LiDAR has two movable groups and is unchanged.
- **Measured once** (LiteLLM proxy, same `gemini-4-argon`, seeded (D76), verifier-v2 with the panel (D83), near-miss advice and FOLLOW_ADVICE on, `-search cross`, planner concurrency 2, fresh cache; not re-run):

  | Heat gun move | Result | Check | Supporting | Panel |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **Motor-fan + PCBA + switch/cord (new triple)** | **shown** | **0.95** | N347421 | — |
  | Motor-fan + PCBA | below the bar | 0.82 | N346720 | 2/3 |
  | Motor-fan alone | below the bar | 0.75 | N347421 | — |
  | Motor-fan + switch/cord | below the bar | 0.75 | N345859 | — |
  | PCBA alone; switch/cord alone; PCBA + switch/cord | no supporting precedent | 0.65 / 0.75 / 0.65 | — | — |

  The triple is CBP's real 2026 change: the backtest says it moved the motor, fan, PCBA, knob, power cord and back cover. Of N357771's moved parts, only the outer shells were not moved (that group, housing moulding, is already Thai in the 2022 setup). Net $285k/yr (duty $500k, added cost $215k, illustrative). Engine Thailand 1.00. 7 moves, 8 engine calls, 7 verifier runs (29 model calls, 54 tool calls), $0.238 promo, 108 s.

  | Tub light move | Result | Check | Supporting | Panel |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | LED PCBA | shown | 0.94 | N344188 | 3/3 |
  | LED PCBA + lens | shown | 0.92 | H304910 | 3/3 |
  | LED PCBA + base, harness and seals | shown | 0.96 | N344188 | — |
  | **LED PCBA + lens + base, harness and seals (new triple)** | **shown** | **0.96** | N323710 | — |
  | Lens, base/harness/seals, or both | engine predicts China | — | — | — |

  7 moves, 4 verifier runs (28 model calls), $0.272 promo, 101 s. LED PCBA + lens moved from 0.90 (production, D77) to 0.92 here; that is this run's panel, not a re-check of production's verdict.
- Rows: `eval/results/plan-{heatgun-2022,tublight-2025}-triples-follow-litellm.json`. Production's stored verdicts are untouched: singles and pairs keep their cache keys, so turning this on only adds the triple's check to a replayed search.

## D85 — Follow near-miss advice with one more move, off by default (18 Oct)
- Near-miss advice (D81) names what would match the supporting ruling, but the planner stopped there. `FOLLOW_ADVICE=on` (needs `NEAR_MISS_ADVICE=on`) closes the loop: after advice, it takes the advised near misses, highest check first, and adds the groups the advice's `would_match` names to that move. If the result is a move not already searched (in the target country, groups the plants can perform), it is run once through the normal engine and precedent check and added with `from_advice: true`. At most one per search. It is a new move, never a re-check: a superset that was already searched is skipped.
- **Mapping:** first deterministic. Group names, component names and pieces of operations are matched as whole words in the advice. Only if none match, one small structured call (`near-miss-map-v1`, ids from the template list only) is the fallback; its tokens count with the advice's.
- The followed move streams like any other (`move_started`/`move_done`, `total` grows by one) and gets economics. A failed check on it shows as `check_failed` like any move.
- **Measured** (same runs as D84): **nothing was followed, because there was no near miss to follow.** The heat gun's closest below-bar move checked 0.82, under near-miss advice's 0.85 floor (D81), so no advice was written. The tub light had no below-bar move. With triples in the search, the advice D81 measured on the heat gun (motor-fan + PCBA at 0.90: also make the knob, back cover, support ring, switch and fasteners in Thailand) maps to motor-fan + PCBA + switch/cord. That triple is now searched directly, so following it would be skipped as already searched. The feature matters when the search is capped below the triples or a product has more groups than the cap covers. Tests cover the mapping, the skip, the one-per-search cap, the fallback, the failure path and that existing verdicts are unchanged.
- Off by default. The near-miss floor and cap are unchanged.

## D86 — Precedent-check calibration on the Accuracy page (18 Oct)
- The Accuracy page showed the engine's calibration but, for the precedent check, only precision, recall and specificity from one dev run. The 0.92 plan bar had no evidence beside it. Every verifier-v2 flip run is already committed, so the calibration needs no model calls.
- `GET /v1/eval/summary` has a new `verifier_calibration` object, built by `eval summary` from every `eval/results/flips-{dev,final}-verifier-v2*-<mode>-<provider>.jsonl`. That covers the first runs, run2, seeded, 3-vote and panel runs, in both known-a and unknown-a modes. verifier-v1 and v3 files are left out. Dev and the 2026 pairs are kept separate.
  - **Bands:** precedent-supported verdicts by confidence (≥ 0.95, 0.92–0.95, 0.85–0.92, < 0.85), with how many were real origin changes and a Wilson 95% CI.
  - **Shown at the bar:** real vs wrong at ≥ 0.92, with precision and recall CIs.
  - **Pair level:** repeated runs of the same pairs inflate pooled n, so each pair is also counted once. A real-change pair is shown-correct only if every run that scored it showed it; a no-change pair is wrong if any run showed it.
  - Rows with a provider error are left out of every count and reported.
  - The proxy note (same gemini-4-argon as Vertex, D74) and the 2026 contamination caveat (D74, D82) come in the payload.
- **Numbers** (LiteLLM proxy; heat-gun pair excluded):

  | | dev (54 pairs, 9 runs, 485 verdicts) | 2026 pairs (21 pairs, 4 runs, 83 verdicts) |
  |---|---|---|
  | supported at ≥ 0.95 | 76/76 real (95.2–100%) | 10/10 (72.3–100%) |
  | supported at 0.92–0.95 | 83/83 (95.6–100%) | 13/13 (77.2–100%) |
  | supported at 0.85–0.92 | 95/97 (92.8–99.4%) | 15/17 (65.7–96.7%) |
  | supported below 0.85 | 31/31 (89.0–100%) | 5/5 (56.6–100%) |
  | shown at 0.92, pooled | 159 real, 0 wrong (precision 97.6–100%) | 23 real, 0 wrong (85.7–100%) |
  | pair level: shown in every run / real-change pairs | 13/46; 11 more shown in some runs; 0 no-change pairs ever shown | 4/13; 4 more in some runs; 0 ever wrong |

- **Reading:**
  - No verdict at or above the bar has ever been wrong: 182 shown verdicts pooled, 17 pairs at pair level.
  - The only wrong precedent-supported verdicts sit in the 0.85–0.92 band, 2 on dev and 2 on the 2026 pairs. That band is exactly where the bar cuts.
  - Below 0.85, support is still right when given, but the check rarely gives it there.
  - The cost of the bar is recall: about 30% of real-change pairs are shown in every run.
- Tests: band edges (0.92 is inclusive, 1.0 is in the top band), Wilson, the pair-level rule, dev/2026 separation, v1/v3 files excluded, errors excluded. `summary.json` regenerated; the 50 existing items are unchanged.

## D87 — Cost and time estimate before a plan search (18 Oct)
- A fresh plan search took 8+ minutes before D79, and the frontend could only say "usually 1–2 minutes". `POST /v1/cases/{id}/plan/estimate` takes the plan body and answers with no model call: moves to search, how many engine predictions and verdicts are already stored, and a range for model calls, cost (USD, promo prices) and wall time.
- **How:** the planner enumerates the same moves and renders the same facts as `Plan`, then reads the caches read-only. It uses `engine.Cache.Peek`, `verifier.Cache.Peek` and `nearmiss.Cache.Has`, the same keys `Predict`, `Verify` and `Advise` use, which now call the same lookup.
  - A stored verdict, or a stored prediction that misses the target, costs nothing.
  - A move with no stored prediction may or may not need a check: from 50% (tub light, 3 of 6 moves reach the target) to 100% (heat gun, 6 of 6).
  - The panel (D83) adds 2 views on 29–42% of checks: the share in [0.89, 0.95) on the dev and 2026 flip pairs.
  - Near-miss advice (D81) adds up to 2 calls.
  - Wall time is the longest chain of one move's calls, or the calls queued through `LLM_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` (D79), whichever is longer.
  - The constants and their sources are in `planner/estimate.go`.
- **Access:** same ownership as the plan route. It takes from the light rate-limit bucket, not the heavy one, so the estimate does not use up the search it is for. The spend cap does not refuse it, because it spends nothing. The route exists only when the planner can estimate.
- **Sanity check against measured fresh searches** (LiteLLM, no panel, no in-flight cap, as those runs used):

  | | estimate | measured |
  |---|---|---|
  | heat gun, cost | $0.10–0.22 | $0.15–0.22 (5 runs: `plan-heatgun-2022-*`) |
  | heat gun, wall | 26–50 s | 30–34 s |
  | tub light, cost | $0.10–0.22 | $0.12–0.14 (4 runs: `plan-tublight-2025-*`) |
  | tub light, wall | 26–50 s | 35–51 s |

  Every measured cost is inside the range. The heat gun's measured costs sit in the top half (every move reached the target). The tub light's sit near the bottom (3 of 6 did). The tub light's slowest run (51 s) was 1 s above the range.
  - With production's settings (panel on, near-miss on, 4 in flight), a fresh 6-move search estimates $0.13–0.38 and 34–132 s.
  - The range assumes a healthy provider; under 429/504 a search can take several times longer (D78, D79), and the response says so.
- **Merged with D84 (18 Oct):** the estimate counts moves with `EnumerateUpTo`, the same three-group-aware list `Plan` searches. With `PLAN_MAX_MOVES` (default 12) it includes the heat gun's triple, so the heat-gun estimate is 7 moves, not 6.

## D88 — A provider outage degrades a plan search instead of failing it; the slice reports outages as inconclusive (18 Oct)
- On 18 Oct, Vertex `global` returned 429s and 504s for hours.
  - A fresh plan search (a cordless-drill BOM) failed twice with HTTP 502, because one move's engine prediction failed, even though 4 of its 5 checks had finished.
  - The CI eval slice scored 11/30 against a baseline of 27: 18 cases got no answer (504 after about 122 s) and were counted as wrong. The 8 confident answers it did get were 8/8 right.
- The engine's 3.8 Flash fallback (D73) did not help. A probe on 18 Oct showed that 3.8 Flash and 3.5 Flash are served only on `global` for this project; `us-central1`, `us-east4` and `europe-west4` return 404. So the fallback shares the primary's outage, and a regional fallback is not possible. Left as is.
- **Plan search:** when a move's engine prediction fails because the provider is unavailable (`llm.Unavailable`: a 429, a 5xx, a timeout or exhausted retries), that move is returned as `check_failed` with "search again to retry this move", with no economics. The other moves are returned as usual. The failed prediction is not cached, so the next search retries only that move. A 400 or a bad response still fails the search. A failed baseline prediction (the current setup) still fails it too, because no move can be priced without it.
- **Eval slice:** when more than `EVAL_SLICE_MAX_ERRORS` cases (default 3) fail or are missing, the accuracy check is not judged and the report says INCONCLUSIVE. The job passes with a warning to re-run. A confidently wrong answer among the cases that did answer still fails it. The slice job now uses production's pacing (`LLM_MAX_IN_FLIGHT=4`, `LLM_CALL_GAP=8s`, `LLM_COOL_DOWN=60s`, D79).

## D89 — CROSS search relaxed and filled from the corpus, so checks see evidence (18 Oct)
- Production's check on the heat gun's three-group move (`backend-00049-8kf`) came back 0.96 that origin changes, with no supporting ruling, so it was not shown. Its trail showed why: 4 of its 6 searches returned nothing.
  - CROSS search returns only rulings that contain every query word. "electric motor stator rotor laminations SMT PCBA Thailand China" has 0 hits, and "heat gun" alone has 92.
  - In the LiteLLM heat-gun run (D84), 26 of 41 searches were empty.
  - The dev flip evals never saw this: they search the local BM25 corpus, which matches partially. So production checks ran with far less evidence than the measurements assumed, which likely added to the run-to-run swings (D75, D76).
- **Change:** `verifier.Relaxed` wraps CROSS search on the server (`VERIFIER_SEARCH=cross`, the default).
  - If the full query returns fewer than 8 hits, it retries up to 4 shorter forms: without country names and search operators, then the two halves, then the first two words.
  - It then fills the remaining slots from the local labelled corpus (BM25, same date bound and exclusions).
  - Hits keep the order found, full query first. Read still goes to CROSS, so the agent reads CBP's full text.
  - If CROSS is down, the corpus still answers. If both fail, it is an error, and the verdict is not cached (D58).
  - The near-miss adviser and the case agent use the same searcher.
- **Measured once** (LiteLLM proxy, seeded, panel and near-miss on, live CROSS, fresh cache; not re-run):
  - Searches: heat gun 34, tub light 11, **0 empty**.
  - **Heat gun:** the three-group move (motor-fan + PCBA + switch/cord → Thailand, CBP's real N357771 change) is **shown at 0.96**, supported by N345938 (hair clipper kit, 6 Mar 2025), no counter-ruling, net $285k/yr. The backtest moved every part CBP's change moved except the outer shells (housing moulding is already Thai). Every other move is below the bar (0.75–0.85) or unsupported.
  - **Tub light:** all four LED-PCBA moves shown, 0.94–0.96.
  - Cost per search $0.27 / $0.23, wall time 136 s / 93 s.
  - Rows: `eval/results/plan-{heatgun-2022,tublight-2025}-relaxed-search-litellm.json`.
- The cache key is unchanged, so stored verdicts stay. Production's three-group verdict for the heat gun (0.96, unsupported) is stored. It changes only if that one verdict is deleted and checked again, which is Anas's call (D56).

## D90 — Section 301 duties for every product, parsed from HTS Chapter 99 (18 Oct)
- Live HTS rates (D65) gave only the MFN rate. For a Chinese-origin product that understates the duty the plan saves: the heat gun's real China–Thailand gap is 25 points (Section 301 List 2), and MFN alone shows 0. The tariff saving is the product's main incentive, so it has to be right beyond the one hand-sourced line (D52).
- **Data** (`backend/internal/duty/additional.json`, built by `scripts/duty301/build.py`), parsed from the official HTS 2026 Rev 21 Chapter 99 PDF:
  - **China Section 301, U.S. note 20:**
    - List 1, 9903.88.01: 856 subheadings
    - List 2, 9903.88.02: 283
    - List 3, 9903.88.03: 5,906
    - List 3's named products, 9903.88.04 (note 20(g)): 11. The task brief called it 20(h); the extractor corrected that.
    - List 4A, 9903.88.15: 2,958, at 7.5%
    - Lists 1–3 are at 25%, read from each heading's note.
    - 10,014 subheadings in all, none on two lists.
  - Each list was extracted twice by independent parsers (a regex over joined text, and line-wise fixed-width chunks) and diffed: 0 disagreements.
