  - **2026 country-wide Section 301** (headings 9903.05.20–.84, 91 FR 47318), 65 headings:
    - +12.5% for 38 countries (China, Thailand, Vietnam among them)
    - +10% for 17 (Malaysia, Mexico, Canada, India among them)
    - a floor of 10% or 12.5% total for the EU, Japan, South Korea, Switzerland and Taiwan (note 52(k)). EU duties apply to each member state.
  - **Note 52 exemptions:**
    - Whole-subheading lists (52(b), and the country lists in 52(j)) are applied.
    - Lists that depend on a use or an entry claim (civil aircraft 52(d), pharmaceutical use 52(e), CAFTA-DR claims in 52(j)(6)(iii) and (7)(iii)) and named articles (52(c), 52(j)(n)(ii)) are not applied. They are named in the note instead.
    - Two parsers agreed on all 34 lists.
  - **Product exclusions** (9903.88.69/.70, 178, until 9 Nov 2026) are all for described products or statistical numbers, so none removes a subheading's duty. When one may apply, it is named in the note.
- **Behaviour:** with live MFN on, each origin's rate is MFN plus its additional duties. `duty_additional` lists them per origin, and the note names the headings and what to check. Section 232 is still not included and is said so. The hand-sourced table (D52) still wins for its line. A test checks that the parsed data reproduces it exactly (heat gun: China 41.7%, Thailand 16.7%, Vietnam 16.7%, Malaysia 14.2%).
- **Caveats:**
  - This is a text-layer extraction, not proofread against the rendered PDF.
  - The HTS text does not say whether note 52(b) is the notice's Annex II Part A.
  - Classification is the user's: a wrong HTS number gives wrong duties.

## D91 — Every built feature on in production (18 Oct)
- **Turned on:**
  - `STREAM_PREDICT` (D71)
  - `HTS_SUGGEST` (D64)
  - `EXPORT_CFR177` (D66)
  - `BOM_VERSIONS` (D67)
  - `FOLLOW_ADVICE` (D85)
  - `API_KEYS` (D69)
  - `TEAMS` (D68)
- **API keys:** the backend is private on Cloud Run, so brokers could not reach it. The frontend proxy now forwards `X-API-Key` unchanged; the backend still checks it.
  - Checked locally through the proxy: a bad key gets 401, a valid key 200, and a browser request without a key is unchanged (200).
  - The Developers page shows the public base URL (`…/api`).
  - Keys need `CASES_STORE=firestore` (already set) and `ADMIN_TOKEN` (the existing secret).
- **Teams** needs a Google OAuth client ID (Web), created in the GCP console with the frontend URL as an authorised JavaScript origin:
  - backend: `TEAMS_AUDIENCE=<client id>`
  - frontend: `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=<client id>`
  - The sign-in button and workspace panel already exist (D68).
- **Still off:** `FAST_PATH_BY_FAMILY` (D63). It measured worse on the held-out split.

## D92 — HTS suggestions search CBP's rulings live, and are measured (18 Oct)
- **Why:** on production (D91) the heat gun's suggestions were 8421.23 (filters), 8456 and 8459 (machine tools); the right code, 8419.89.95, was missing. The local index (D64) holds only the 815 rulings of the origin corpus that state a subheading, all from 2023 on, and none classifies a heat gun. CBP has five that do.
- **Change:**
  - Candidates now come from a live CROSS search, whose hits carry each ruling's tariff numbers:
    - The queries are the product's name words, then the start of the product text, skipping the request letter's boilerplate.
    - Only classification rulings count. Chapter 98/99 numbers are dropped.
    - Each ruling votes once per subheading, weighted by query and rank.
    - A subheading must be in today's HTS: older rulings cite numbers since renumbered, e.g. 8419.89.90 → 8419.89.95.
  - The pool is half CROSS and half the local index (interleaved, up to 6). The model ranks it by fit (`hts-suggest-v2`: "what it is and does, not shared words"), and the top 3 are returned.
  - If CROSS is unreachable, the local index answers alone. The response says which source answered (`source`).
  - The money prefill, which takes the top suggestion to price duties, uses the same suggester.
- **Measured (first accuracy test for D64):**
  - **Setup:** 40 indexed rulings, sampled with a fixed seed. Each ruling was hidden from both the index and CROSS, and its own code had to come back. Same model (gemini-3.8-flash, LiteLLM proxy).

    | | top 1 | top 3 |
    |---|---|---|
    | old (index + model) | 23/40 | 25/40 |
    | **new (CROSS + index + model)** | **29/40** | **33/40** |

  - New fixed 9 and broke 1.
  - A version that put CROSS candidates first scored 26/40 (fixed 9, broke 8): CROSS alone missed jewellery and paper products that the index had.
- **Spot checks (live):**
  - heat gun → 8419.89.95 first, then 8516.79.00 and 8467.29.00
  - LED tub light → 8539.52.00 (LED lamps)
  - cordless drill → 8467.21.00
- **Cost:** up to 4 CROSS searches and a few HTS lookups per suggestion, cached for 24 h. The one model call is unchanged.


## D93 — The landing's figures scroll sideways on one pinned stage (18 Oct)
- **Why:** the three figures read as separate pages stacked down a long scroll, with a bridge wire between fig.1 and fig.2. The owner asked for one continuous journey that ends on the way into the cases.
- **Change (wide screens, over 900 px):**
  - The hero sits above one stage pinned under the AppBar. Its track holds three full-width panes: fig.1 the heat gun, fig.2 how it works, fig.3 the test sheet.
  - Scrolling down moves the track sideways. The page position, in screens past the point where the stage pins, is split into 11: fig.1 plays over 0–4, the track slides over 4–5.5, fig.2 fills over 5.3–7.5, the track slides over 7.5–9, fig.3 settles over 8.9–10.5 and holds until 11.
  - Scrolling stays native and 1:1: the wheel is not intercepted, unlike the scroll stops removed in #203.
  - fig.1's step list is replaced by a caption box in the sheet's lower left, one phase at a time, with the colour legend under it. The figure's label still tells screen readers the whole story.
  - The bridge wire is gone. fig.3's footer ends with "Open the cases →" (to /cases) once the squares settle.
  - A progress line along the stage's bottom edge marks fig.1 · fig.2 · fig.3; each mark jumps to its figure.
  - Tabbing into a pane that is off to the side scrolls the page to it.
- **Unchanged:**
  - Phones (900 px and under) keep the stacked figures.
  - With reduced motion the panes cut instead of sliding, and the figures show their final state.

## D94 — undelabs.dev, and Google sign-in on (18 Oct)
- **Domain:** the frontend is mapped to `https://undelabs.dev` through a Cloud Run domain mapping, with DNS on Cloudflare. The run.app URL stays valid. The Developers page and docs now give the domain. `www.undelabs.dev` was still provisioning its certificate when this was written.
- **Google sign-in (D68):** turned on with OAuth client `88903435351-i8mugbokimlne3ns8rlsql93qrc7mths.apps.googleusercontent.com`, a Web client.
  - It allows the JavaScript origins `https://undelabs.dev` and the run.app URL. It has no redirect URIs, because the popup flow doesn't use one.
  - It is set as the backend's `TEAMS_AUDIENCE` and the frontend's `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`. The client ID is public; the client secret is unused and never stored in the repo.
  - The consent screen is External and in Testing: only listed test users can sign in. The home, privacy and terms links are on undelabs.dev.
- **Visitor cookies are per domain:** anonymous cases made on the run.app URL do not show on undelabs.dev. Pinned demos and signed-in users' cases show on both.

## D95 — Broker API keys issued from the command line, and on (18 Oct)
- **Problem (D91 check f):** issuing a key is an admin call (`POST /v1/admin/api-keys` with `X-Admin-Token`). The backend is private on Cloud Run, and the public proxy deliberately does not forward the admin token. Forwarding it would make the token usable from the internet. So the cloud agent got 403, and `API_KEYS` was turned back off.
- **Change:** `go run ./cmd/apikey issue -name NAME [-quota N] | list | disable ID` writes keys straight into Firestore, the store the server reads them from, using the operator's Application Default Credentials. It uses the same `apikey.Keys` and `store.FirestoreAPIKeys` as the server. The secret is printed once; only its hash is stored. There's no network path to the private backend and no admin token on the wire.
- **Brokers call** `https://undelabs.dev/api/v1/...` with `X-API-Key`. The proxy forwards it (D91).
- `API_KEYS=on` is set again in deploy.yml. It needs `CASES_STORE=firestore` and `ADMIN_TOKEN`, both already set.

## D96 — Explicit local fake mode; no silent fallback in production (19 Nov, #280)
- **Decision:** keep `LLM_PROVIDER=fake` as an explicit opt-in for offline UI work and tests. Do not make it the local default: ordinary local predict/plan output would be meaningless to a newcomer even though the provider is labelled.
- **Verified:** the provider already exists (`config.ProviderFake`), is wired in `cmd/server/newBase`, needs no `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, and is refused when `K_SERVICE` is set. `vertex` is the default, deploy.yml sets `LLM_PROVIDER=vertex`, and engine fallback reuses the configured model rather than dropping to fake. Config boundary tests cover fake locally vs. fake on Cloud Run. The corpus is bundled (`backend/data`, baked into the image), so fetching is only needed to rebuild it; the Accuracy page uses embedded `summary.json`. Simulated answers are labelled through the recorded `llm_provider` (`isProxy()` still recognises historical `litellm` results) and published evaluation provenance is unchanged.
- **Docs:** README and DETAILS.md now give a credential-free path (`LLM_PROVIDER=fake make run`, frontend with `BACKEND_URL`), state that ADC is only needed for `vertex`, and that the corpus is committed. D3 is marked superseded by D49; the OpenAPI enum drops `litellm`.


## D97 — A plan, comparison or export built from superseded case inputs is refused (19 Nov, #287)
- **Problem:** a plan search, SSE plan search or country comparison that started before a new BOM was applied could finish afterwards and attach a result for the old product to the new one. A chosen-plan export could combine old plan facts with the new product.
- **Decision:** before the work starts, the handler takes a fingerprint of the case inputs (product, facts, excluded rulings, current origin, family, date; an empty and a missing exclusion list count as the same). The save runs in the store update and refuses when the latest case no longer matches. A chosen-plan export also checks that the stored plan search it was chosen from is unchanged. Money, chat and history are not part of the fingerprint.
- **Result:** the request fails with 409 `conflict` and saves nothing. On the plan event stream the same failure is an `error` event with `retryable: true`. The caller runs it again.
- **Watch re-check:** the same check guards a watch result (`POST /v1/cases/{id}/watch` and the scheduler run), because a new BOM or answers clear the watch. A superseded re-check is a 409 and, in the scheduler run, counts as failed.
- **Export in the UI:** Download and Print record the export, and the recording call answers with the letter as the case stands now. The Draft screen compares it with the previewed letter of the same format (raw content, before the requester's own fields are filled in) and lets the letter out only when they match. Anything else holds it back: a 409, a different letter, a chosen move or plan that no longer exists, an authorization failure, a 4xx or 5xx, a network failure, or a success with no letter. The error shows with the server's own message (Retry only where the error is retryable), the stale draft is dropped, and nothing is downloaded or printed. There is no fail-open path: a letter whose freshness cannot be verified is not released, so a transient recording error costs a retry.
- **Answers (#288):** the answers handler uses the same check, so answers worked out against superseded inputs get 409 `conflict` and are not saved. When accepted answers add facts, the plan search, target comparison, watch result and export mark are cleared (the same clear as applying a BOM); history and the user's scenarios stay.

## D98 — A failed scope router sends the case to review, never to auto-accept (19 Nov, #289)
- **Problem:** when a configured scope router errored, the case was treated as in scope. A textile, pharmaceutical or Part 102 case with a high-confidence general answer could be auto-accepted, although nothing had established which legal regime applied (D7 needs confidence and scope).
- **Decision:** scope is now three-valued in practice: confirmed in scope, out of scope, or unavailable. When a router is configured and fails, the case keeps its provisional prediction, the route is `needs_review`, and the reason is exactly: `scope unavailable: the scope router failed, so the legal regime is not confirmed; an expert reviews`. Specialist engines, the global and per-family auto bars, and the fallback fast path do not apply, and the follow-up questions step is skipped. The case is not described as out of scope.
- **Unchanged:** with no router configured (a deployment choice, not a failure) triage behaves as before. A working router behaves as before.
- **Not measured:** how often the router fails in production; this is a safety rule, not a calibrated one.

## D99 — Case-insensitive upload media types, and no empty success on bad money (19 Nov, #324/#325)
- **Multipart dispatch:** the batch, product-import and BOM-version endpoints parse the media type and compare it case-insensitively (`mime.ParseMediaType` with `strings.EqualFold`) instead of a case-sensitive prefix check. Media types and subtypes are case-insensitive (RFC 9110), while the boundary parameter keeps its casing. A malformed or unsupported type falls through to the JSON body path, which returns a coherent error; browsers' lowercase `FormData` uploads are unchanged.
- **Money import:** `amount` rejects NaN and Inf, and derived products and sums that overflow are skipped with a warning. Invalid cells keep the existing "warn and ignore" contract rather than failing the whole import.
- **Response encoding:** `writeJSON` encodes into a buffer before committing the status. A value that cannot be serialized (such as a non-finite float) now returns a 500 JSON error instead of an empty 200 that clients cannot parse.

## D100 — A selected specialist is judged on its own; failure, missing label and unknown regime are explicit review states (19 Nov, #304 #300 #294 #319 #303)
- **Problem:** a healthy specialist (textile 102.21, Part 102 marking, pharmaceutical) failed whenever the unused general engine failed. A specialist error fell through to an ordinary triage as "out of scope", family confidence bars could override fallback-model review (D63 left bars off by default), the watch re-ran the general engine on specialist cases, Ask what-ifs bypassed the dispatch saved scenarios use, and a broker draft asked the substantial-transformation question under every non-textile regime.
- **Decision:** one dispatch and one triage policy (`httpapi/dispatch.go`) serve classify, scenarios, the watch and Ask. A selected, enabled specialist is called first and does not need the general engine to succeed. If it fails and the general engine succeeded, the general result is kept only as provisional and the route is `needs_review` with a reason naming the regime (`specialist unavailable: the <regime> engine failed, so the legal regime is not applied and the prediction is a provisional general one; an expert reviews`); if both fail the request fails. A confirmed special regime with no enabled engine stays an out-of-scope review.
- **Scope states:** `no_router` (a deployment choice, unchanged), `configured_failed` (D98, `ScopeUnavailableReason`), `confirmed`, and `label_missing` (a stored case with no scope label: `LabelMissingReason`, always `needs_review`, never auto). `configured_failed` and `label_missing` both mean scope is unavailable but stay distinct structured states.
- **Fallback before family bars:** a fallback-model answer is judged by the fallback policy before any family bar, so a family bar cannot auto-accept it. Family bars stay off by default.
- **Actual regime:** the regime that produced a stored prediction is read from its trace prompt version (`PredictionRegime`). It is `""` (unknown) for an unrecognised version, and consumers treat that as unknown, never as the router label and never as general. Export drafts and saved what-ifs use it.
- **Export:** the draft is written under the actual regime in both formats; unknown or legacy predictions render the general wording with a review note outside the printable letter that the framework is not confirmed.
- **Ask:** a what-if carries regime, specialist state, scope, route, reason and a provisional note, and these are saved with the chat turn; a saved what-if with no recorded regime reads as `unknown` and provisional.
- **Not measured / not validated:** how often a specialist or router fails in production, answer quality on real models (the prompt snapshot was updated offline, no model calls; D72 regression needs separate approval), and the legal text of the new drafts, which a broker must review.

## D101 — The ruling watch stays on the case's own regime and never reports a method change as a ruling change (19 Nov, #294)
- **Problem:** the re-check ran the general engine on every case, so a Part 102, textile or pharmaceutical case could come back with another origin and be reported as changed by today's rulings, although the legal test had changed.
- **Decision:** the re-check pins the case's stored scope label and uses the same dispatch and triage as a new case (D100); it does not route again. Each result records a `basis`: `precedent` (same regime and method; any change is the rulings), `regime` (another regime's engine answered), `method` (same regime, another engine version), `specialist_unavailable` (the stored regime's engine failed; the general answer is provisional) or `unverified` (no stored label, or the original regime is unknown, so the two are not comparable). It also records the regime that answered, the case's original regime, scope, specialist state and the triage of the result. `status` stays the origin comparison. The D97 check still decides whether the result may be saved (409 when the inputs moved).
- **Consumers:** only `basis: precedent` is a change caused by new rulings. The scheduler's `changed` list can include method and regime differences, so a consumer must read `changed_basis` (case id to basis) and not alert on a ruling change for any other basis. A result saved before this carries no basis and so cannot say whether the rulings moved or not: it is read as unknown, and the screens show "Re-check needed" (never Changed or Unchanged) until the case is re-checked; nothing is deleted or migrated.
- **Screens:** a watch result whose basis is not `precedent`, or whose triage is `needs_review`, shows as "Needs review", never as Changed or Unchanged by today's rulings. This is deliberate: when the basis or the triage is uncertain, the screen does not give legal assurance in either direction, and the cost is that more cases show as needing review than the old general watch label did. A reviewer objected that a `precedent` basis with `needs_review` triage would be better kept as "Unchanged"; the ruling is to keep it as review.
- **Reasons shown:** a Needs review watch gives the reason for its basis in words (a different regime, a different engine version, a failed specialist, an unconfirmed regime). The triage reason is added only when its route is a review route; an approving reason such as "confidence 0.97 >= 0.92 and in scope" is never shown beside Needs review.
- **Changed and review together:** a genuine precedent change stays labelled "Changed". When the watch result's triage route is a review route, the case page adds that a person must also look, with the reason; an approving reason is never shown. Every state that needs a person (Changed, Needs review, Re-check needed) is styled as needing attention in the list; only a plain Unchanged is styled as quiet.
- **Ask what-ifs (D100):** an Ask what-if card shows the auto-accept ruler only when its regime is known, its route is auto and it carries no provisional note. Otherwise it says Needs review, Needs more facts or Provisional answer, names the regime in words and gives the reason, and a scenario saved from it carries a warning that it is re-run under the full policy.
- **Not measured:** how many cases the watch will send to review in production.

## D102 — Landing input guards: zoom, focused controls, backward paging (19 Nov, #332/#333/#335)
- **Decision:** the landing's wheel/key classification moved into a small pure module (`landing/input.ts`). Ctrl- and Meta-modified wheel events return direction zero before burst bookkeeping, so browser zoom/pinch keeps its default action and cannot drive or suppress a story step. Space on a focused button, link or ARIA control is left alone so the browser activates it. Shift+Space maps to `back`; plain Space still maps to `forward`, and arrow/page keys are unchanged.
- **Why:** all three were regressions from #330's single input handler; keeping the modifiers in one tested place avoids reintroducing them while editing the animation loop. Node unit tests cover each classification without a browser harness.
- **Follow-up:** #334 (native scrolling during fig.2/fig.3 arrival replay) is an animation-lifecycle issue, addressed in D103.

## D103 — A visitor's scroll interrupts the figure arrival replay (19 Nov, #334)
- **Problem:** `goTo` starts a timed replay for fig.2/fig.3 but the frame loop selected it only by pane and elapsed time. Scrolling away during the replay kept the old animation running and, on expiry, snapped to the scroll-derived state without another user action.
- **Decision:** seek and arrival state moved into a pure `landing/arrival.ts`. A programmatic seek seeds the expected distance; while it is in progress a growing gap (the visitor pulling the page away) cancels both seek and replay, and once it has settled any position change does the same. The app's own smooth scroll shrinks the gap, so a normal arrival is untouched. On interruption fig.2 snaps straight to its scroll-derived state instead of easing from the replayed one; neither figure can jump when the old replay clock expires.
- **Why:** a scroll event alone cannot tell an application `scrollTo` from a visitor's scroll, so the distinction is made from position relative to the app's own target. Keeping the logic pure makes both figures testable in Node.

## D104 — Three small backend corrections (19 Nov, #323/#305/#313)
- **#323:** `bom.plantKey` is now the single fallback identity for both the product parser and the money importer. With no plant column the *normalized* country is the key, so `PRC` and `Viet Nam` group costs attach to the canonical `china`/`vietnam` plants instead of dangling aliases. This restores the imported baseline used to price a what-if move. Explicit plant columns are unchanged.
- **#305:** `Adder.ForAsOf` filters partial exclusions by an inclusive ISO `end_date`; `For` delegates with the current UTC date, while `MFN.FillWith` uses the rate snapshot's `FetchedAt`. Undated or malformed dates are kept rather than hidden. Exclusions are suggestions only — no duty amount is changed.
- **#313:** the spend meter keeps a `debt` of spend counted locally after a failed store write. A later successful write or refresh reconciles as `max(storeTotal + debt, total)` instead of replacing the meter with a store total that never saw the failed charge, so recovery cannot lower an already-over cap. The debt resets with the UTC day.
- **Why:** each is a localized accounting/identity mismatch rather than a design change; tests pin the alias keys, the expiry boundary and rate-snapshot date, and monotonic accounting across a store outage (including concurrent writes).

## D105 — Supplier identity is separate from the plant ID slug; money import uses the product's resolved IDs (19 Nov, #302 #323)
- **Decision:** a supplier is identified by its normalized name (Unicode-folded, punctuation and spacing ignored), not by the display slug. Distinct suppliers whose slugs collide, or whose slug is empty (for example non-Latin names), get a deterministic suffix hashed from the normalized name, independent of row order and of country. Repeated identical suppliers stay one plant. IDs that were unambiguous before do not change. One resolver (`NewPlantIDs`) serves CSV and XLSX import, BOM-version diffing and `KeepPlantIDs`. Money import asks the same resolver for each row's ID (explicit plant cell, else the canonical country fallback such as PRC to `china`, Viet Nam to `vietnam`), so annual group costs always refer to a product plant.
- **Why:** two real suppliers were merged into one plant with unioned capabilities, and imported baseline costs were attached to plant IDs that did not exist, so a relocation was priced against the wrong baseline (30000 added instead of 10000).
- **Limits:** an ID can depend on the other names in the same sheet (a colliding new name changes both IDs), so costs are valid only against the product from the same sheet. No route carries money costs through `KeepPlantIDs` today; the pairing would need a remap if one is added.

## D106 — Every direct-economics entry point validates before it prices or saves (19 Nov, #293)
- **Decision:** plan, plan estimate, scenarios and compare-targets run the same economics validation as case creation and the economics update, on the effective economics (supplied, or reused from the case). Negative annual values or group costs, rates outside 0 to 1, negative target costs and non-finite numbers return 400 `invalid_input` before any pricing, planner or model call, and nothing is saved.
- **Why:** invalid values were forwarded to the planner and persisted as the latest inputs.

## D107 — A change of component origin, plant country or operations is a real scenario change (19 Nov, #296)
- **Decision:** the target comparison suppresses an origin disagreement only when the two structured setups are materially equivalent. Equivalence covers component country of origin, plant identity and country, capabilities and operations, ignoring names, descriptions, HTS, ordering and country spelling. Without a plant move, a changed component origin is still a sourcing change and is priced at the scenario origin. Capabilities or names that only differ in duplicate form are priced conservatively.
- **Why:** a purchased part moved from China to Thailand was priced as China with zero saving.

## D108 — One strict HTS syntax before any sourced, live or automatic pricing (19 Nov, #306)
- **Decision:** `duty.ParseHTS` accepts 8 digits, or 10 with a statistical suffix, with one consistent separator (dot, space or dash). Anything else (letters, other lengths, mixed separators) is invalid. The sourced table, live MFN, the duty route, plan auto-pricing, money prefill and product import all use it; a 10-digit number maps to its 8-digit subheading. On `GET /v1/duty-rates` a malformed number, or a query string that cannot be parsed at all (even in another key), is 400 `invalid_input` with a short fixed message that does not echo the input; a present but empty `hts` is 400; only an absent `hts` lists every line. With duplicate `hts` parameters the first wins. A malformed HTS makes no network call and fills no rate; money prefill reports it in `hts_note`.
- **Why:** letters around a known number, or an overlength string, used to return researched rates and auto-fill economics.

## D109 — Section 301 exclusion applicability is judged at one current UTC instant per request (19 Nov, #305)
- **Decision:** each pricing request reads one injected current-UTC instant (`Deps.Now`, real UTC when unset) and uses it for every origin; it is not the case date. An exclusion is valid through its stored end date, a whole UTC day (inclusive). After that the note says it is historical and not applicable as of that date per the embedded end date, and makes no statement about any later extension. If the end date cannot be read, the note asks the user to confirm its dates. Exclusions stay conditional and are never applied automatically (D90).
- **Why:** all 230 embedded exclusions end 9 Nov 2026 yet were still suggested as possibly applicable.
- **Limits:** end dates come from the embedded D90 data, not a fresh legal check; there is no start date, and nothing here claims whether any exclusion was or was not extended. The older `FillWith` still reads the clock per origin; production pricing uses `FillWithAsOf`.

## D110 — Batch C builds on #340 for #323 and #305; it keeps the collision-safe resolver and the request clock (19 Nov)
- **Decision:** #340 (D104) closed #323 and #305 with narrower fixes. This branch does not reopen or re-close them; it merges #340 and keeps the stronger earlier-reviewed behaviour on top: money import uses the collision-safe `NewPlantIDs` resolver (D105) instead of `plantKey`, which cannot tell two suppliers apart when their slugs collide; exclusion dates are judged at the pricing request's own current UTC instant (D109) rather than the rate snapshot's `FetchedAt`, because when rates were fetched is not the date a user is pricing for. `Adder.For` keeps the clock semantics, and `MFN.FillWith` stays a wrapper over `For`; production pricing uses `FillWithAsOf`. #340's spend change (#313) is unchanged. Its alias, expiry-boundary and spend tests are kept or adapted to the same assertions.
- **Why:** both changes were already independently reviewed here; merging #340 should add its coverage, not drop robustness.

## D111 — Verifier support needs validated, directly retrieved rulings; cached verdicts replay only with current proof (20 Nov, #295)
- **Decision:** eligible support is limited to rulings the agent retrieved directly: search hits and read results whose header names the requested id, dated before the case bound and not excluded. A ruling mentioned only in another ruling's text is never eligible, and the exclusions are checked again when the answer is accepted. A supported verdict needs a supporting ruling. A cached verdict that cites a ruling replays only with a stored proof of the current `ValidationEpoch` (same date bound and exclusions, every cited id in the proof's eligible list); the display trail is not authority. A counter ruling taken from another panel lane needs that lane's proof. A fresh verdict that fails the check is cleaned (the unproven citation removed, no longer supported), not just left unstored. Replacements are written to `<key>-<epoch>` because the blob stores never overwrite a key.
- **Why:** an excluded ruling named in an eligible ruling's text could be accepted as support, and old cached verdicts could replay it.
- **Cost:** every cached verdict that cites a ruling, seeded demo verdicts included, misses once and is re-run. `ValidationEpoch` is a validation tag, not a prompt version; the `verifier-vN` names are unchanged because the prompts did not change.
- **Limits:** a counter ruling is checked through the panel's proofs only; `ProofFor` is exported for fixtures.

## D112 — A claimed origin change needs distinct, known, well-formed origins (20 Nov, #297)
- **Decision:** a verdict is supported only if both origins are usable and different. Each answer origin is checked as raw text before normalisation: an empty origin, an empty or "unknown" member (`Unknown/China`, `China/`, `A//B`) or any comma makes it unusable, so it is never read as the one country left over. Usable origins go through the domain country rules and are compared as sets: equivalent origins (USA and United States of America, PRC and China, Viet Nam and Vietnam, Thai and Thailand, Burma and Myanmar) are not a change. When a known baseline for variant A is given, `origin_a` must equal it, and a baseline that is set but unusable fails closed instead of being skipped. The ruling citation stays as validated; only the supported flag is cleared. The same check runs on fresh answers and on every cached replay. `ValidationEpoch` becomes `val2`.
- **Why:** an answer with the same country on both sides and `changes_origin=true` was marked precedent-supported, and equivalent spellings or a half-unknown origin could pass as a change.
- **Cost:** every cached `val1` verdict that cites a ruling misses once and is re-run; no prompt or model version changed. Two aliases (`thai`, `burma`) were added to the domain country table, nothing more.
- **Limits:** a comma anywhere makes an origin unusable, so a country name that contains a comma and is not in the alias table (for example "Korea, Republic of") leaves a verdict, or a known baseline, unsupported: a possible false negative, chosen over a guess. Set comparison is strict (Thailand/China differs from Thailand; a baseline of China contradicts China/Thailand). The cache sees only normalised stored origins, so it relies on the fresh check never storing a supported verdict with a malformed raw origin; entries from before this check are `val1` and miss.

## D113 — The engine prediction cache keys on the evidence revision (20 Nov, #316)
- **Decision:** `corpus.RevisionOf` hashes every field the index or prompt reads (id, date, origin, holding, subject, facts, reasoning) in corpus order. The server stamps each engine's retriever with the revision of the exact slice it indexes, once at startup: main pool, textile after its reasoning overlay, USMCA and pharma subsets (`evidenceBM25`). The prediction cache key includes it. A retriever without a revision makes the cache a pass-through: nothing is read or written, and no fixed marker stands in. Seed and panel cache policies are unchanged. The hybrid retriever is eval-only.
- **Why:** after a corpus rebuild, a persistent cache replayed predictions that cited removed or superseded precedents.
- **Cost:** every existing retrieval-engine cache entry misses once.

## D114 — Corpus rebuild and bundle reconcile only what they generated (20 Nov, #317)
- **Decision:** `build` and `bundle` record the ids they wrote in a manifest (`.generated_manifest.json`, `.bundle_manifest.json`) and remove only obsolete `<id>.txt` files from the previous manifest, never by glob and never recursively, after checking each is a regular file. A directory from before manifests is bootstrapped only from the previous build's `split_meta.jsonl`; any other unexplained ruling file stops the build before anything changes. Staged files are created exclusively and without following links. A missing or unreadable `-keep-labels` file is an error (`""` opts out). A ruling-shaped name that is not a regular file (a link or directory) stops build and bundle until the operator replaces or moves it. `Store.Revision` and `RevisionOf` give the shared evidence revision (D113). `verify-data` ignores exactly the two manifest files.
- **Why:** a removed raw ruling kept its generated facts file, and `corpus.Load` kept admitting it.
- **Limits:** the facts directory itself being a link, and races between the check and the write, are not guarded; the rename sequence is not atomic, but a crash leaves an owned state that a rerun repairs.

## D115 — A clarification sentence that mixes a fact with something unknown is kept whole (20 Nov, #318)
- **Decision:** for the re-analysis, a sentence that states an independent fact and also something unknown is passed on whole and verbatim, qualifiers included. Sentences that are only unknown, conditional, hedged, elided or built on a relative or wh-clause are left out whole. No clause is ever cut out, because a cut can turn a qualified claim into a certain one.
- **Why:** "The motor is made in Thailand, but its value share is unknown" lost the motor; earlier attempts to keep only the known clause kept producing false certainty ("…, but the accuracy of this is unclear").
- **Cost:** the model reads more uncertainty text, and some legitimate mixed answers are dropped whole. `answers-v2` is unchanged.

## D116 — Ask can read every accepted fact; what-if uses one canonical facts string (20 Nov, #320, #321)
- **Decision:** `get_case` shows the first 6000 characters and says when facts are incomplete; `get_case_facts(offset, limit)` returns up to 4000 characters of the current case per call. `what_if` takes the full revised facts: trimmed, at most 12,000 characters, stored and returned with one terminal newline (not counted). Longer text is refused with a message, nothing is predicted, no card is made and no what-if is used. The predicted, returned, saved and Add-as-scenario facts are the same string. `Case.Validate` ignores one terminal newline, which fixes an off-by-one for 12,000-character facts on every path. `case-agent-v4` and the prompt snapshot change once. Limits are unchanged: 6 tool calls, 6 history turns, 2 what-ifs.
- **Why:** the agent could not see facts past 6000 characters, and an overlength what-if predicted a truncated prefix while the card claimed the full revision.

## D117 — Every model attempt is metered once, at the raw client, and reported usage is charged even when the answer is unusable (20 Nov, #312)
- **Decision:** the daily spend meter (D59) is installed exactly once per raw model client, directly over it and below pace, retry, fallback and the engine caches. Each provider attempt is charged at its own model's price, including an attempt that fails or returns no usable candidate after reporting usage (`ErrBadResponse`, a safety stop). Vertex now returns the reported usage and model ID with the error. An attempt that reports no usage metadata (a transport failure, an HTTP error body, a cancelled call) is charged nothing; nothing is invented.
- **Consequences:** a primary and a fallback model that both report usage are priced separately; a cache hit never reaches the raw client and never charges; the D104 outage-debt rule is unchanged. The smoke check stays unmetered. Limit: provider billing for calls that return no usage metadata can still exceed the meter.
- **Why:** the outer meter only saw the one response a call finally returned, so a failed attempt's tokens and a primary attempt's usage were never counted.

## D118 — Each deep-health store probe has its own case ID (20 Nov, #315)
- **Decision:** `GET /health?deep=1` writes, reads and deletes `healthcheck_probe_` plus 32 random lowercase hex characters, so overlapping checks, in one process or across instances, never delete each other's record. `IsHealthProbeID` (the legacy fixed ID, or that exact form) keeps probes out of case listings. A probe that fails midway is removed best-effort on a detached 5 second context; a Delete that the store denies can leave one hidden record per failing call.
- **Why:** the single shared ID made two healthy overlapping checks report a false degraded store.

## D119 — The case list is scoped to the caller before the recent-case cap (20 Nov, #301)
- **Decision:** `GET /v1/cases` reads pinned demos, the caller's own cases (`OwnerLister`), and their workspace's cases, filters out health probes and cases the caller cannot see, then applies the configured cap, newest first with pinned first. Foreign activity can no longer push an authorized case out of the window. Admin keeps the global window, widened until it holds the cap of real cases.
- **Legacy documents:** Firestore cannot query a field a document lacks, so the owner and pinned listings read every case document, decode the stored case, and filter on the decoded `Owner` and `Pinned`. No migration, backfill or index is required for correctness, and a stale top-level pinned flag cannot expose a case that is not pinned.
- **Cost, stated plainly:** these listings are not database-scoped. A signed-in user's list does one full-collection read for pinned cases and two for owner cases (three in total), a visitor or broker key two, plus the workspace query; the cost grows with the whole collection until a separate migration writes the fields and lets the reads become queries. Corrupt documents are skipped.
- **Why:** the global window was filled by other principals' activity.

## D120 — A pinned demo keeps the visitor's own Ask conversation without storing it (20 Nov, #309)
- **Decision:** `POST /v1/cases/{id}/ask` accepts an optional `history` of at most 6 completed `{question, answer}` turns (question 2000 characters, answer 4000, both non-empty). It is used only on a read-only pinned demo and is ignored on every other case, where the saved chat is the only source. It is never stored. The agent sees the seeded demo chat followed by the visitor's turns, cut to the last 6, and the answer-memo key covers exactly that history, so different conversations never share an answer. More than 6 turns, or an over-long entry, is a 400.
- **Long answers:** the browser replaces an entire answer over 4000 characters with a fixed omission marker in the context it sends, keeps the question, keeps the original answer on screen and tells the visitor the answer was left out of the context. The agent loses that answer's content; the visitor can restate it. Nothing is truncated to a prefix and nothing is summarized.
- **Which turns are the visitor's:** the browser remembers them per tab and per case in `sessionStorage` (`op:ask-own:<case>`), as a marker for each successful completed turn (its timestamp and the first 80 characters of the question), bounded to the last 6. Seeded, pending and failed turns are never marked, a marker from another case is not used, and Reset clears them, so a full page reload keeps the conversation context. If storage is unavailable the ask simply sends no history. The markers are never sent to the server.
- **Why:** the drawer showed the visitor's earlier turns, but the next request gave the agent only the seeded chat, so dependent follow-ups lost context.

## D130 — A model's configured identity is tracked apart from the version the provider reports (20 Nov, #344)
- **Decision:** a trace carries `requested_model`, the configured model the call was made to (the primary or the fallback), alongside `model_id`, the version the provider reported, which may differ (alias `gemini-4-argon`, version `gemini-4-argon-001`). `Trace.Identity()` is `requested_model`, or `model_id` on traces recorded before the field existed. `model_id` keeps its meaning as the diagnostic.
- **Cache and triage:** the prediction cache stores and reads under the requested identity, so a primary answer replays whatever version the provider reported, and `triage.AnsweredByFallback` compares the requested identity with the configured primary. A genuine fallback answer is stored under the fallback's key and never satisfies a primary lookup, so a later primary success is not blocked (D56). Entries written before the field existed are read as the primary's.
- **Unchanged:** spend prices the model that answered, from the reported version, below the cache and the fallback (D104); a cache hit costs nothing.
- **Known limit:** when votes are mixed, the winning vote's trace decides the identity, so a primary vote that wins over fallback votes is cached and triaged as primary.
- **API:** `requested_model` is an optional string in `prediction.trace`, which the schema leaves an open object, so `openapi.yaml` and the generated types do not change.
- **Why:** a successful primary response with a version suffix was treated as a fallback answer: it forced review with a false reason and was never replayed from the cache.

## D131 — A scenario comparison is only shown against the inputs it was priced with (20 Nov, #288, #347)
- **Decision:** each saved scenario comparison carries a basis stamp: a hash of what it was priced against, namely the case's product, facts, answers, current origin (CBP's if known, else the prediction), family, date and excluded rulings, plus the money the planner prices with (import value, duty rates, group costs). A comparison whose stamp differs from the case's current basis is recomputed from the stored predictions (cached tariff rates only, no model call) when the case is read, never shown stale. Reading does not write the store.
- **Scenario definitions stay:** a new BOM, new answers or new money clear or reprice the derived comparison, moved groups and `origin_differs_from_current`, but never the scenario's label, facts, product or prediction (D97's "scenarios are the user's own setups" holds). `clearDerived` drops the comparisons with the plan and targets.
- **Money:** `PUT /v1/cases/{id}/economics` reprices and restamps the saved scenarios in the same update as the economics, and returns them as `scenarios` (absent when there are none). A scenario save that was priced against superseded inputs, including the money, is discarded with a 409 (the guard runs before the economics are assigned), so it cannot restore stale numbers.
- **Missing inputs are null, not zero:** with no annual import value, or a duty rate or cost that is missing, the comparison is `null` and the screen shows "add duty rates or costs". There is no stale flag.
- **Browser:** when the browser cannot re-read the case after saving money, it uses the `scenarios` from the PUT response instead of keeping the old ones.
- **Cold live-rate cache:** a read or a money save reprices from the researched table, rates the user gave, and live tariff rates already in the cache; it never fetches a live rate and never keeps an old comparison. When a scenario needs a live rate that is not cached (no table line for its tariff code, and no rate given for that origin), its comparison is `null` ("add duty rates or costs"), not an old or invented number. This applies to every money edit and every baseline change, not only to scenarios saved before the stamp existed, and the comparison can stay `null` until the scenarios are run again, which prices them with live rates. Rates the user gave are never affected.
- **Why:** after a BOM change or a money edit the case still showed savings, moved groups and a changed-origin flag worked out from the old inputs, for example $380,000 after the inputs implied $880,000.

## D132 — Every review gate that applies must pass before a case is accepted automatically (20 Nov, #300)
- **Decision:** `triage.AllGates` combines independent gates: a case is `auto` only when every applicable gate is `auto`, and no gate overrides another. For a specialist regime (textile, USMCA, pharma) the specialist bar always applies, and when the fallback model answered, the fallback bar applies as well. The general path keeps its order (fallback check before the family bars).
- **Reasons:** the review reason joins the reasons of every gate that sent the case to review, so simultaneous reasons stay visible; when every gate passes, all their reasons are kept. A single gate is returned unchanged.
- **Zero and defaults:** a bar of 0 still means "always review" and applies to its own gate; all fast paths stay off by default. Specialist-failed, scope-unavailable (D98), label-missing and unconfigured-specialist cases are unchanged and always review.
- **Not changed:** with mixed votes the winning vote's trace decides the answering model (D130).
- **Why:** the USMCA and pharma specialist fast paths ignored the fallback bar, and the textile branch replaced its own bar with the fallback decision, so a fallback answer could be accepted while one gate required review.

## D133 — U.S. scope; EU BOI and USMCA RVC deferred (20 Nov)
- **Context:** the audits listed "19 CFR Part 102 / Part 177" as the corpus scope and noted missing EU BOI and USMCA RVC (#282).
- **Decision:** document the U.S. scope as configured (`docs/scope-and-jurisdiction.md`); correct the audit reading (Part 177 is a ruling-request procedure; Part 102 here is marking origin, not USMCA qualification or RVC); defer EU BOI and USMCA RVC as two separate roadmap scopes. Neither is implemented and no coverage is claimed.
- **Before any coverage claim for either:** a data source and licence, deterministic rule encoding, a separate evaluation set and protocol, review by a qualified legal professional, and product and copy changes.
- **Not done:** no routing, prompt, config or schema change. A case the router labels `unclear` or `substantial_transformation` still goes to the general engine, and there is no jurisdiction detector or guard for a question about another country's rules. Deployed flag values were not inspected. The unsupported-use-case wording is D134.

## D134 — Broker-review notes sit beside results, never in the letter; "best" is bounded; scope says what the tool is for (20 Nov, #278, #282)
- **Decision:** the origin, plan, compare, scenarios and draft screens each get one short local note (`ReviewNote`, `frontend/src/components/op/review-note.tsx`; text in `copy.review` and `copy.jurisdiction` in `frontend/src/lib/copy.ts`). It is screen-only (`no-print`) and sits outside the broker letter. The letter text, the Markdown download and the printed page are exactly what the server returned with the requester's fields filled in (#265, D97); the server's own reviewer notes stay beside the letter as before.
- **Bounded wording:** a best plan, best country or highest-net scenario is the best of the moves (or scenarios) searched, using the duty and cost inputs shown, which may be prefilled or illustrative. The legacy plan ranking may count a blank cost as zero (not a verified expense); compare and scenarios leave a missing required input unpriced. Factors the model does not include are not counted or assessed. It never says optimal, cheapest or guaranteed and reads no API field, so it holds in the legacy mode and the operations mode without a DTO dependency. A later operations-mode "not modelled" copy must stay compatible with "required input missing" and read the labels from the result, not from a fixed list.
- **Scope wording:** it states what the tool is for (U.S. origin only; not trade-agreement claims, certificates of origin or non-U.S. rules; a Part 102 label is marking origin, not USMCA qualification or RVC; Part 177 is how to ask CBP for a ruling, not an origin rule). It does not say unsupported questions are detected or blocked: there is no jurisdiction detector (`docs/scope-and-jurisdiction.md` section 3). The accuracy page lists what is not covered (EU BOI, USMCA RVC and preferential-origin claims, certificates of origin, drawback, antidumping and countervailing duty questions; Section 232 duties are not included in the comparisons) under "What we don't claim".
- **Terms:** the Auto-accepted term no longer says it skips expert review: it passes configured confidence and scope checks, is not legal approval, and is still a prediction for a licensed customs broker or counsel to review. The Part 102 regime is labelled "Part 102 marking origin" on the origin chip and the accuracy card (internal ids unchanged).
- **Not done:** no global navigation disclaimer (the owner removed it on 18 Oct; the landing footer line stays); no no-template line (no screen for it); no server change.
- **Why:** the audit (M4) asked whether verdicts, plans and drafts carry the same broker-review framing as HTS suggestions; a high-confidence verdict had none, and "best plan" read as unbounded.

## D135 — Server logs are allowlisted; one request reference; errors are a class and a status (20 Nov, #285)
- **Decision:** one `logsafe` handler (`backend/internal/logsafe`) is the root `slog` handler of `cmd/server`, the `http.Server.ErrorLog` and `slog.Default()`. Unknown attribute keys and groups are dropped, unknown messages become `[message masked]`, values outside a closed set become `[masked]`. The routes, models, tools, health checks, audit actions and failure classes are closed sets built before the handler is made.
- **Request reference:** `logsafe.RequestRef` runs once at the top of the pipeline; the same value is the `X-Request-ID` response header, the `request_id` log field and the audit event's reference. A caller-supplied value is never echoed or stored (it is hashed); `audit.NormalizeRequestID` now hashes every raw value (no 16-hex or `h_` pass-through; this supersedes that part of the #284 contract). The reference is an unkeyed SHA-256, log hygiene, not anonymisation.
- **Errors and prompts:** errors are logged as a class and a status number; fatal startup errors as a class only; no model prompt, message or answer is logged at any level (the DEBUG line carries sizes only, no environment switch brings it back). The Next proxy logs one fixed-shape line and never a body, header or error object.
- **Not changed:** authorized case data, model input, exports and caches are untouched (no pattern scanner, no PII detector, no silent redaction of origin countries, costs or suppliers); history keeps the member's email for the workspace; earlier history lines, stored reasons and caches are not rewritten or purged.
- **Not claimed:** this is not a redaction, anonymisation, compliance or certification claim, and says nothing about retention, Cloud Logging, residency or what a model provider does. The offline CLIs are not wired. See `docs/design/data-handling.md` for the wiring list and the known limits.

## D136 — Optional operating-cost model: modelled net, explicit inputs, cost only demotes (20 Nov, #281)
- **Decision:** a request may carry `economics.operations`. When it is active, plans, the country comparison and scenarios use one shared calculation (`backend/internal/planner/opcost`): modelled net per year = duty saved − operating cost difference − one-time costs spread over a user-chosen horizon. Without an active block every path, response and stored case is as before.
- **Inputs:** the user types every figure; none is defaulted or estimated. An empty field is not entered, an explicit 0 is a known zero. Labour and logistics need an explicit `included` or `additional` basis to avoid double counting. Capex and retooling count at the destination plant only; capital at the current plant is sunk and ignored. Horizon is required whenever a one-time cost applies.
- **Results:** an unknown amount is `null`, never 0, and makes only the affected total unknown. A factor not entered is not modelled and is listed. `not_modelled` is returned with every result, also a fully priced one, and clients must show it beside a priced figure (the API contract; this decision does not claim any screen does). "Best" means the best of the searched moves under the inputs supplied, not a global optimum.
- **Feasibility:** lead time (the slowest moved group, an assumption of parallel retooling) and minimum order quantity (exact unit match, no conversion) give feasible, infeasible or unknown, kept apart from the money. A known infeasible move or a fully priced negative net is demoted to the rejected rows with a reason, even when the other side is unknown; unknown money or unknown feasibility on its own never demotes. Cost never promotes a plan and never changes a legal result.
- **Money source and staleness:** an explicitly active `operations` block uses the request's whole economics even at an annual value of 0 (nothing priced, feasibility kept); otherwise the old rule (own economics with a positive annual value, else stored). A plan computed in the operating-cost mode records a hash of the raw money inputs (legacy plans do not); changing a cost, rate, constraint or the annual value makes the API mark the stored plan stale (a client must not present its money as current; the legal result and the money-free letter stay). Notes and sources change no result and no hash.
- **Not claimed:** no cost-optimal, cheapest, approved, legal or real-price claim; not modelled: capacity use, discounting and time value, taxes and residual value, ramp-up and yield, subsidies. Operating-cost plant entries are bound to the plant's name (or id when unnamed) and country, not a unique identity; the legacy `group_costs` table is still keyed by plant id, so #372 (a re-added plant inheriting an old group cost) is not fixed. Figures above 9e13 dollars become unknown (`arithmetic_range`); totals are sums of per-term cent-rounded values, so the two sides can differ from the net by a cent. Legacy golden tests compare parsed, normalised JSON, not raw HTTP bytes. Full description: `docs/modelled-operational-costs.md`.


## D137 — The public API: shared limits in production, per-client caps, security headers (21 Nov, review follow-up)
- **Why:** a review asked that the API given to the public have proper rate limiting and other security. An audit of main found: production counted limits per instance (deploy does not set `RATE_LIMIT_STORE`, so with up to 3 instances a client got about 3 times its limit); case reads, rename, delete, economics, export, demos, eval summary, workspaces and the duty-rate lookup had no limit, and the duty-rate lookup bypassed the shared counters; invalid API keys were unthrottled; no cap on concurrent model calls per client; only a global spend cap; no `RateLimit-*` headers; the HTTP server had only a header timeout; CSV uploads were parsed whole before any row cap; no security headers on the backend or the frontend.
- **Shared limits in production:** with `K_SERVICE` set, a project set and `RATE_LIMIT_STORE` unset, the store defaults to `firestore` (D70). If the Firestore client cannot be made at startup in that defaulted case, the instance starts with its own limits and logs a warning; an explicit `firestore` still fails startup. Each instance also deletes ended windows every 10 minutes (older than 5 minutes, 500 a batch, up to 10 batches), so `rate_limits` stays bounded with or without a TTL policy. The deploy workflow is unchanged.
- **Every route has a rate class:** a new `basic` class (120 a minute per client, `RATE_BASIC_PER_MIN`) covers the routes that make no model call, with a 30 s handler deadline. It is counted per instance only, not in Firestore, so a read costs no store write. A test fails if a `/v1` route is registered without `limit`, `rateOnly`, `cheap` or (admin routes only) `adminOnly`. The duty-rate live lookup now goes through the same path as ruling lookups (shared counters when configured).
- **Per-client caps:** at most 4 model-calling requests running at once per client on an instance (`RATE_MAX_IN_FLIGHT`; 429 `too_many_in_flight`, released when the handler returns, streams included); 10 invalid API keys a minute per address (`RATE_AUTH_FAILURES_PER_MIN`; then 429 `auth_rate_limited` before the key lookup); one client may spend at most a tenth of the daily model budget (`DAILY_SPEND_CLIENT_SHARE`; 429 `client_budget_reached`; admin exempt). Model calls are charged to the client through the request context; per-client totals are stored in `model_spend` under `<day>_c_<hash>`. If the API-key usage counter fails, requests are still served but counted on the instance, so a key stops at its quota there.
- **Headers:** limited responses carry `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining`, `RateLimit-Reset`; the frontend proxy passes them and `Retry-After` through. Backend: `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store` on `/v1`. Frontend: HSTS, `nosniff`, `strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Permissions-Policy` (camera for this site only). The Content Security Policy is shipped as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`: nonces would force per-request rendering, which `cacheComponents` rules out, and Google sign-in (scripts and frames from `accounts.google.com/gsi`) could not be exercised locally. No CORS headers are sent; cross-origin browser calls are not supported.
- **Server:** `ReadTimeout` 30 s, `IdleTimeout` 120 s, `MaxHeaderBytes` 64 KB beside the 10 s header timeout. No `WriteTimeout`, because predict and plan stream; a test shows a stream outlasting `ReadTimeout` keeps its context (Go 1.27).
- **Uploads:** CSV is read row by row with the sheet caps: 1,000 rows, 64 columns, and a new 2,000-character cap per cell for both formats.
- **MCP (#379):** `POST /mcp` takes the basic class (no deadline: a tool call may run a plan). Each tool call re-enters the handler chain in-process and takes its `/v1` route's limits there, so the in-flight cap, spend share, quota and failed-key limit apply once per tool call (tested). Security headers and `Cache-Control: no-store` cover `/mcp`; the frontend `/mcp` proxy passes the RateLimit fields through. In-process calls skip the `REQUIRE_BEARER` check (their POST was checked).
- **`REQUIRE_BEARER`** (default off): refuses requests other than `/health` without `Authorization: Bearer`. The frontend proxy always sends one on Cloud Run (`backend.ts`), and the deploy smoke test calls only the frontend, but whether Cloud Run passes the header to the container was not checked against the live service, so it stays off.
- **Checked, not changed:** the ruling and ask memos were already bounded (500 entries each). The 4xx messages that echo an error were spot-checked; none carried internals.
- **Measured:** unit and handler tests only (`internal/httpapi/api_security_test.go`, `internal/spend`, `internal/store` prune against the fake Firestore server, `internal/config`, `internal/bom`, `cmd/server`), and a local run with the fake model on ports 8183 and 3103: 429 after the basic limit with `RateLimit-*` and `Retry-After`, 429 `auth_rate_limited` on the 11th invalid key, the frontend security headers, and no CSP violation while moving through the main pages. Not run on Cloud Run or real Firestore.
- **Not done:** per-user (signed-in) limits are still per address or key; an invalid `X-User-Token` is not throttled like an invalid key; the in-flight and failed-key caps are per instance; no CSP enforcement; no Firestore TTL policy was created (owner action, below).
- **Owner action:** optional but cheaper than the prune: `gcloud firestore fields ttls update expires_at --collection-group=rate_limits --enable-ttl --project=<project>`.

## D138 — Ten more planner families, chosen and written from CBP precedent (21 Nov, review follow-up)
- **Problem:** the planner had templates for three families (motorised tools and small appliances, LiDAR modules, LED lights). Origin prediction works for any substantial-transformation product, but a plan needs a template, so most products got an origin and no plan.
- **Choice by evidence:** the 845 substantial-transformation rulings outside the dev, held-out and pilot splits (the selection pool) were labelled with a product category by gemini-3.8-flash from the subject and the first 1,500 characters of the facts, and whether the good is assembled from separately made parts (`eval/results/families-selection.json`, `families-selection-pool-labels.jsonl`). Rule: assembled-goods categories with at least 10 assembled pool rulings and no template; regimes with their own engines (textiles, pharmaceuticals), processed goods (food, chemicals, paper) and mixed categories (machinery, metal articles, toys and sporting goods, plastic and rubber articles, other household goods) left out. Gas grills are the household-goods subset that qualifies on its own (11). Electric motors, pumps and compressors (10) are the next candidate; they overlap the motorised template and vehicle parts. Batteries (8), solar (4) and bicycles and scooters (4) have too few rulings.
- **Families added:** PCBA-based electronic devices (86 assembled pool rulings), vehicle parts (88), hand tools (67), cables and harnesses (59), medical devices (26), power supplies and chargers (23), furniture (15), display modules and cameras (14), water filters (12), gas grills (11).
- **Writing the templates:** gemini-4-argon drafted each template from up to 45 of its family's pool rulings (subject, held origin, holding sentence, start of the facts). Each group lists `precedent.confers_origin` (rulings where that work in a country was among the reasons it became the origin) and `precedent.not_enough` (rulings where it was not). gemini-3.8-flash then checked every citation against that ruling's facts and holding: 267 of 326 checks supported, the rest dropped (`eval/results/families-citation-checks.jsonl`). Notes were rewritten to cite only the kept rulings. Two edits after review: the water-filter media group keeps carbon blocks only (its RO-membrane wording made true carbon-block citations fail), and the power-supply cable group, with no supported citation, was merged into housing and minor parts. 156 distinct rulings are cited across the ten. Scripts: `eval/families/`.
- **Guards:** `planner.Template.Sources` and `TemplateGroup.Precedent` document the template; the planner does not read them, and a plan is still checked by the verifier against rulings dated before the case. `TestTemplateCitationsAreRealRulings` fails if a cited ruling is not a substantial-transformation ruling in `backend/data/labels.jsonl`, is in the dev, held-out or pilot split, is cited by a note but not the group's lists, or if a movable group cites nothing. `TestTemplateSetIsPinned` pins the 13 families. `frontend/src/lib/families.ts` feeds the family picker and the group suggestions; `scripts/families.test.mjs` keeps it equal to the templates.
- **Fixtures:** one per new family in `backend/testdata/families/`, built from a pool ruling's facts (a model draft, then hand edits; `eval/families/buildfx.py`). Eight start from CBP's own setup; the ratchet wrench is an illustrative all-China start built from N347615, and the server cable is scenario one of N331047, whose holding names no origin. `TestFamilyFixturesPlan` runs each through the plan search with a fake engine and verifier.
- **Family check output cap:** at 400 output tokens the family check cut off 31 of 469 development checks before the JSON (thinking is billed as output). It is now 1024. 17 of 469 development and 9 of 377 held-out checks still failed; the product then leaves the family as the user gave it.
- **Coverage (product scope), measured with the product's family check** (gemini-3.8-flash, confidence ≥ 0.6, the import's bar; subject plus the first 1,500 characters of the facts; `go run ./cmd/eval familycov`). Share of rulings matched to a family with a template, failed checks counted as not covered: development 469: 23 (4.9%) with the three templates, 258 (55.0%) with all 13. Held-out 377, scored once per set after the templates were committed: 16 (4.2%) before, 215 (57.0%) after; on the 368 checks that answered, 58.4% (53.3–63.3). This is product scope, not accuracy: a match means a template exists for the product, not that a plan will be found or is right. A spot check of 15 development matches found one stretch (a pet bed matched to furniture).
- **Live checks:** each fixture was run once through the gateway (engine and verifier gemini-4-argon, verifier-v2, local corpus, plan bar 0.92): 7 of 10 showed a precedent-supported plan, 3 (water dispenser, speaker stand, server cable) had none above the bar and go to a broker. The engine's own view of the current setup disagreed with CBP's holding in 5 of the 8 fixtures where CBP named one (it named the assembly or target country where CBP held China); the planner starts from CBP's holding when it is given. $0.97 promo for the ten (`eval/results/families-live-checks.json`). These are development checks on fixtures from the selection pool, not an evaluation of plan quality.
- **Not done:** no held-out test of plan quality for the new families; fixtures are one per family; the three earlier templates are unchanged and carry no per-group citations; the family check ran through the gateway, not Vertex; no template builder for users.
- **Correction (D264, #392):** the ruling-grounded templates were drafted from up to 45 rulings each, so several groups list the union of different products' processes. `Render` used those operations whenever a fixture supplied none for a moved group, telling the engine that a water dispenser moved to South Korea would also make UV lamps and RFID chips, and that a needle moved to Vietnam would forge surgical forceps. The eleven CBP-grounded families now set `union_default_operations`; with no supplied operations the renderer writes a neutral "made in" line and no steps, so no other product's parts are asserted as facts. The three earlier templates keep their product-specific defaults. The one-run live checks above ran on the old facts and are not a validation of the corrected facts; the result files are kept as run and no model was re-run.

## D150 — A second held-out split from rulings published after the first one (21 Nov)
- **Why:** the engine's held-out split (377 rulings, Jan–Sep 2026) has been scored and reported. A new test set, built and frozen before anything is scored on it, is the clean way to check the headline and to score a new candidate.
- **Finding the rulings.** CROSS's search endpoint now answers scripted requests with "Security parameters are missing from this request context", so it was not used. Single rulings are still served at `/api/ruling/<id>`, and New York rulings are numbered roughly in order of issue. `go run ./cmd/corpus probe` walked ruling numbers (N362500–N367300 and H361000–H362333) at about one request a second on 21 Nov 2026. Every number tried and CROSS's answer is in `eval/data/final2_probe.jsonl`; a ruling was saved when its category line names origin or its text says "country of origin", and it is dated 26 Sep 2026 or later. Headquarters numbers are assigned at intake, so this range finds only some recent HQ rulings; the split is almost all New York rulings. Rulings CBP has not yet posted are missing, which thins the last weeks.
- **Labelling.** `go run ./cmd/corpus final2-label` applies the same regex holding extractor that produced the "regex" rows of `labels.jsonl` (`corpus.LabelHolding`, a port of the research labeller) and a reconstructed regime rule (`corpus.ClassifyRegime`). `go run ./cmd/corpus label-check` re-labels all 2,790 rulings of `labels.jsonl` with them, with no model calls (`eval/data/final2_labeller_check.json`): the held origin matches on all 1,620 regex-labelled substantial-transformation rulings; the regime rule finds substantial transformation with precision 97.6% and recall 98.4%; the multi-country flag agrees on 98.5% of 2,790. Every candidate in the split had its holding sentence read against the regex label, and the four without a single regex origin were read in full; corrections are in `eval/data/final2_manual.json` with a reason, and those rows say `"method": "manual"`. The reading was done by the agent building the split, not by a customs expert.
- **Rule:** the one that reproduces the first held-out split (docs/EVALUATION.md 2.2): substantial-transformation regime, facts naming two or more countries, a single held origin ("A | B" holdings excluded), a facts section. `eval/splits/build_final2_split.py` writes `eval/splits/splits_final2.json` with the funnel, the composition and `frozen_at`. The labels, the split, the probe index, the manual corrections and the facts files (`eval/data/facts_final2/`) are pinned in `eval/SHA256SUMS`, committed before any engine was scored on the split.
- **Result:** 6,135 numbers tried, 1,013 rulings found, 163 saved and labelled. Of those, 85 are substantial transformation with facts naming two or more countries; 2 hold two or more countries (N365226 tires, N366357 avocado oil) and are excluded, leaving **83 rulings dated 28 Sep to 13 Nov 2026**, all New York rulings; 81 regex labels, 2 manual (N364778, N365630). The multi-scenario scan of D23 (`eval multiscenario -split-names final2`) flags none. Frozen at 13:50 +08:00 on 21 Nov in commit dfc712b, which `RUNLOG.jsonl` records for the scoring run; rebasing this branch onto main later gave the same eval files the commit id 49a103b. Scoring: `eval run -split final2 -confirm-final -splits ../eval/splits/splits_final2.json -labels ../eval/data/labels.jsonl,../eval/data/labels_final2.jsonl`. Precedents are strictly earlier rulings from both label files, as on the first split.
- **Limits:** smaller than the first split, from about eight weeks of rulings; the regime rule is a reconstruction; the label check is one reader, not a panel.

## D151 — An OpenAI-compatible gateway provider for offline evaluation only (21 Nov)
- **Decision:** `cmd/eval` accepts `LLM_PROVIDER=litellm` (`internal/llm/litellm.go`): an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini, with temperature 0, the fixed seed (D76) and JSON-schema output, like the Vertex provider. Every row and `RUNLOG.jsonl` entry records `litellm`, and the row carries the cost the gateway reported (`cost_usd_gateway`). The server's config still accepts only `vertex` and `fake` (D49), so this path cannot reach production. Vertex behaviour is unchanged.
- **Why:** the team's model budget for this work is on the gateway, and it serves Gemini 4 Carbon and 3.1 Pro preview, which have no Vertex dev runs here.
- **Parity:** the same engine-v1 Argon configuration on the development set: Vertex 91.1% (88.2–93.4), gateway 91.8% (88.9–94.0); fixed 8, broke 5, exact McNemar p = 0.58. The gateway is not a different model.
- **Caveats:** the gateway caches identical requests. A call that timed out and was retried can come back from that cache in under a second, so a few latencies are too low and their cost is not reported; for those, the token-priced cost is used where a price is known. Calls use a 120 s timeout instead of the server's 30 s so that long answers are measured instead of retried. When a model spends its whole output budget thinking, the gateway returns no text; the row records the finish reason and counts as wrong, as any failed call does.

## D152 — Model sweep and engine candidates on the development set; selection rule fixed before the sweep finished (21 Nov)
- **Question:** does a newer Gemini model, or a cheap change around the engine, beat engine-v1 on Gemini 4 Argon (held-out 90.2%, D39)?
- **Arms, all on the development split only (469 rulings, 463 after the D23 adjustments), engine-v1 prompt, BM25, K = 5, temperature 0, seed 20261017, through the gateway (D151):** Gemini 4 Argon (to measure the gateway against the Vertex AI run of the same configuration), Gemini 4 Carbon, Gemini 3.1 Pro preview. Computed from those rows with no further calls: a three-model majority vote, and an isotonic calibration layer (D156). Gemini 3.8 Flash already has a Vertex dev run and is reported from it.
- **Selection rule, written while the Carbon and 3.1 Pro runs were still in progress (about 50 and 30 of 469 cases done) and before any held-out file exists for them:** the candidate is the arm with the highest adjusted dev accuracy; ties go to the higher fast-path accuracy at the 0.92 bar. An arm is eligible only if its dev fast-path accuracy is at least 95% (the rule the bar was set by, D32) and it costs at most $0.05 per case. The candidate is pre-registered in D153 with its dev numbers before it is scored on any held-out split, and it is scored once on the 377-ruling split and once on the second held-out split (D150).
- **Production rule:** the candidate replaces engine-v1 in the code defaults only if it is better on dev with an exact McNemar p below 0.05 against the gateway Argon run, is not worse on the held-out splits, and its model is served on Vertex AI's global endpoint. Otherwise engine-v1 stays and the result is reported as it is.

## D153 — Pre-registered candidate: engine-v1 on Gemini 4 Argon, unchanged, plus a calibration layer (21 Nov)
- **Development results (D152), 463 rulings after the D23 adjustments,** `eval/results/accuracy-v2-metrics.json` (`dev`), built by `python3 -I eval/charts/build_accuracy.py .`:

| Arm | Accuracy (95% CI) | Failed calls | Fast path (≥ 0.92, in scope) | Cost / case | p50 / p95 | vs Argon on Vertex: fixed / broke, p |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Gemini 4 Argon, Vertex AI (in use) | 91.1% (88.2–93.4) | 0 | 79.5% at 96.5% | $0.0079 | 4.8 s / 8.1 s | — |
| Gemini 4 Argon, gateway | 91.8% (88.9–94.0) | 0 | 81.0% at 95.7% | $0.0079 | 6.8 s / 10.8 s | 8 / 5, p = 0.58 |
| Gemini 4 Carbon, gateway | 88.1% (84.9–90.8) | 27 | 81.9% at 96.3% | $0.0155 | 13.0 s / 52.2 s | 6 / 20, p = 0.009 |
| Gemini 3.1 Pro preview, gateway | 87.3% (83.9–90.0) | 10 | 83.6% at 92.8% | $0.0178 | 10.6 s / 24.7 s | 12 / 30, p = 0.008 |
| Gemini 3.8 Flash, Vertex AI (earlier run) | 90.1% (87.0–92.5) | 0 | 87.7% at 93.1% | $0.0050 | 5.5 s / 20.4 s | 13 / 18, p = 0.47 |
| Majority of Argon, Carbon, 3.1 Pro (Argon breaks ties) | 91.6% (88.7–93.8) | 0 | 76.2% at 96.3% | $0.0432 | 14.3 s / 55.3 s | 8 / 6, p = 0.79 |

- **Reading:** no newer model beats Argon. Carbon's failed calls are answers that used the whole 8,000-token output budget on thinking (`finish_reason` "length"); on the 436 cases it answered it matches Argon (408 against 406 right, p = 0.77), and Argon gets 19 of its 27 failures right. 3.1 Pro returned 10 empty answers and is worse on the cases it answered (404 against 416, p = 0.05). The majority vote costs five times as much for no gain. Gateway and Vertex agree on Argon (p = 0.58).
- **Error clusters, Argon on Vertex, 41 dev errors (excluded and alternative-gold rulings left out):** 21 predict China where CBP held the assembly country, 10 the reverse, 7 two other countries, 3 involve the United States. CBP itself splits close cases (air filters N351081, China, and N352485, Thailand, both 2025). Earlier prompt and retrieval changes aimed at these did not move accuracy (section 3.4 of docs/EVALUATION.md), so no new prompt was tried.
- **Candidate, by the D152 rule (highest adjusted dev accuracy, fast path at least 95%):** Gemini 4 Argon with engine-v1, BM25, K = 5, i.e. the production engine, unchanged. Added to it: the isotonic calibration layer of D156, which changes stated probabilities, not predictions.
- **Held-out plan, fixed now:** no engine changes, so the 377-ruling split is not re-scored (its Vertex run stands; D39). The candidate is scored once on the second held-out split (D150) through the gateway, with the router, after that split is frozen. The calibration layer is applied unchanged to both held-out splits. Production stays on engine-v1 and the code defaults do not change.

## D156 — A calibration layer fitted on the development set (21 Nov)
- **Problem:** stated confidence is overconfident by about 4.5 points on held-out (94.7% stated, 90.2% right; ECE 0.059; docs/EVALUATION.md 4.2).
- **Layer:** isotonic regression (pool-adjacent-violators) from stated confidence to the observed accuracy, one step per confidence value the model emits, fitted on the 463 development rows of engine-v1 on Vertex only, and capped at 0.99 because no errors in a hundred cases is not certainty. The map is in `eval/results/accuracy-v2-metrics.json` (`calibration_layer_engine_v1.map`); built by `eval/charts/build_accuracy.py`, no model calls.
- **Held-out, 377 rulings, applied once with the dev-fitted map:** ECE (one bin per value) 0.059 (0.041–0.091) before, 0.030 (0.020–0.059) after; Brier 0.081 (0.059–0.105) before, 0.072 (0.056–0.092) after; mean stated 94.7% before, 90.4% after, against 90.2% right. Bootstrap 95% intervals, 2,000 resamples, seed 20261017.
- **Scope:** built and measured in the eval only. The API still returns the model's stated confidence and the fast path still uses the 0.92 bar on it; the layer is monotone at that cut (stated 0.92 → calibrated 0.90), so it would accept the same cases there. Showing calibrated probabilities in the product is planned, not done. **Correction (D262, #397):** "monotone" means weakly monotone here — pooling and the 0.99 cap tie levels (11 stated → 6 calibrated), so there is no equivalent calibrated cut at 0.99/1.00, and the before/after ECE uses different bins. See D262.
