Where a ruling is cited as support (a plan's supporting ruling, the origin step's cited precedents) and the change is dated on or before the case date (before it, since D370), it adds "not counted as support for this case"; the guard means only results stored before D320 can show that. /method question 2 and the /accuracy plan-checks section say in one line what the guard does, with D320's counts (35 changes to 34 rulings; 43 of 12,998 committed rows had shown one and 10 had cited one; 23 and 8 of 13,166 since D370). DATASHEET §8 now says what the API, MCP and site each show. The site has no ruling-search page; the API and MCP search already mark such rulings.
- **Not done:** the guard's effect on accuracy is still not measured (D320).

## D344 — The backtest finds kept parts by component name when a demo has no map (#519, 22 Nov)
- **Problem:** D310 made "Matches CBP's actual change" also require that the plan moves nothing CBP's change kept in place, but both `planner.backtest` and the frontend's `backtestView` found kept parts only through `still_from_china_as`, which only the heat gun has. The gas grill's committed plan moves the burners, control panels and cart frames that N335085 kept in China and still read as a match; the cases-list badge (`backtestMatch`) applied no kept-parts rule at all.
- **Decision:** a kept word resolves to its `still_from_china_as` entry, else to a product component of the same name, ignoring case (`planner.ActualChange.KeptPart`, frontend `keptPart`). `backtestView` trusts a stored non-empty `plan_also_moves_kept` and otherwise works the list out, so a stored backtest that predates this fix is read the same way; the cases-list card fetches the demo's change and passes it with the product and top move, so the badge and the plan step agree. Every demo and test fixture's kept words now resolve, except the heat gun's terminal blocks, which the product has no component for. Tests: `TestBacktestFindsKeptPartsByComponentName` (the committed gas-grill plan is not a match; lids and fireboxes alone are), `TestBacktestTubLightLEDAloneStillMatches`, `TestEveryKeptPartResolvesToAComponent`, and three cases in `backtest-view.test.mjs`.
## D350 — PDF pages are counted through the xref, once per node, within one budget per upload (22 Nov, #503, #504)
- **Problem:** D302's parser collected objects with a `N 0 obj` regex over the whole file and let every object found in an object stream overwrite what was found at top level. An incremental update that rewrote a compressed page tree at top level was counted from the old tree (3 pages then 40 counted 3; 12 then 5 counted 12), and `2 0 obj` text inside an uncompressed XMP stream replaced the real page tree (40 counted 1) (#503). The walk cleared its visited set on return, so `/Kids [n 0 R n 0 R]` chains took 2^levels steps (24 levels: 1.5 KB, about 24 s), and each object stream had its own 4 MB inflate cap with no total, about 1,000 times the upload in memory (#504). Writing this fix also showed that `/Type` was matched anywhere in a dictionary: a `/Pages` root carrying `/X << /Type /Page >>` counted as 1 page.
- **Decision:** `pdf.go` locates objects through the cross-reference data, as a PDF reader does: the last `startxref`, then classic tables and xref streams (`/W`, `/Index`, Flate with PNG predictors), following `/Prev` and a hybrid file's `/XRefStm`; the newest entry for an object decides whether it is at an offset, in an object stream, or free. Only bytes an xref entry points at are parsed, and the object number there must match. Dictionaries are read by a small lexer into their own top-level keys (names `#xx`-decoded), so nested `/Type` values and strings are not the object's type. The page tree is walked once per node: a node reached twice (repeated kid, shared subtree, cycle) is refused. Objects expanded from object streams copy only their own bytes. A `media.Budget` covers one upload: 4 MB per decoded stream, 8 MB for all streams of all files together, and 2 seconds; the extract-media handler shares one budget across its files. A file without followable xref data is refused ("pages could not be counted"), as #503 asks, rather than counted from a scan that can under-count.
- **Evidence:** `pdf_test.go` generates the files in the test: the #503 shapes (3 then 40 and 12 then 5 with xref streams, with and without the Up predictor; 40 pages with a fake one-page `2 0 obj` in XMP) count 40, 5 and 40; the D302 parser on the same files gives 3, 3, 12 and 1. A 64-level doubled-kids chain is refused in well under 200 ms, with and without an xref; cycles and repeated kids are refused. Two pages in two 4 MB object streams count 2, three are refused, two such files in one budget are refused, and 100 unreferenced 4 MB streams are never decoded. 3,000 seeded mutations (truncation, byte flips, inserted tokens, cut ranges) of eight generated files never panic or take more than 250 ms; a `FuzzPDFPages` target holds the seeds. `-race` passes on `media` and `httpapi`. The test PDFs of `media` and `httpapi` now carry correct xref tables or streams. Offline, on 300 PDFs shipped with the operating system, installed apps and npm packages on one development machine, the new parser and D302's gave the same count for all 300 (none refused), the slowest under 1 ms; the frontend sample `heatgun-bom.pdf` counts 2. These files are not committed.
- **Not changed:** the 10-page limit, the 8 MB upload cap, the encryption check and the error messages. A damaged PDF whose xref offsets are wrong is now refused where D302 might have counted it; a user can re-save it.

## D351 — An invalid-key lookup in flight holds a token until it ends (22 Nov, #505)
- **Problem:** D282 admitted a key lookup while the failure bucket had a token and fewer than `AuthFailuresPerMin` lookups were in flight; tokens were spent only when a lookup finished. Guesses from one address a few milliseconds apart, while earlier lookups were still in the store, kept being admitted as finished lookups freed slots: with a 50 ms store and a limit of 10, up to 19 reached the store. `fail` used `AllowN`, which spends nothing once the bucket is empty, so a late failure did not lengthen the wait.
- **Decision:** `authFailures.begin` admits a lookup only while the bucket's tokens, less the lookups in flight, are at least one, under the same lock as before; the separate in-flight cap is no longer needed. A refused lookup gets the refill wait for the next free token. `fail` takes its token with `ReserveN`, into debt if the bucket is empty, so a late failure delays the client further. A valid key or a store error still gives its slot back without spending a token. Broker review links (D302) use the same `begin`, so they get the same bound. SECURITY.md says so. No limit changed.
- **Evidence:** `TestAPIKeyFailuresThrottledWhenStaggered` sends 60 unknown keys from one address through the real handler with a 50 ms store and a limit of 10, starting one every 0, 2, 5 and 10 ms: at most 10 lookups and 10 x 401 at every gap, the rest 429. Before this change it failed at 2, 5 and 10 ms (14 to 19 lookups). `TestAuthFailuresDebtLengthensWait` records a failure after the bucket is spent and checks the wait grows (it stayed 6 s before). The burst test (D282) and the review-link test (D302) still pass; `-race` passes on `httpapi`.

## D352 — Scrolling code samples on /developers are focusable, named regions (22 Nov, #517)
- **Problem:** at 390 px, four code samples on /developers (the Gemini CLI settings, the ADK agent, the Claude Desktop config and the REST example) scroll sideways but could not be focused, so a keyboard user could not read the rest of each line (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`).
- **Decision:** the page renders every sample through one `Code` component. A sample that can scroll is a `<pre role="region" tabIndex={0}>` with its own `aria-label`; the global `:focus-visible` outline marks it. The three one-line commands wrap on a phone, never scroll, and stay out of the tab order.
- **Evidence:** in the dev server at 390 px, the four samples that scroll are focusable named regions (a scrolling sample that is not focusable: 0, was 4); Tab reaches them with a 2 px accent outline, and the arrow keys scroll them. At 1280 px nothing scrolls and the page has no sideways overflow. `developers-code-focus.test.mjs` fails if a raw `<pre>` is added, if labels repeat, or if the region loses `tabIndex`, its role or its label.

## D353 — Pre-registration: the 18 post-cutoff flip pairs, run twice more (22 Nov, judge panel round 6, item 3)
Committed before any of the runs below. Nothing in the product, the pairs or the bar is changed because of their result.
- **Question:** the post-cutoff figures the landing, /accuracy and EVALUATION §7 quote (18 frozen pairs, D170 + D192; starting origin given: 9 of 9 shown were right, 9 of 12 real changes found, 0 of 6 controls shown) come from one gateway run. How much do they move when the same check is run again?
- **Set:** unchanged and frozen: `eval/data/flips_post.jsonl` (15 pairs, D170) and `eval/data/flips_post_addendum.jsonl` (3 pairs, D192), pinned in `eval/SHA256SUMS`.
- **Runs:** two more runs, N = 2 then 3, each in both modes and on both files, from `backend/`, one after another:
  `LITELLM_NO_CACHE=1 LLM_PROVIDER=litellm go run ./cmd/eval flips -post [-known-a] -workers 3 -facts <facts dir> -raw <raw CROSS dir> -tag run<N>`
  and the same with `-flips ../eval/data/flips_post_addendum.jsonl -tag addendum-run<N>`. Configuration as run 1 (D170): verifier-v2 on Gemini 4 Argon, one vote, no panel, local corpus search with each pair's date cutoff, temperature 0 and the fixed seed, plan bar 0.92, through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini, with `LITELLM_NO_CACHE=1` so no answer comes from the gateway cache. The code is today's main, which adds the revocation guard (D320) since run 1. In plan searches production also runs the panel (D83) on checks between 0.89 and 0.95; `eval flips` has no panel, as in run 1, so the three runs measure the same thing.
- **Reported, for every run and mode, on the combined 18 pairs:** moves shown at the bar and how many were right (a real change with CBP's later origin named), real changes shown and right (recall), controls shown, failed rows, gateway-reported cost. Across the three runs: the range of each figure, and per pair how many of the 3 runs showed it and whether every move shown in run 1 is shown again. All runs are reported whatever they show.
- **Failures:** a row that fails on a gateway error counts as not shown, as in run 1, and is not re-run. A whole run that fails to start may be started once more; that is disclosed.
- **Surfaces:** run 1's figures stay where they are. EVALUATION §7, NUMBERS, RESULTS, /accuracy and the landing say "one run" or "3 runs" accurately and give the range only where a figure is quoted as a single run.
- **Cost cap:** $4 for both runs (run 1 cost $1.34 for both modes and both files). If run 2 costs more than $2, run 3 is not started.

## D354 — The 18 post-cutoff flip pairs, run twice more: results (22 Nov, judge panel round 6, item 3)
- **Ran as registered in D353** (commit ea48b5a4, kept as branch `improve/panel6-flip-prereg`): runs 2 and 3, both modes, both files, gateway cache off. No run was restarted. One row failed on a gateway timeout in run 2 (the ratchet-wrench control, starting origin not given); it counts as not shown, as run 1's failed row did. Rows: `eval/results/flips-post-verifier-v2-{run2,run3,addendum-run2,addendum-run3}-{known-a,unknown-a}-litellm.jsonl`; summary: `eval/results/planner-post-repeats-summary.json` (`python3 -I -B eval/planner/summarize_repeats.py . --check`, now in `make reproduce`); eight RUNLOG lines.
- **Starting origin given: the same in all 3 runs.** Each run showed the same 9 moves, all right (9 of 9), found 9 of 12 real changes and showed 0 of 6 controls. The 9 shown moves kept their confidence within 0.02, and 8 of 9 cited the same supporting ruling in every run (the wax warmer cited N345085 in run 1 and H342414 in runs 2 and 3). Pooled over the 3 runs: 27 of 27 shown right (87.5–100), 27 of 36 found (58.9–86.3), 0 of 18 controls (0–17.6). The pooled intervals describe these runs, not new pairs: the 18 pairs are the same each time.
- **Starting origin not given: one wrong move came and went.** Shown 6, 6 and 5; right 5 each time (recall 5 of 12 in every run); controls 0 of 6 each time. The fracking unit, the one wrong move in run 1, was shown again at 0.92 in run 2 and fell below the bar (0.88) in run 3. The other 5 shown moves were shown in all 3 runs.
- **Cost:** $1.28 (run 2) and $1.38 (run 3), $2.66 gateway-reported, against the $4 cap.
- **What changes on the surfaces:** the figures stay run 1's, which equal runs 2 and 3 when the starting origin is given. Where they were called one run, the landing, /accuracy, EVALUATION §7, NUMBERS and RESULTS now say they were the same in 3 runs. The not-given row gives its range (shown 5 to 6, 0 to 1 wrong).
- **What it does not show:** run-to-run agreement on the same 18 pairs says the check is repeatable at temperature 0 with the fixed seed. It does not make the set larger: the intervals on new pairs are still run 1's (70–100, 47–91, 0–39). The panel (D83), which production runs on plan-search checks between 0.89 and 0.95, was not applied, as in run 1.

## D360 — A deterministic 19 CFR 102.20 rule check beside the Part 102 engine (22 Nov, judge panel round 6, "the main remaining larger build")
- **What:** `backend/internal/tariffshift` encodes the 102.20 tariff-shift rows for chapters 73, 82, 84, 85, 87, 90 and 94 and applies 19 CFR 102.11(a) and (b) to a good's HTS code and its materials (HTS code, origin, optional value share). Section XVI (84, 85) as asked; the other five chapters are where the goods of the 14 planner families fall (grills and stoves 73, hand tools 82, vehicle parts 87, medical and optical devices 90, furniture and lamps 94). The scope was set from the families before any evaluation case was read; no other chapter is encoded.
- **Source:** the verbatim row text of 19 CFR Part 102 from the eCFR API (point in time 18 Nov 2026, retrieved 22 Nov), public domain. `rules.json` keeps each row's text, its 102.20 paragraph and the parsed alternatives; `go run ./cmd/tariffshift rules -ecfr <xml> -as-of 2026-11-18 -retrieved 2026-11-22` rebuilds it, and a test re-parses the committed text so the data cannot drift from the parser. 360 rows; 332 of 515 "A change to ..." sentences are encoded. The other 183 describe goods in words ("printer units of ADP machines of subheading 8443.31 through 8443.32") and are never decided by code; the codes they name are kept so a sentence that cannot apply to a good is skipped.
- **How it decides:** a foreign material passes an alternative when its code is outside what the "from any other chapter/heading/subheading" clause excludes (with "outside that group", "including another ... within that group" and "within that group" read as written; a range rule that says neither is read as "outside that group", marked `group_implied`), or in a listed source, and not in an exception. Conditions the code cannot see (simple assembly, GRI 2(a), bending to shape, goods descriptions, a code too short) make the material "needs a fact", never a pass or a fail. All foreign materials pass: origin is the country of production, 102.11(a)(3). All materials domestic: 102.11(a)(2). Some fail: 102.13 de minimis when value shares are given (7%; not for the chapters in 102.13(b)); then 102.11(b) through 102.18(b)(1): when exactly one material is classified where the rule allows no change it is the essential-character material (102.18(b)(1)(iii)); when several are, and all come from one country, that country; otherwise undetermined. A value that is not given leaves the answer undetermined, with the answer that holds if de minimis does not apply. The paragraph (p) note barring changes to 8708 from four subheadings is an exception on every 8708 rule; the chapter 94 bedding note marks its rule so 102.11(b) is not applied. Each answer cites the rule row and paragraphs and lists what it assumes (102.17 operations; not a set).
- **What it is not:** not USMCA qualification (General Note 11), not textile (102.21), not a classifier: it needs the HTS codes it is given. It does not read 102.11(c) and (d).

## D361 — Pre-registration: the rule check on the committed Part 102 rulings (22 Nov)
Committed before any extraction call and before any rule result was seen. Nothing in the rules, the parser or the evaluator is changed because of a held-out result.
- **Cases:** the Part 102 splits as frozen: development `splits_usmca.json` `dev` (72), held-out `final` (32) and `splits_usmca_final2.json` `final2` (13). CBP's answer is `primary_origin` in `eval/data/labels_usmca{,_final2}.jsonl`; the model's answer is `pred` in the usmca-v1 rows the docs already quote (`eval/results/usmca-{dev,final}-gemini-4-argon-prec-usmca-v1-litellm.jsonl`, `usmca-final2-gemini-4-argon-prec-usmca-v1-litellm-router.jsonl`).
- **Why a model reads the inputs:** a regex finds any HTS code in 25 of the 72 development facts and two or more codes in 7; it cannot pair a code with a material and an origin. So Gemini 3.8 Flash, through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini, temperature 0 and the fixed seed, reads each text once into: the good's HTS code, the country of production, and the materials with HTS code and origin, each code with a verbatim quote. Code checks every quote: a quote not found in the text, or a code not in its quote, is dropped (the field becomes empty). Conditions (simple assembly, GRI 2(a), bending) and value shares are not extracted; they stay unknown.
- **Two inputs, reported apart:** (A) **facts**: the facts section the engine reads (`backend/data/facts`, `eval/data/facts_final2`), cut before CBP's analysis. This is what the product would have. (B) **full ruling text**: the whole CROSS text, which includes CBP's own classifications and its analysis. B checks the rule encoding given CBP's codes, not the product; it is labelled that way everywhere. The extractions are committed (`eval/tariffshift/inputs-*.jsonl`) with the quotes; the raw texts are not.
- **Development and freeze:** the extraction prompt (`tariffshift-extract-v1`), parser and evaluator may change on development cases only. Before the held-out extraction the code is committed and that commit is named in RESULTS; the held-out cases are then extracted and scored once.
- **Reported, per split and input:** coverage (decided / n), agreement with CBP among decided (exact Wilson interval), disagreement with the model among decided, the undetermined reasons, out-of-scope count, and a second line "decided if de minimis does not apply". Every run is reported whatever it shows, and every gateway call's cost is logged in RUNLOG.
- **Expectation stated now:** coverage on facts will be low, because most facts name no codes for the materials.
- **Cost cap:** $3 for all extraction (estimate: 117 rulings × 2 inputs, about 4k tokens in and 0.5k out each, well under $1).

## D362 — The rule check on the committed Part 102 rulings: results (22 Nov)
Run as pre-registered in D361. Development runs on 22 Nov; code frozen at commit 89552154 before the held-out extraction (kept as branch `improve/tariff-shift-prereg`, with the D361 commit 65b1cb2f); each held-out file extracted and scored once. Rows: `eval/tariffshift/`; recompute with `cd backend && go run ./cmd/tariffshift eval -check` (in `make reproduce`).

| Split | Input | n | Out of scope | Decided | Agrees with CBP | Differs from the model |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| dev (development) | facts, prompt v1 | 72 | 5 | 0 | – | – |
| dev (development) | full ruling text | 72 | 39 | 2 | 2 of 2 | 0 |
| final (held out) | facts | 32 | 6 | 0 | – | – |
| final (held out) | full ruling text | 32 | 17 | 2 | 2 of 2 | 0 |
| final2 (held out) | facts | 13 | 3 | 0 | – | – |

- **Coverage is very low, as expected.** On the facts the engine reads, the rule check decides 0 of 117 rulings: most facts give no HTS code for the good (68 of 117), and no in-scope facts give a code for every foreign material. On the full ruling text, which carries CBP's own classifications, it decides 4 of 104, all four as CBP held and as the model answered. The interval on 2 of 2 is 34.2–100%; nothing about accuracy can be read from it.
- **Where the full text still does not decide:** the good is outside the seven chapters (56 of 104); a foreign material has no code in the text (23); a material turns on a fact the rule names (simple assembly, GRI 2(a), a goods description) or only "outside heading X" is known (13); the text gives no code for the good (3); the extraction failed (4); one development case turns on a de minimis value the text does not give, and its answer if de minimis does not apply (China) is CBP's.
- **Changes made on development cases only:** prompt v2 asks for country names, not adjectives, and adds `not_in` for "classified outside heading X", which CBP writes instead of a code; the evaluator accepts that and treats a material of unknown origin as foreign. The output-token limit went from 4,096 to 8,192 after nine v2 rows ran out of tokens; those nine were re-run once (dev only). The v1 rows are kept in `eval/tariffshift/dev-v1/`. The development facts cell is the v1 run (not re-run, to stay within the $3 cap); the held-out facts used v2.
- **Not run:** the full text of the 13 final2 rulings. Their raw CROSS texts are not in the local corpus, and fetching them would have gone past this change's fetch cap.
- **Cost:** $2.58 gateway-reported for all seven extraction runs (RUNLOG `tariffshift-extract-*`). Scoring makes no model calls.
- **What this means for the product:** the rule check can only cross-check cases where the user gives an HTS code for the good and for each material. Real requests rarely do, and the committed rulings mostly do not. It is shown beside the model as a check, never in place of it (D363).

## D363 — The rule check is shown beside the model, display only (22 Nov)
- **What:** a case whose product gives an HTS code for the good and at least one part carries `rule_check` in the API (`GET /v1/cases/{id}`, predict), in the MCP `predict_origin` and `get_case` summaries, and as a "Rule check (19 CFR 102.20)" panel under the answer. A part's code is a new optional `hts` on each component (API, MCP and the parts table); it is not part of the facts the engine reads.
- **Never changes an outcome:** the check is computed when the case is read, from the stored product, and is not stored or fed to the engine, the triage, auto-accept, clarify or the planner. A case without codes reads byte for byte as before. `RULE_CHECK=off` removes it; the default is on because it is display only.
- **Where it applies:** `applies` is `part102` when the router sent the case to the Part 102 rules, and `informational` otherwise: Part 102 is the marking rule for goods processed in Canada or Mexico, so on a substantial-transformation case it is a comparison, labelled that way. The country of production is the final-assembly plant, else the plant of the last operation, else the only plant.
- **What the screen shows** (coordinator review of #544): the full panel only when the check decided, agreeing or not. When it did not decide, one calm line: "add HTS codes for N parts to run it" with a link to the parts table, or the open fact or value in a few words. On a Part 102 case whose parts carry no codes, the same "add codes" line. On a substantial-transformation case whose parts carry no codes, nothing (the demo fixtures give only the good's code). The API and MCP output are unchanged by this.
- **Disagreement:** when the check decides and names a different country from the model, `review` is true and the panel says so in the amber "worth a broker's look" style. It does not move the case to review; that is a person's call.
- **Why not more:** the offline evaluation (D362) decides almost nothing from real facts, because they rarely give codes. Letting it change answers would need a measured benefit it does not have.

## D370 — A revocation counts from the day after the revoking ruling's date (22 Nov, #527, judge panel round 7, item 1)
- **Problem:** D320 hid a precedent CBP revoked or modified "on or before" the case date. The edge date is the revoking ruling's own issue date, so when the case being backtested is the revocation letter itself, its own change hid the ruling it revokes. The label's document decided what evidence the model saw. A ruling dated on the case date is never usable as its precedent (`!r.Date.Before(caseDate)`), and the verifier's `inBound` is strict, but the supersession check was not.
- **Decision:** `domain.SupersededAsOf(id, at)` counts an edge only when its date is strictly before `at` (`d.Before(at)`), the same rule as ruling dates. Everything that reads it follows: `UsableAsPrecedentFor` (so `retrieval.PrecedentFilter`, the engine, the eval harness and the adjudicator), the verifier's `superseded`, its search marks and `read_ruling` note, the cached-verdict and cached-prediction checks, and the ruling API. The frontend's `supersededAsOf` marks "not counted as support for this case" from the day after the change. A zero date still means "today": any known change counts.
- **Evidence:** N331974 is usable for case H325607 dated 2025-08-29 and not for a case dated 2025-08-30; every earliest edge in the map is usable on its own date and hidden the next day; flip pair MS-H305371, bounded by 2024-07-12, still sees N303338 (`TestACaseThatIsTheRevocationStillSeesTheRulingItRevokes`, `TestPrecedentFilterSkipsRulingsRevokedBeforeTheCaseDate`, `TestEligibilityOnTheRevocationDate`, `TestCachePeekMissesRevokedPrecedents`, `scripts/supersession.test.mjs`).
- **Offline effect** (`eval/supersession/impact.py`, same `<` rule, no model calls): of 13,166 committed rows with rulings in 148 files, 23 showed and 8 cited a ruling superseded before their case date (it was 43 and 10 under the first rule; the published 135 files and 12,998 rows were also stale, #512 and #526 had regenerated the JSON). All 20 H325607 development rows drop out. The one supported verifier verdict on a superseded ruling (N322364, heat-gun bound) stays. EVALUATION 2.5, NUMBERS, DATASHEET, /method and /accuracy now give these counts.
- **Not done:** nothing was re-run. No pinned demo cites a ruling revoked on its case date, so no stored answer changes.

## D371 — Compare ranks countries by their best plan's tier, then net (22 Nov, #532, judge panel round 7, item 2)
- **Problem:** D341 ranks plans inside a target by tier, then net, but `rankTargets` ordered countries by status and net alone. A country whose best plan was at the bar outranked one with a clear-margin plan, and the tier rule inside a target could flip the comparison toward the borderline country.
- **Decision:** among countries with a shown plan, `rankTargets` puts a clear-margin best plan before an at-the-bar one, then keeps the old order (priced first in operations mode, then net). Below-bar, no-plan and no-plant countries are unchanged. The compare table shows the same tier chip as the plan step under each shown plan's check, and the caption, the "How the ranking works" note, the README, DETAILS and the OpenAPI description say so.
- **Evidence:** `TestRankTargetsByBestPlanTierThenNet` (the issue's example: Thailand's 0.96 plan at $100k ranks above Vietnam's 0.92 plan at $150k). The frontend legacy golden was re-captured once for the compare screen: only the caption, the note and the chip changed.
- **Not done:** stored comparisons keep their stored order until compared again.

## D372 — The former group name comes from the stored plan's template version (22 Nov, #533, judge panel round 7, item 3)
- **Problem:** D342 gave "Switch, cord and minor parts" to any product that filed parts under the switch group and nothing under `cord_knobs_covers`. A motorised product with no cord at all, a cordless tool or the stove heater, met that test and read "cord".
- **Decision:** the motorised-tools template is now version 2 (the split), and its `former` carries `since: "2"`. `planner.GroupNameAt(product, template, id, madeWith)` gives the former name only when `madeWith`, the template version the stored plan was made with (`template_versions`), is older than `since`, and the product still files nothing under the split-out group. A plan made now (`GroupNameFor`, `Describe`) always reads the new name. `Redescribe`, the draft letter and the compare rows pass the stored version. In the frontend, `CaseShell` provides the case's plan version (`planTemplateVersion`) and `groupName(id, family, product, madeWith)` uses the same rule.
- **Evidence:** the pinned heat gun's plan (version 1, cord still in the group) reads "Switch, cord and minor parts"; the same parts at version 2, the stove heater, N350310, the waffle purifier and a cordless tool read "Switch, wiring and minor parts" (`TestGroupKeepsItsFormerNameForAPlanMadeBeforeTheSplit`, `TestProductsWithoutACordReadTheNewGroupName`, `TestRedescribeUsesTheStoredTemplateVersion`, `scripts/families.test.mjs`). The legacy plan goldens were regenerated; the only change is `template_versions` "1" to "2".
- **Limit:** plans made between D330 and this change carry version 1 although they were made after the split. A cordless product planned in that window still reads the former name until it is planned again.

## D373 — The 15 post-cutoff pairs say "run 1 of 3", not "scored once" (22 Nov, #543, judge panel round 7, item 3)
- **Problem:** after D354, the 15 D170 pairs' rows on /accuracy (their source text in `summary.json`), EVALUATION §7, NUMBERS and the model card still said "scored once", next to a "same in 3 runs" chip.
- **Change:** `cmd/eval` writes "run 1 of 3, runs 2 and 3 gave the same figures, D354" as the source of the three `planner_post_known-a_*` rows; `summarize.py`'s description, the EVALUATION §7 heading, the two NUMBERS rows, the model card and the RESULTS heading say the same. The not-given row gives runs 2 and 3 (shown 5 and 4, right 4 each time), recomputed from the committed rows. `TestPostCutoffPairSourcesSayThreeRuns` fails if a `planner_post_` source says "scored once".


## D380 — HTS codes suggested for each part, so the 102.20 rule check can run; the route is on by default (22 Nov)
- **Problem:** the 19 CFR 102.20 rule check (D360, D363) needs an HTS code for the good and for every foreign part. Users rarely have the parts' codes, so on a real case it almost never decides; the screen says "add HTS codes for N parts to run it".
- **Part index:** `go run ./cmd/corpus part-hts-index` reads every raw CROSS ruling of the corpus and keeps each sentence that classifies a named thing ("The aluminum pegs will be classified in subheading 8302.50, HTSUS."): the thing, the code at the level stated (4, 6 or 8 digits), the ruling and its date (`hts.PartStatements`; chapters 98 and 99, generic names and legal text such as "the country of origin of a good classified in ..." are skipped). It writes `backend/data/part_hts_index.json`, which ships in the image with the other data files.
- **Route:** `POST /v1/products/parts-hts-suggest {product}`. For each named part (1 to 20; more is a 400), BM25 over the part index gives up to 4 codes CBP stated for similar parts, with their sentences. One router-model call for the whole product (`parts-hts-suggest-v1`) returns up to 3 codes per part, best first, each with one sentence of reason. A code equal to one of the part's ruling codes is labelled `rulings` and keeps only citations of those rulings. Any other code is labelled `model`: the model's own reading of the HTSUS, with no supporting ruling; at 8 digits it is kept only when the USITC HTS lists it today (the D92 check). When the product has no `hts`, the good's code is asked in the same call, from the same candidate search as `/v1/products/hts-suggest` (CROSS plus the local index). Answers per part are cached for a day in memory (at most 2,000). Until D410 the key was the part's and the product's name only, shared by every caller, so one caller could get reasons the model wrote from another caller's description (#577); D410 scopes the cache to the caller and keys it on the description too. If the model call fails, the ruling codes come back without reasons (`reasons_unavailable`). Every response says "unverified suggestions — check every code with your customs broker before you rely on it".
- **Nothing is filled in by itself.** The parts table offers "Suggest codes for all parts"; each part shows its codes with the source and reason, and the user accepts one per part or all top suggestions at once. Accepted codes are ordinary part codes: the rule check reads them when the case is read (D363), and it still never changes the answer.
- **MCP:** `suggest_part_hts` calls the same route in-process (same limits, spend cap and audit).
- **Local runs:** with `LLM_PROVIDER=fake`, the fake model answers each part with the first code its nearest ruling lists, and the reason says it was not judged (`hts.SimulatedPartsAnswer`), so the suggest, accept and rule-check loop can be tried without a model (DEMO.md §8).
- **On by default (`PARTS_HTS_SUGGEST`, default on; `PART_HTS_INDEX`, default `data/part_hts_index.json`).** This turns on a model-calling route in production with no deploy change. It is safe for the same reasons as the other model routes: it goes through `limit(light, ...)` (`TestEveryV1RouteHasALimitClass` lists it among the model routes), so the per-client light rate, the in-flight cap, the daily spend cap and the client's share of it apply, and its model call is metered at the raw client like every other. One call per request, at most 20 parts, a cache, and no CBP search per part. Unset, a missing index turns the route off with a warning instead of stopping the server; `PARTS_HTS_SUGGEST=off` removes it (404). The product-level route is unchanged. Note: `HTS_SUGGEST` is already on in production (D91, deploy workflow), so `/v1/products/hts-suggest` answers there today.

## D381 — Pre-registration: per-part HTS suggestions on the Part 102 rulings and the family fixtures (22 Nov)
Committed before any suggestion run and before any gold code was read. Nothing in the gold rule or the scoring is changed because of a result.
- **Products.** Development: the Part 102 `dev` split (`splits_usmca.json`) rulings that have a full-text extraction with parts (`eval/tariffshift/inputs-dev-full_text.jsonl`, D361), and the 23 per-family plan fixtures (`backend/testdata/planner_families`, all dev or train, D171). Held out: the Part 102 `final` split, the same way. final2 is not scored: its raw texts are not in the local corpus, so it has no gold.
- **What the suggester sees.** Part 102: name from the ruling's subject (the words after its last "origin"/"classification"/"marking" phrase), description = the facts the engine reads with every HTS number replaced by "[code removed]", parts = the names and origins of the full-text extraction (made by Gemini 3.8 Flash in D361; names only). Families: the fixture's name, description (numbers removed) and parts. No product carries a code. Only rulings dated before the scored ruling are searched, in both the part index and the product index, and never the ruling itself. No CROSS search and no USITC check run: the evaluation makes no web fetches. Production adds both for the good and for 8-digit model codes; that difference is not measured.
- **Gold.** `hts.PartStatements` on the scored ruling's full CROSS text (offline, no model). A part's gold is the code of the sentences whose thing matches the part's name: the content words of one (3+ letters, stop words out, a plural "s" dropped) are a subset of the other's. When several sentences match, their codes must agree (each a prefix of the longest) and the longest is gold; when they disagree the part has no gold and is counted as ambiguous. A part with no matching sentence has no gold. The good's gold is CBP's code from the full-text extraction (D361, quote-checked).
- **Scoring.** A suggestion matches when it equals the gold at the gold's level, up to 8 digits; a shorter suggestion never matches. Reported per split and set: products, parts, coverage (parts with at least one suggestion), gold parts, top-1 and top-3 with exact 95% Wilson intervals, top-1 at the same heading, top-1 split by source (rulings, model) with its accuracy, the good's top-1 and top-3, cost per product (gateway-reported), tokens. The rule check (Part 102 only), three inputs: (a) D362's full-text input as committed (reference); (b) CBP's code for the good with the top suggestion for every part; (c) the top suggestion for the good and every part. Each: out of scope, decided, agrees with CBP's held origin, undetermined reasons.
- **Model and runs.** Gemini 3.8 Flash (the server's router model) through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini, no response cache. Every run is a file `eval/tariffshift/parts/suggest-<split>[-<tag>].jsonl` with a RUNLOG line; `go run ./cmd/tariffshift parts-eval -check` recomputes `parts/summary.json` from the committed rows (in `make reproduce`).
- **Development and freeze.** The prompt, retrieval and part index may change on development products only; every development run is kept and reported. Before the held-out run the code is committed and that commit is named in RESULTS; then the held-out gold is read once and the held-out products are suggested and scored once.
- **Expectation stated now:** modest. Many parts are generic ("fasteners", "small hardware") and many rulings state no part codes, so gold parts will be few and intervals wide. The rule check cannot decide a good outside the seven encoded chapters whatever the codes (56 of 104 in D362), so most rulings stay undetermined even with every part coded.
- **Cost cap:** $8 for all runs (estimate: about 100 products a run at about $0.02 each).

## D382 — Per-part HTS suggestions: results (22 Nov)
Run as pre-registered in D381. Development runs on 22 Nov; code frozen at commit dde4fec9 (kept, with the D381 commit 65415b9a, as branch `improve/hts-suggest-parts-freeze`; the merged history has new hashes) before the held-out gold was read; the held-out products were suggested and scored once. Rows: `eval/tariffshift/parts/`; recompute with `cd backend && go run ./cmd/tariffshift parts-eval -check` (in `make reproduce`).

| Run | Products | Parts with a suggestion | Gold parts | Top-1 | Top-3 | Good: top-1 / top-3 | Cost per product |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| dev, Part 102 rulings | 69 | 389 of 393 | 27 | 20 (55.3–86.8%) | 22 (63.3–91.8%) | 36 / 42 of 68 | $0.0103 |
| dev, family fixtures | 23 | 200 of 200 | 2 | 2 | 2 | – | $0.0139 |
| final, Part 102 (held out) | 29 | 225 of 245 | 3 | 2 (20.8–93.9%) | 2 | 14 / 15 of 27 | $0.0123 |

Rule check on the Part 102 rulings, the same products (69 of the 72 dev and 29 of the 32 held-out rulings have a full-text extraction with parts; the rest are left out):

| Run | Input | n | Out of scope | Decided | Agrees with CBP |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| dev | CBP's codes as stated in the full text (D362, reference) | 69 | 38 | 2 | 2 |
| dev | CBP's code for the good, top suggestion for every part | 69 | 38 | 9 | 9 |
| dev | top suggestion for the good and every part | 69 | 38 | 10 | 9 |
| final (held out) | CBP's codes as stated (reference) | 29 | 16 | 2 | 2 |
| final (held out) | CBP's code for the good, top suggestion for every part | 29 | 16 | 7 | 6 |
| final (held out) | top suggestion for the good and every part | 29 | 15 | 8 | 7 |

- **Gold parts are few.** Only 27 dev parts and 3 held-out parts have a code CBP states in a sentence the regex reads. The held-out accuracy (2 of 3) says nothing on its own; the dev interval is wide too.
- **Gold quality (post hoc, by reading the 29 dev sentences):** 4 dev gold codes look wrong. "vinyl skin" took the kit's code; "adhesive" took the paper's; "brake caliper core" took the finished caliper's; and "pressure switch" took the receiver drier's code from a "respectively" sentence. The model answered 8536.50 there, which matches the sentence. Without those four, top-1 is 19 of 23 and top-3 21 of 23. This line is post hoc. The pre-registered figures above stand.
- **Where the top suggestions come from:** on the dev Part 102 gold parts, 9 top suggestions came from earlier rulings (7 right) and 18 from the model's own reading (13 right). Most parts have no similar part in the 1,159 classifying sentences of the corpus, so most codes are the model's. The screen labels each one.
- **The rule check decides more, not most.** With CBP's code for the good and the suggested part codes, it decides 9 of 69 dev and 7 of 29 held-out rulings, against 2 and 2 with only the codes CBP states. 15 of the 16 agree with CBP. The exception is held-out N358639 (a pipe assembly): the check decided Mexico, CBP held South Korea; the same with the suggested good code. Dev N340017 disagrees only when the good's code is suggested too. One held-out ruling the reference decided (N362506) is not decided with suggestions. More than half of the rulings stay out of scope: the good is outside the seven encoded chapters, and no part code changes that.
- **Failures:** 1 held-out product (N359001) and the first smoke product returned truncated JSON; they count as products with no suggestion. The smoke run (3 products, output cap 1,024 + 400 per item) led to the 8,192-token output cap; it is kept as `suggest-dev-smoke.jsonl`. No other development change was made: v1 is the frozen code.
- **Not measured:** the production path adds a live CROSS search for the good and drops model codes that are not in today's HTS. The evaluation ran neither, to make no web fetches. Origins came from the full-text extraction, as a user would type them.
- **After the freeze** only the local fake answer, the eval's per-ruling disagreement list and docs changed; the suggestion code the held-out run used is unchanged.
- **Cost:** $1.42 gateway-reported for all three runs (RUNLOG `parts-hts-*`), about one cent per product.

## D390 — No tool input schema has anyOf, oneOf, allOf or not at its root (22 Nov, judge panel round 7)
- **Problem:** #514 (D323) found that an ADK agent offered all 13 MCP tools through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini got a 400 for the whole request, so it ran with two tools only. The cause was not known.
- **Diagnosis:** each tool sent alone through the gateway was accepted except `predict_origin`. Removing any one of its properties still failed; removing its root `anyOf: [{required: [facts]}, {required: [product]}]` made it pass, and then every listed tool passed together, on a local server and on the live server's tool list. The gateway's message was only "The request was invalid". `extract_product_from_media`'s `oneOf` inside `files.items` was accepted. Gateway cost of the probes: about $0.01.
- **Change:** `predict_origin` no longer declares the root `anyOf`; its handler refuses a call with neither `facts` nor `product` before any API call, with an `invalid_input` tool error. `TestInputSchemasHaveNoRootComposition` fails if any listed tool's input schema has `anyOf`, `oneOf`, `allOf` or `not` at its root; `TestPredictNeedsFactsOrProductBeforeAnyAPICall` covers the handler check.
- **Limit:** found on one gateway path to Gemini 3.8 Flash. We did not test Gemini's own API with a Google key, nor other clients. Until this is deployed, the live server still has the `anyOf`; `examples/adk-agent` strips root composition keywords on the client (`drop_root_composition`), which does nothing once the server has the fix.

## D391 — A Google ADK agent runs a whole case over MCP (22 Nov, judge panel round 7)
- **Change:** `examples/adk-agent` is an ADK `LlmAgent` with an `McpToolset` filtered to `extract_product`, `predict_origin`, `suggest_part_hts`, `plan_production_moves`, `export_ruling_request_draft` and `get_ruling`, an instruction for a trade-compliance assistant that ends every answer with "Not a ruling — confirm with a licensed customs broker", and `run_case.py`, which prints a redacted transcript. The model is Gemini by name with a Google API key or Vertex AI, or ADK's `LiteLlm` wrapper when a gateway is set. Versions are pinned in `requirements.txt`. The MCP read timeout is 900 s.
- **Run:** once, 22 Nov 2026, against `https://undelabs.dev/mcp`, anonymous, Gemini 3.8 Flash through the gateway: extract, predict (China, calibrated 90%, citing N357771 and N358092), part HTS suggestions, plan to Thailand (1 supported plan, moving the motor-fan, PCBA and heating element), ruling request draft. One case created. Gateway-reported model cost $0.0465 over 7 model calls ([transcript](mcp-transcripts/adk-full-flow.md)).
- **Limit:** the first plan call passed ADK's default 300 s read timeout and the agent called it again (the second answered in 103 s), so the server may have run the plan search twice. The 900 s timeout was set after the run and not re-run. The Google API key and Vertex AI paths were not run. One product, one run: this shows the flow works, not how well.

## D392 — /developers, README and MCP.md point to the ADK agent (22 Nov, judge panel round 7)
- **Change:** /developers' MCP section has an "Agents (Google ADK)" part with the agent's toolset snippet, what the run did and links to `examples/adk-agent` and the transcript. README, docs/MCP.md and the roadmap say the same.

## D400 — Revocations from the Customs Bulletin; pre-registration of the parse (22 Nov, judge panel round 7, larger build)
- **Problem:** the supersession map (D320, D370) only knows revocations whose letter is in our corpus (35 edges). CBP publishes proposed and final revocations and modifications of ruling letters (19 U.S.C. § 1625(c)) in the weekly Customs Bulletin and Decisions, and most of those letters are not in the corpus.
- **Source, as fetched on 22 Nov:** 28 bulletins from vol. 57 no. 32 (6 Sep 2023) to vol. 60 no. 30 (30 Sep 2026), listed with URL and SHA-256 in `eval/data/bulletins/index.jsonl`. They were found by web search for bulletins whose first notice is a ruling-letter notice, within a cap of 40 web requests. The bulletin is weekly, so this is a sample chosen for having such notices, not a census. cbp.gov answered direct downloads from our network with HTTP 403, so the PDFs were retrieved with a web-reading tool; `go run ./cmd/corpus bulletins fetch` is the repeatable fetcher (one request a second) and checks the hashes.
- **Extraction (built, `internal/bulletin`):** `eval/bulletins/pdftext.py` (pypdf) turns each PDF into text. `corpus bulletins extract` cuts it into notices at each ACTION line whose text names a ruling letter, and commits a short excerpt per notice (`eval/data/bulletins/notices.jsonl`, public domain): the header to the effective date or comment deadline, and per attached ruling its number, subject line and the sentences that revoke or modify. 81 notices.
- **Parse rule (frozen with this entry):** a ruling is changed when an attached letter says it "is/are hereby revoked/modified", "we hereby revoke/modify" it, or lists it under EFFECT ON OTHER RULINGS as revoked or modified; numbers count only after "NY", "HQ" or "PC" or in a list that starts with one. The new ruling is the attached letter. A letter that another attachment revokes is an attached old letter and its own sentences are ignored. A notice is final when it states an EFFECTIVE DATE, withdrawn when its ACTION is a withdrawal, otherwise proposed. A parse is flagged when a final notice has no effective date, no ruling is found, a ruling the changing letter's subject line names is not changed in its text, or the count differs from the ACTION line's count (a floor when the subject says some rulings change "by operation of law"). At this freeze 3 of 81 notices are flagged (vol. 59 no. 8 p. 1, vol. 59 no. 35 p. 7, vol. 60 no. 16 p. 58).
- **Model fallback (pre-registered, not yet run):** only flagged notices go to `gemini-3.5-flash` through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM gateway to Gemini, temperature 0 with the fixed seed, one call per notice, JSON answer `{rulings: [{ruling, kind}]}` from the excerpt alone. For a flagged notice an edge is kept when the regex and the model both name it; a ruling only the model names is added only if its number appears verbatim in the excerpt. A failed or unparseable reply drops the notice's edges, counted as a parse failure. Replies are committed (`eval/data/bulletins/model_reads.jsonl`) so `make reproduce` makes no model call. Cap $6 (expected under $0.05). No prompt or rule changes after the run; any change is a new decision with both results.
- **Merge rule:** final changes become supersession edges with source `customs_bulletin`, dated by the effective date (D370: they count for cases dated strictly after it), and cite the bulletin's volume, number, printed page and URL. When the corpus already has the same edge (same ruling and revoking letter) the corpus edge and its letter date stay, and the bulletin citation is added. Proposed changes are listed separately as proposed: shown, never used to bar a precedent. A proposal is dropped when a fetched final notice changes the same ruling, or a fetched withdrawal names it.
- **Not a model evaluation:** nothing is scored and no held-out split is read. The effect is reported as the offline recount of `eval/supersession/impact.py`.

## D401 — Customs Bulletin revocations: results (22 Nov)
Run as pre-registered in D400. The rules were frozen at commit 0ec6b447 and the model runner at 31bbeb16, before the run; both are kept as branch `improve/customs-bulletin-freeze` (the merged history has new hashes).
- **Notices.** 28 bulletins, 81 ruling-letter notices: 38 final, 42 proposed, 1 withdrawal. 77 of the 80 non-withdrawal notices passed every parse check.
- **Model fallback.** The 3 flagged notices were each read once by `gemini-3.5-flash` ($0.0403 gateway-reported, `eval/data/bulletins/model_reads.jsonl`). Every reply parsed, and the model named every ruling the regex found, so none was dropped. It added 2 numbers that are in the excerpts: N159975 (vol. 59 no. 35 p. 7, a proposal; the letter's text misprints it as N15997, which stays because both readers name it) and 080651 (vol. 60 no. 16 p. 58, revoked by operation of law and named only in the subject line; a proposal). In vol. 59 no. 8 the EFFECT ON OTHER RULINGS section names H303733 where the subject line says H303773; both readers name both, so both are edges, as they already were from the corpus copy of the letter.
- **Merge.** 117 final changes (ruling and changing letter): 109 new edges, dated by the notice's effective date, and 8 already in the corpus, which keep their letter date and now cite the bulletin. The map (`supersession-v2`) has 144 edges on 143 rulings (70 revoked, 74 modified), 14 of them in a precedent pool (4 general, 10 Part 102; the new one is N326416, general pool, modified by H342508 effective 18 Oct 2026). 78 proposals are pending, none in a pool. The withdrawal names no fetched proposal.
- **Offline recount** (`eval/supersession/impact.py`, no model calls): unchanged at 23 rows showing and 8 citing a superseded precedent of 13,166 rows in 148 files. Only N326416 of the bulletin-only rulings appears in a committed row: 6 textile development rows for a case dated 11 Dec 2025, before the change took effect, so none counts.
- **What changes in the product.** The precedent filter, the verifier, the case agent's search, the cached-prediction check and the ruling API read the larger map, so a ruling a bulletin revoked is not cited for a case dated after its effective date, and its link says "superseded by". A proposal is shown beside the ruling ("CBP proposed to revoke it in H…") and in the API's `proposed` field; it never bars the ruling.
- **Limits.** The 28 bulletins are a sample of a weekly publication, chosen by web search for issues with ruling-letter notices, within a 40-request cap; changes in other issues are missed, and a proposal made final in an unfetched issue is still shown as proposed. The PDF text comes from pypdf and the parse reads CBP's own misprints as written. Corpus letters are dated by issue date and bulletin-only changes by effective date, so the two sources are not dated the same way; D400 kept the corpus date where both exist. cbp.gov refused direct downloads from our network (HTTP 403), so the PDFs were retrieved with a web-reading tool; the hashes in the index pin what was read.

## D402 — A duplicate plan search joins the one running (22 Nov, #574)
- **Problem:** the ADK run (#574, D391) showed an MCP client timing out at 300 s on `plan_production_moves` and retrying. Each call ran its own plan search, the heaviest model work the API does, so a retry could double the cost of a search whose answer was seconds away.
- **Decision:** the JSON plan route keeps a table of searches in flight, keyed by the case and a hash of everything the answer depends on (the planner request as priced, the money basis, the case's input fingerprint and whether the result is stored). A request with the same key waits for the running search and gets its answer. The search runs on a context detached from the request that started it, and stops 2 minutes after its last waiting caller leaves unless someone rejoins. An optional `Idempotency-Key` header (MCP: `idempotency_key`) keys the search by case and key instead, replays a finished answer for 15 minutes (at most 256 per instance), and refuses the same key with other inputs (409 `conflict`). A failed search is not kept. The result is stored on the case once, by the search.
- **Alternatives:** a shared table in Firestore would join retries across instances, at a write per plan request and a lease to manage; not done. Joining streamed requests would need fan-out of the event stream; the website does not retry plan searches.
- **Evidence:** `TestPlanFlightsRunOncePerKey` (8 concurrent callers, 1 run), `TestPlanFlightsOutliveATimedOutCaller`, `TestPlanFlightsCancelWhenNobodyComesBack`, `TestPlanFlightsIdempotencyKey`, `TestPlanRouteJoinsADuplicateSearch` (3 concurrent requests, 1 planner call; replay; 409; bad key 400), `TestPlanSendsTheIdempotencyKey`; run with `-race`.
- **Limits:** in memory, per instance. A joining request still takes a heavy rate-limit token and an in-flight slot while it waits; the model cost is charged to the caller that started the search. Streamed requests are not joined. Correction (D411, #581): as first built, a search that kept running after every caller had left held no in-flight slot, so one client could run more searches at once than `RATE_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` by dropping requests. The starting caller's slot now stays with the search until it ends.

## D410 — The per-part HTS cache is per caller and keyed on the description (22 Nov, #577)
- **Problem:** D380 cached each per-part answer for a day under the part's and the product's name, in one map shared by every caller. The description goes into the prompt and the model writes its reasons from it, so a later caller with the same part and product name got the first caller's codes and reasons, written from the first caller's description, flagged `cached`. The route is classed as holding no case data, but the cache carried one caller's text to another.
- **Decision:** the cache key is a SHA-256 of the prompt version, the caller's scope, and everything about the part that goes into the prompt: the part's words, where it is made, and the product's name and description (lower case, punctuation dropped, the description cut as it is sent). The scope is the signed-in user's workspace (else the user), the API key, the visitor ID, else the client address bucket. A request with no scope (the evaluation, `NoCache`) caches nothing. Cached answers are never served across scopes, so no caller sees model text made from another caller's input.
- **Alternatives:** caching only the ruling candidates, which hold no caller input, was simpler but would drop the saving on a repeated request; the BM25 search is local and cheap, so there was nothing to share across callers. Not caching at all was the fallback.
- **Evidence:** `TestPartsCacheNeverCrossesCallersOrDescriptions` (hts: another scope, another description, or both, each asks the model again, and no reason carries the first description), `TestPartsHTSSuggestCacheIsPerCallerAndDescription` (route: two visitors and an anonymous caller), the key test in `TestPartsSuggestDeduplicatesAndCachesPerProduct`.
- **Limits:** members of one workspace share cached answers, as they share cases. A visitor who changes their visitor ID loses their cache. Still in memory, per instance, a day, at most 2,000 answers.

## D411 — A detached plan search keeps its caller's in-flight slot (22 Nov, #581)
- **Problem:** D402 runs a JSON plan search on a context detached from its request, and keeps it for 2 minutes after the last caller leaves. The in-flight slot (`RATE_MAX_IN_FLIGHT`) was given back when the handler returned, so a client that dropped its requests could have more searches running than the cap: the issue's offline repro showed 4 at once with a cap of 1.
- **Decision:** `limit` puts the request's slot in its context. When a request starts a new search, the search takes the slot over and gives it back when the search ends (finished, failed, or cancelled after the grace period), not when the handler returns. A joining or replayed request does not take it over: it holds its own slot only while it waits, as before. Each slot is given back exactly once.
- **Evidence:** `TestDetachedPlanSearchKeepsItsInFlightSlot` (cap 1: start a search, drop the request, then three plan requests with other targets get 429 `too_many_in_flight` and start no search; after the first search ends a new one runs), `TestJoiningPlanRequestHoldsASlotOnlyWhileWaiting`; both with `-race`. With the hand-off removed the first test fails with 4 searches running.
- **Limits:** per instance, like the cap itself. A client still gets the heavy rate limit on starts; a dropped search counts against its cap for up to the search time plus the 2-minute grace. The dropped request is still logged as an error.
